<purpose>
This document contains the source code for the RiskQuant 'MARKET_DATA' profile.
Treat this source code as the authoritative implementation.
</purpose>

<llm_guidance>
RiskQuant Design Principles:
- Data-Oriented Design (DOD)
- Structure-of-Arrays (SoA)
- Human-readable at the edge
- C-contiguous at the core
- Strings must die early
- Memory locality matters
- CPU efficiency matters
- Memory efficiency matters
- Kernels do not contain policy - they are purely computational
- Compilers are responsible for policy and orchestration; they must fail fast on invalid inputs
- Avoid unnecessary branching in hot paths
- Skip '__init__.py' files (they are always empty)

When proposing code:
- Preserve existing SoA layouts
- Avoid object-heavy designs in hot paths
- Avoid pandas in performance-critical code
- Minimize allocations
- Prefer vectorized NumPy operations
- Preserve existing architectural patterns
</llm_guidance>

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\market_data\calibrate\implied_vol\black76\demo_calibrate.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/market_data/calibrate/implied_vol/black76/demo_calibrate.py
"""
Demo: the end-to-end Black-76 implied-vol calibration edge.

Pipeline:
    raw market quotes (discounted prices, mixed calls/puts)
      -> validate_option_quotes        (dtype/shape/domain + no-arbitrage)
      -> compile_canonical_option_quotes(put-call parity + undiscounting)
      -> calibrate_black76_iv_...       (implied vol + vega quality weight)

A synthetic volatility smile is generated with the package's own model kernel,
quoted as out-of-the-money options (puts below the forward, calls above),
discounted to market prices, then run back through the edge to recover the
smile. Also shows the vega quality weight, the reliability gate, and the
no-arbitrage guard rejecting a bad quote.

Run:
    python -m scripts.demos.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.demo_calibrate
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.calibrate import (
    calibrate_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.kernels.black76_model import (
    _black76_call_price_undiscounted,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.compile import (
    compile_canonical_option_quotes,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.validate import (
    validate_option_quotes,
)


def _banner(title: str) -> None:
    print("\n" + title)
    print("-" * len(title))


def _synthetic_quotes():
    """A skewed smile quoted OTM (puts K<F, calls K>=F) at a single tenor."""
    fwd = 100.0
    tte = 0.50
    df = 0.985

    strikes = np.array([70.0, 80.0, 90.0, 100.0, 110.0, 120.0, 130.0])
    true_iv = np.array([0.34, 0.29, 0.245, 0.20, 0.215, 0.25, 0.30])  # skew

    # undiscounted call for every strike, from the known smile
    undisc_call = np.array(
        [
            _black76_call_price_undiscounted(fwd=fwd, strike=k, tte=tte, vol=v)
            for k, v in zip(strikes, true_iv)
        ]
    )

    # quote OTM: calls at/above the forward, puts below it (parity to put leg)
    flag = (strikes >= fwd).astype(np.int8)
    undisc_quote = np.where(flag == 1, undisc_call, undisc_call - (fwd - strikes))

    option_price = df * undisc_quote  # discounted market price

    return {
        "fwd_price": np.full_like(strikes, fwd),
        "strike": strikes,
        "tte": np.full_like(strikes, tte),
        "call_put_flag": flag,
        "discount_factor": np.full_like(strikes, df),
        "option_price": option_price,
        "_true_iv": true_iv,
    }


def main() -> None:
    q = _synthetic_quotes()
    true_iv = q.pop("_true_iv")

    validated = validate_option_quotes(
        fwd_price=q["fwd_price"],
        strike=q["strike"],
        tte=q["tte"],
        call_put_flag=q["call_put_flag"],
        discount_factor=q["discount_factor"],
        option_price=q["option_price"],
    )
    canonical = compile_canonical_option_quotes(validated)
    calibrated = calibrate_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices(canonical)

    _banner("Calibrated option quotes")
    print(calibrated.to_string())

    _banner("Smile recovery (calibrated iv vs true iv)")
    print(
        f"{'K':>8} | {'flag':>4} | {'true iv':>9} | {'calib iv':>9} | {'abs err':>10}"
    )
    for i in range(calibrated.n_quotes):
        flag = "C" if q["call_put_flag"][i] == 1 else "P"
        print(
            f"{q['strike'][i]:8.1f} | {flag:>4} | "
            f"{true_iv[i]:9.5f} | {calibrated.iv[i]:9.5f} | "
            f"{abs(calibrated.iv[i] - true_iv[i]):10.2e}"
        )
    max_err = float(np.max(np.abs(calibrated.iv - true_iv)))
    print(f"\nmax |calibrated - true| iv = {max_err:.2e}")

    _banner("Quality gate")
    reliable = calibrated.reliable_mask()
    print(f"solved   : {calibrated.n_solved} / {calibrated.n_quotes}")
    print(f"failed   : {calibrated.n_failed}")
    print(f"reliable : {int(reliable.sum())} (vega above the default floor)")

    _banner("No-arbitrage guard rejects a bad quote")
    try:
        validate_option_quotes(
            fwd_price=[100.0],
            strike=[100.0],
            tte=[0.5],
            call_put_flag=np.array([1], dtype=np.int8),
            discount_factor=[0.985],
            option_price=[99.0],  # exceeds df * F = 98.5
        )
    except ValueError as exc:
        print(f"rejected: {exc}")

    print("\nOK")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\market_data\calibrate\implied_vol\black76\demo_kernel_black76_model.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/market_data/calibrate/implied_vol/black76/demo_kernel_black76_model.py
"""
Demo: the Black-76 model kernels used by implied-vol calibration.

Exercises the undiscounted call-price kernel and its analytic vega, and shows
the properties the calibrator relies on:

    * call price is bounded by [intrinsic, forward] and decreasing in strike,
    * put-call parity holds at the undiscounted (forward) level,
    * vega peaks at-the-money and decays into the wings -- the basis for using
      it as the calibration quality weight,
    * analytic vega matches a central finite-difference of the price.

Run:
    python -m scripts.demos.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.demo_kernel_black76_model
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.kernels.black76_model import (
    _black76_call_price_undiscounted,
    _black76_undiscounted_vega,
    _numba_norm_cdf,
    _numba_norm_pdf,
)


def _banner(title: str) -> None:
    print("\n" + title)
    print("-" * len(title))


def main() -> None:
    fwd = 100.0
    tte = 0.50
    vol = 0.20
    strikes = np.array([60.0, 80.0, 90.0, 100.0, 110.0, 120.0, 140.0])

    _banner(f"Undiscounted Black-76 call (F={fwd:g}, T={tte:g}, vol={vol:.0%})")
    print(f"{'K':>8} | {'Call':>12} | {'Vega':>12} | {'ImpliedPut':>12}")
    prev_call = np.inf
    for k in strikes:
        call = _black76_call_price_undiscounted(fwd=fwd, strike=k, tte=tte, vol=vol)
        vega = _black76_undiscounted_vega(fwd=fwd, strike=k, tte=tte, vol=vol)
        put = call - (fwd - k)  # put-call parity, undiscounted (forward) measure
        print(f"{k:8.1f} | {call:12.6f} | {vega:12.6f} | {put:12.6f}")

        # no-arbitrage bounds the calibrator trusts
        assert call >= max(fwd - k, 0.0) - 1e-9, "call below intrinsic"
        assert call <= fwd + 1e-9, "call above forward"
        assert put >= max(k - fwd, 0.0) - 1e-9, "put below intrinsic"
        assert call <= prev_call + 1e-12, "call not monotone decreasing in strike"
        prev_call = call

    _banner("Vega is the quality weight: peaks ATM, decays in the wings")
    vegas = {
        k: _black76_undiscounted_vega(fwd=fwd, strike=k, tte=tte, vol=vol)
        for k in strikes
    }
    atm = max(vegas, key=vegas.get)
    print(
        f"max vega at K={atm:g} (vega={vegas[atm]:.4f}); "
        f"deep-ITM K=60 vega={vegas[60.0]:.4f}, "
        f"deep-OTM K=140 vega={vegas[140.0]:.4f}"
    )
    assert atm == 100.0, "vega should peak at-the-money for this grid"

    _banner("Analytic vega vs central finite-difference of price")
    h = 1e-6
    print(f"{'K':>8} | {'analytic':>14} | {'finite-diff':>14} | {'abs err':>10}")
    for k in (80.0, 100.0, 120.0):
        up = _black76_call_price_undiscounted(fwd=fwd, strike=k, tte=tte, vol=vol + h)
        dn = _black76_call_price_undiscounted(fwd=fwd, strike=k, tte=tte, vol=vol - h)
        fd = (up - dn) / (2.0 * h)
        an = _black76_undiscounted_vega(fwd=fwd, strike=k, tte=tte, vol=vol)
        print(f"{k:8.1f} | {an:14.8f} | {fd:14.8f} | {abs(an - fd):10.2e}")
        assert abs(an - fd) < 1e-4, "analytic vega disagrees with finite difference"

    _banner("Limit behaviour")
    near_zero = _black76_call_price_undiscounted(
        fwd=fwd, strike=90.0, tte=tte, vol=1e-9
    )
    huge_vol = _black76_call_price_undiscounted(fwd=fwd, strike=90.0, tte=tte, vol=8.0)
    print(f"vol->0  call(K=90) = {near_zero:.6f}  (intrinsic = {fwd - 90.0:.6f})")
    print(f"vol=800% call(K=90) = {huge_vol:.6f}  (approaches forward = {fwd:.6f})")
    assert abs(near_zero - max(fwd - 90.0, 0.0)) < 1e-6
    assert huge_vol < fwd

    _banner("Normal helpers")
    print(f"Phi(0) = {_numba_norm_cdf(0.0):.6f}  (expect 0.5)")
    print(f"phi(0) = {_numba_norm_pdf(0.0):.6f}  (expect 1/sqrt(2*pi) = 0.398942)")

    print("\nOK")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\market_data\curves\demo_interp_methods_3d.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/market_data/curves/demo_interp_methods.py

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

# Import the Enums and Dispatcher (adjust paths based on your project structure)
from riskquant.market_data.curves.extrap_methods import (
    EXTRAP_FLAT,
    EXTRAP_LINEAR,
)
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_dispatch import CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_methods import (
    INTERP_LINEAR,
    INTERP_LOCF,
    INTERP_LOG_LINEAR,
    INTERP_NOCB,
)


def run_full_visualization_demo():
    # 1. Define a highly erratic, "kinky" input curve
    # We've added more pillars to really show off the zigzag.
    raw_tenors = np.array([1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0, 7.0, 10.0, 15.0, 20.0, 30.0])

    # Notice how the discount factors go down, UP, down, UP.
    # A Df going up over time means negative forward rates!
    raw_dfs = np.array([0.96, 0.70, 0.90, 0.50, 0.75, 0.40, 0.60, 0.20, 0.35])

    # 2. Reshape to match Numba signatures: (C, M) and (C, M, S)
    tenors = raw_tenors.reshape(1, -1)
    values = raw_dfs.reshape(1, -1, 1)

    # Warning: If you use negative values in raw_dfs, np.log() will throw a NaN error.
    # Log-linear interpolators expect strictly positive discount factors.
    log_values = np.log(values)

    # 3. Create targets extending beyond boundaries
    targets = np.linspace(0.0, 35.0, 1000)

    # Helper function to invoke the dispatcher and handle allocation
    def get_curve(interp_id, extrap_id, use_log=False):
        # Fetch the correct Numba kernel from the registry
        kernel = CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY[(int(interp_id), int(extrap_id))]

        # Pre-allocate output array
        out = np.empty((tenors.shape[0], targets.shape[0], values.shape[2]))

        # Inject log_values for the Log-Linear methods, else raw values
        input_vals = log_values if use_log else values

        # Execute the kernel
        kernel(tenors, input_vals, targets, out)

        # Return the 1D array (Curve 0, Scenario 0) for plotting
        return out[0, :, 0]

    # 4. Generate all 8 combinations via the dispatcher
    lin_flat = get_curve(INTERP_LINEAR, EXTRAP_FLAT)
    lin_lin = get_curve(INTERP_LINEAR, EXTRAP_LINEAR)

    log_flat = get_curve(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR, EXTRAP_FLAT, use_log=True)
    log_lin = get_curve(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR, EXTRAP_LINEAR, use_log=True)

    step_l_flat = get_curve(INTERP_LOCF, EXTRAP_FLAT)
    step_l_lin = get_curve(INTERP_LOCF, EXTRAP_LINEAR)

    step_r_flat = get_curve(INTERP_NOCB, EXTRAP_FLAT)
    step_r_lin = get_curve(INTERP_NOCB, EXTRAP_LINEAR)

    # 5. Plotting in a 2x2 Grid
    fig, axs = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(14, 10))
    fig.suptitle(
        "RiskQuant Interpolation & Extrapolation Behaviors via Dispatcher", fontsize=16
    )

    # Top-Left: Linear
    axs[0, 0].plot(targets, lin_flat, label="Flat Extrap", linewidth=2)
    axs[0, 0].plot(targets, lin_lin, label="Linear Extrap", linestyle="--")
    axs[0, 0].set_title("Linear Interpolation")

    # Top-Right: Log-Linear
    axs[0, 1].plot(targets, log_flat, label="Flat Extrap", linewidth=2)
    axs[0, 1].plot(targets, log_lin, label="Linear Extrap", linestyle="--")
    axs[0, 1].set_title("Log-Linear Interpolation (Discount Factors)")

    # Bottom-Left: Step Left
    # (Note: Flat and Linear extrapolate identically on step functions per your code)
    axs[1, 0].plot(targets, step_l_flat, label="Flat Extrap", linewidth=4, alpha=0.5)
    axs[1, 0].plot(targets, step_l_lin, label="Linear Extrap", linestyle=":")
    axs[1, 0].set_title("Step Left (LOCF)")

    # Bottom-Right: Step Right
    axs[1, 1].plot(targets, step_r_flat, label="Flat Extrap", linewidth=4, alpha=0.5)
    axs[1, 1].plot(targets, step_r_lin, label="Linear Extrap", linestyle=":")
    axs[1, 1].set_title("Step Right (NOCB)")

    # Apply common styling to all subplots
    for ax in axs.flat:
        ax.scatter(
            raw_tenors, raw_dfs, color="red", zorder=5, label="Input Pillars", s=50
        )
        ax.axvline(x=1.0, color="gray", linestyle=":", alpha=0.5)
        ax.axvline(x=30.0, color="gray", linestyle=":", alpha=0.5)
        ax.set_xlabel("Tenor (Years)")
        ax.set_ylabel("Value")
        ax.legend()
        ax.grid(True, alpha=0.3)

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.subplots_adjust(top=0.92)  # Adjust for the suptitle
    plt.show()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_full_visualization_demo()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\market_data\curves\demo_interp_methods_3d_throughput.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/market_data/curves/demo_interp_methods_3d_throughput.py

from __future__ import annotations

import random
import statistics
import time

import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.curves.extrap_methods import (
    EXTRAP_FLAT,
    EXTRAP_LINEAR,
)
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_dispatch import CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_methods import (
    INTERP_LINEAR,
    INTERP_LOCF,
    INTERP_LOG_LINEAR,
    INTERP_NOCB,
)


def bytes_to_gib(num_bytes: int) -> float:
    return num_bytes / (1024**3)


def run_3d_throughput_benchmark() -> None:
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
    print("   RISKQUANT NUMBA 3D KERNEL THROUGHPUT BENCHMARK")
    print("=" * 60)
    print()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Configuration
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    C_CURVES = 500
    M_PILLARS = 30
    D_TARGETS = 5000
    S_SCENARIOS = 500

    total_interps = C_CURVES * D_TARGETS * S_SCENARIOS

    print("Workload Dimensions:")
    print(f"  Curves:    {C_CURVES:,}")
    print(f"  Pillars:   {M_PILLARS:,}")
    print(f"  Targets:   {D_TARGETS:,}")
    print(f"  Scenarios: {S_SCENARIOS:,}")
    print(f"  Total Interpolations: {total_interps:,}")
    print()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Data Generation
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    print("Generating synthetic market data...\n")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(42)

    tenors = np.sort(
        rng.uniform(0.0, 50.0, size=(C_CURVES, M_PILLARS)),
        axis=1,
    )

    values = rng.uniform(
        0.1,
        1.5,
        size=(C_CURVES, M_PILLARS, S_SCENARIOS),
    )

    log_values = np.log(values)

    targets = np.linspace(
        -5.0,
        55.0,
        D_TARGETS,
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Memory Report
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    output_bytes = C_CURVES * D_TARGETS * S_SCENARIOS * np.dtype(np.float64).itemsize

    print("Approximate Memory Footprint:")
    print(f"  Values:      {bytes_to_gib(values.nbytes):.2f} GiB")
    print(f"  Log Values:  {bytes_to_gib(log_values.nbytes):.2f} GiB")
    print(f"  Output:      {bytes_to_gib(output_bytes):.2f} GiB")
    print()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Kernel Names
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    method_names = {
        (int(INTERP_LINEAR), int(EXTRAP_FLAT)): "Linear + Flat Extrap",
        (int(INTERP_LINEAR), int(EXTRAP_LINEAR)): "Linear + Linear Extrap",
        (int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR), int(EXTRAP_FLAT)): "Log-Linear + Flat Extrap",
        (int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR), int(EXTRAP_LINEAR)): "Log-Linear + Linear Extrap",
        (int(INTERP_LOCF), int(EXTRAP_FLAT)): "LOCF (Step Left) + Flat Extrap",
        (int(INTERP_LOCF), int(EXTRAP_LINEAR)): "LOCF (Step Left) + Linear Extrap",
        (int(INTERP_NOCB), int(EXTRAP_FLAT)): "NOCB (Step Right) + Flat Extrap",
        (int(INTERP_NOCB), int(EXTRAP_LINEAR)): "NOCB (Step Right) + Linear Extrap",
    }

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # JIT Warmup
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    print("Triggering Numba JIT Compilation...\n")

    dummy_tenors = np.ascontiguousarray(tenors[:1, :2])

    dummy_targets = np.ascontiguousarray(targets[:1])

    dummy_out = np.empty((1, 1, 1))

    for key, kernel in CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY.items():
        interp_id = key[0]

        input_vals = log_values if interp_id == int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR) else values

        dummy_vals = np.ascontiguousarray(input_vals[:1, :2, :1])

        kernel(
            dummy_tenors,
            dummy_vals,
            dummy_targets,
            dummy_out,
        )

    print("Compilation complete.")
    print()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Benchmark Setup
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    iterations = 7

    kernel_items = list(CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY.items())

    results: dict[str, list[float]] = {method_names[key]: [] for key, _ in kernel_items}

    print(f"Running {iterations} randomized benchmark rounds...")
    print()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Benchmark Loop
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    for round_idx in range(iterations):
        random.shuffle(kernel_items)

        for key, kernel in kernel_items:
            interp_id = key[0]

            input_vals = log_values if interp_id == int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR) else values

            # Allocate fresh output every run.
            #
            # This removes a large amount of cache-state bias
            # between kernels.
            out = np.empty(
                (
                    C_CURVES,
                    D_TARGETS,
                    S_SCENARIOS,
                ),
                dtype=np.float64,
            )

            t0 = time.perf_counter()

            kernel(
                tenors,
                input_vals,
                targets,
                out,
            )

            t1 = time.perf_counter()

            elapsed = t1 - t0

            # Touch output so the compiler/runtime
            # cannot treat writes as dead.
            checksum = float(out[0, 0, 0])

            if np.isnan(checksum):
                raise RuntimeError("Unexpected NaN.")

            results[method_names[key]].append(elapsed)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Results
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    print("-" * 120)

    print(
        f"{'Kernel Name':<35}"
        f" | {'Median (s)':>12}"
        f" | {'Mean (s)':>12}"
        f" | {'Min (s)':>12}"
        f" | {'Throughput (Interp/s)':>26}"
    )

    print("-" * 120)

    for kernel_name, samples in results.items():
        median_time = statistics.median(samples)
        mean_time = statistics.mean(samples)
        min_time = min(samples)

        throughput = total_interps / median_time

        print(
            f"{kernel_name:<35}"
            f" | {median_time:>12.4f}"
            f" | {mean_time:>12.4f}"
            f" | {min_time:>12.4f}"
            f" | {throughput:>26_.0f}"
        )

    print("-" * 120)

    print()
    print("Notes:")
    print("  • Throughput uses MEDIAN runtime.")
    print("  • Kernel order randomized every round.")
    print("  • Fresh output tensor allocated each run.")
    print("  • Results are less sensitive to cache warming.")
    print()

    print("=" * 60)
    print("   END OF BENCHMARK DEMO")
    print("=" * 60)
    print()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_3d_throughput_benchmark()
```
</file>

```python
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

# Import the Enums and Dispatcher (adjust paths based on your project structure)

    EXTRAP_FLAT,
    EXTRAP_LINEAR,
)

    INTERP_LINEAR,

    axs[0, 0].plot(targets, lin_lin, label="Linear Extrap", linestyle="--")

    # Top-Right: Log-Linear

    axs[0, 1].set_title("Log-Linear Interpolation (Discount Factors)")

    # Bottom-Left: Step Left

    axs[1, 0].plot(targets, step_l_lin, label="Linear Extrap", linestyle=":")
    axs[1, 0].set_title("Step Left (LOCF)")

    # Bottom-Right: Step Right
    axs[1, 1].plot(targets, step_r_flat, label="Flat Extrap", linewidth=4, alpha=0.5)
    axs[1, 1].plot(targets, step_r_lin, label="Linear Extrap", linestyle=":")
    axs[1, 1].set_title("Step Right (NOCB)")

    # Apply common styling to all subplots
    for ax in axs.flat:
        ax.scatter(
            raw_tenors, raw_dfs, color="red", zorder=5, label="Input Pillars", s=50
        )
        ax.axvline(x=1.0, color="gray", linestyle=":", alpha=0.5)
        ax.axvline(x=30.0, color="gray", linestyle=":", alpha=0.5)
        ax.set_xlabel("Tenor (Years)")
        ax.set_ylabel("Value")
        ax.legend()
        ax.grid(True, alpha=0.3)

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.subplots_adjust(top=0.92)  # Adjust for the suptitle
    plt.show()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_full_visualization_demo()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\scripts\demos\market_data\curves\demo_interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_throughput.py">
```python
# scripts/demos/market_data/curves/demo_interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_benchmark.py

from __future__ import annotations

import statistics
import time

import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_defs import (
    interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel,
)


def bytes_to_gib(n: int) -> float:
    return n / (1024**3)


def benchmark_case(
    *,
    n_trades: int,
    k_strikes: int,
    s_scenarios: int,
    runs: int,
) -> dict[str, float]:
    rng = np.random.default_rng(42)

    pillars = np.ascontiguousarray(
        np.sort(
            rng.uniform(
                50.0,
                150.0,
                size=(n_trades, k_strikes),
            ),
            axis=1,
        )
    )

    values = np.ascontiguousarray(
        rng.uniform(
            0.15,
            0.50,
            size=(n_trades, k_strikes, s_scenarios),
        )
    )

    targets = np.ascontiguousarray(
        rng.uniform(
            40.0,
            160.0,
            size=(n_trades, s_scenarios),
        )
    )

    out = np.empty(
        (n_trades, s_scenarios),
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    timings: list[float] = []

    for _ in range(runs):
        # out.fill(0.0)

        t0 = time.perf_counter()

        interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel(
            pillars,
            values,
            targets,
            out,
        )

        elapsed = time.perf_counter() - t0

        checksum = float(out[0, 0])

        if np.isnan(checksum):
            raise RuntimeError("Unexpected NaN.")

        timings.append(elapsed)

    median_time = statistics.median(timings)

    total_lookups = n_trades * s_scenarios

    throughput = total_lookups / median_time

    approx_bytes = values.nbytes + targets.nbytes + out.nbytes

    bandwidth_gib = approx_bytes / median_time / (1024**3)

    return {
        "trades": n_trades,
        "strikes": k_strikes,
        "scenarios": s_scenarios,
        "lookups": total_lookups,
        "median_time": median_time,
        "min_time": min(timings),
        "max_time": max(timings),
        "throughput": throughput,
        "bandwidth_gib": bandwidth_gib,
        "values_gib": bytes_to_gib(values.nbytes),
    }


def run_benchmark() -> None:
    print("=" * 90)
    print("RISKQUANT STRIKE INTERPOLATION PERFORMANCE SUITE")
    print("=" * 90)
    print()

    # ----------------------------------------------------------
    # JIT Warmup
    # ----------------------------------------------------------

    print("Compiling Numba kernel...")

    dummy_pillars = np.array([[90.0, 100.0]])
    dummy_values = np.array([[[0.20], [0.25]]])
    dummy_targets = np.array([[95.0]])
    dummy_out = np.empty((1, 1))

    interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel(
        dummy_pillars,
        dummy_values,
        dummy_targets,
        dummy_out,
    )

    print("Compilation complete.")
    print()

    # ----------------------------------------------------------
    # Scaling Matrix
    # ----------------------------------------------------------

    strike_counts = [5, 15, 30, 60, 120]
    scenario_counts = [100, 1000]

    results = []

    for strikes in strike_counts:
        for scenarios in scenario_counts:
            result = benchmark_case(
                n_trades=10_000,
                k_strikes=strikes,
                s_scenarios=scenarios,
                runs=7,
            )

            results.append(result)

    print("-" * 140)

    print(
        f"{'Strikes':>8} | "
        f"{'Scenarios':>10} | "
        f"{'Values GiB':>10} | "
        f"{'Median(s)':>10} | "
        f"{'Throughput':>18} | "
        f"{'Bandwidth GiB/s':>18}"
    )

    print("-" * 140)

    for r in results:
        print(
            f"{r['strikes']:>8,.0f} | "
            f"{r['scenarios']:>10,.0f} | "
            f"{r['values_gib']:>10.2f} | "
            f"{r['median_time']:>10.4f} | "
            f"{r['throughput']:>18,.0f} | "
            f"{r['bandwidth_gib']:>18.2f}"
        )

    print("-" * 140)

    print()
    print("Interpretation:")
    print()
    print("• If throughput falls sharply as strike count increases,")
    print("  binary search is becoming dominant.")
    print()
    print("• If throughput stays relatively stable as strike count")
    print("  increases, memory traffic dominates.")
    print()
    print("• Bandwidth approaching system memory limits indicates")
    print("  further kernel optimization is unlikely to help.")
    print()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_benchmark()
```
</file>

```python
# scripts/demos/market_data/curves/demo_interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_visualizer.py

"""
DEMO: Strike-Surface Interpolation (Linear + Flat Extrapolation)

This demo visualizes exactly how the
`interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel`
behaves.

Concept
-------
For each trade:

    pillars = known strikes
    values  = volatility smile values at those strikes
    target  = simulated strike (or underlying level)

The kernel:

1. Finds the two surrounding strike pillars.
2. Interpolates linearly between their volatility values.
3. Applies flat extrapolation outside the pillar range.

Flat extrapolation means:

    target < min(strikes)
        -> use first smile value

    target > max(strikes)
        -> use last smile value

Visual Guide
------------
■ = Input strike pillars

— = Continuous interpolated smile produced by the kernel

X = Example Monte-Carlo scenario targets

The plots intentionally include targets both inside and outside
the pillar range so the extrapolation behaviour is obvious.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_defs import (
    interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel,
)


def _build_dense_surface(
    pillars: np.ndarray,
    base_smiles: list[list[float]],
    dense_targets: np.ndarray,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Evaluate the kernel on a dense grid so the interpolated
    smile can be drawn as a continuous line.
    """
    n_trades = pillars.shape[0]
    k_strikes = pillars.shape[1]
    s_dense = dense_targets.shape[1]

    dense_values = np.empty((n_trades, k_strikes, s_dense))

    for s in range(s_dense):
        for n in range(n_trades):
            dense_values[n, :, s] = base_smiles[n]

    dense_out = np.empty((n_trades, s_dense))

    interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel(
        pillars,
        dense_values,
        dense_targets,
        dense_out,
    )

    return dense_out


def _print_results(
    pillars: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Print scenario results to the console."""
    print()
    print("=" * 90)
    print("STRIKE INTERPOLATION RESULTS")
    print("=" * 90)

    for trade_idx in range(targets.shape[0]):
        strike_min = pillars[trade_idx, 0]
        strike_max = pillars[trade_idx, -1]

        print()
        print(
            f"Trade {trade_idx} | Strike Range = [{strike_min:.1f}, {strike_max:.1f}]"
        )

        for scenario_idx in range(targets.shape[1]):
            target = targets[trade_idx, scenario_idx]
            vol = out[trade_idx, scenario_idx]

            if target < strike_min:
                location = "LEFT FLAT EXTRAP"
            elif target > strike_max:
                location = "RIGHT FLAT EXTRAP"
            else:
                location = "INTERPOLATION"

            print(
                f"  Scenario {scenario_idx:<2d} | "
                f"Target = {target:7.2f} | "
                f"Vol = {vol:.6f} | "
                f"{location}"
            )


def run_strike_surface_demo() -> None:
    # ==================================================================
    # DEMO INPUTS
    # ==================================================================

    n_trades = 2
    k_strikes = 5
    s_scenarios = 5

    # Strike grids
    pillars = np.array(
        [
            [80.0, 90.0, 100.0, 110.0, 120.0],
            [50.0, 75.0, 100.0, 125.0, 150.0],
        ],
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    # Volatility smiles
    base_smile_0 = [0.25, 0.22, 0.20, 0.23, 0.28]
    base_smile_1 = [0.40, 0.35, 0.30, 0.32, 0.38]

    base_smiles = [base_smile_0, base_smile_1]

    # Build values tensor expected by the kernel
    values = np.empty((n_trades, k_strikes, s_scenarios))

    for s in range(s_scenarios):
        values[0, :, s] = base_smile_0
        values[1, :, s] = base_smile_1

    # Example scenario targets
    #
    # Includes:
    #   - left extrapolation
    #   - interpolation
    #   - exact pillar hits
    #   - right extrapolation
    targets = np.array(
        [
            [70.0, 85.0, 100.0, 115.0, 130.0],
            [40.0, 62.5, 100.0, 112.5, 160.0],
        ],
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    # ==================================================================
    # RUN KERNEL
    # ==================================================================

    out = np.empty((n_trades, s_scenarios))

    interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel(
        pillars,
        values,
        targets,
        out,
    )

    _print_results(
        pillars=pillars,
        targets=targets,
        out=out,
    )

    # ==================================================================
    # DENSE EVALUATION FOR PLOTTING
    # ==================================================================

    s_dense = 500

    dense_targets_1d = np.linspace(40.0, 170.0, s_dense)

    dense_targets = np.vstack([dense_targets_1d for _ in range(n_trades)])

    dense_out = _build_dense_surface(
        pillars=pillars,
        base_smiles=base_smiles,
        dense_targets=dense_targets,
    )

    # ==================================================================
    # PLOTTING
    # ==================================================================

    fig, axs = plt.subplots(
        nrows=1,
        ncols=2,
        figsize=(16, 7),
    )

    fig.suptitle(
        "Strike Surface Interpolation\nLinear Interpolation + Flat Extrapolation",
        fontsize=16,
    )

    trade_titles = [
        "Trade 0 — Narrow Strike Grid",
        "Trade 1 — Wide Strike Grid",
    ]

    for trade_idx in range(n_trades):
        ax = axs[trade_idx]

        strike_min = pillars[trade_idx, 0]
        strike_max = pillars[trade_idx, -1]

        # --------------------------------------------------------------
        # Continuous smile
        # --------------------------------------------------------------

        ax.plot(
            dense_targets[trade_idx],
            dense_out[trade_idx],
            linewidth=2,
            label="Kernel Output",
        )

        # --------------------------------------------------------------
        # Input pillars
        # --------------------------------------------------------------

        ax.scatter(
            pillars[trade_idx],
            values[trade_idx, :, 0],
            marker="s",
            s=100,
            zorder=5,
            label="Input Pillars",
        )

        # --------------------------------------------------------------
        # Extrapolation boundaries
        # --------------------------------------------------------------

        ax.axvline(
            strike_min,
            linestyle="--",
            alpha=0.5,
            label="Pillar Boundary",
        )

        ax.axvline(
            strike_max,
            linestyle="--",
            alpha=0.5,
        )

        # --------------------------------------------------------------
        # Scenario locations
        # --------------------------------------------------------------

        for scenario_idx in range(s_scenarios):
            ax.scatter(
                targets[trade_idx, scenario_idx],
                out[trade_idx, scenario_idx],
                marker="X",
                s=140,
                zorder=10,
            )

            ax.annotate(
                f"S{scenario_idx}",
                (
                    targets[trade_idx, scenario_idx],
                    out[trade_idx, scenario_idx],
                ),
                xytext=(5, 5),
                textcoords="offset points",
            )

        # --------------------------------------------------------------
        # Region labels
        # --------------------------------------------------------------

        y_mid = (np.min(dense_out[trade_idx]) + np.max(dense_out[trade_idx])) / 2.0

        ax.text(
            strike_min - 15,
            y_mid,
            "Flat\nExtrap",
            ha="center",
            va="center",
        )

        ax.text(
            (strike_min + strike_max) / 2,
            y_mid,
            "Linear\nInterpolation",
            ha="center",
            va="center",
        )

        ax.text(
            strike_max + 15,
            y_mid,
            "Flat\nExtrap",
            ha="center",
            va="center",
        )

        # --------------------------------------------------------------
        # Cosmetics
        # --------------------------------------------------------------

        ax.set_title(trade_titles[trade_idx])
        ax.set_xlabel("Target Strike / Simulated Underlying")
        ax.set_ylabel("Implied Volatility")
        ax.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
        ax.legend()

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.show()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run_strike_surface_demo()
```
</file>

Top-level private module (mirrors `_njit.py`) so leaf cons...

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\_arrays.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/_arrays.py
"""
Cross-cutting NumPy array primitives shared across packages.

Top-level private module (mirrors `_njit.py`) so leaf consumers in
`proxy_resolver`, `simlab`, `portfolio`, `market_data`, and `credit` can import
these without a layering inversion.

No domain imports — pure array algebra; domain sentinels (e.g., `INVALID_UIX`
or `NO_UID_MATCH_IDX_I32`) are passed in by callers rather than imported here.

Contents
--------
unique_first_seen                        : dtype-preserving first-seen dedup + inverse.
searchsorted_membership                  : bool found-mask over a sorted-unique universe.
searchsorted_or_raise                    : STRICT INNER JOIN. Raises on any miss.
searchsorted_soft_join                   : SAFE LEFT JOIN. Probes existence, maps misses to a sentinel.
searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32 : UNSAFE LEFT JOIN. Bypasses probe, blindly maps `< 0` to a sentinel.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Final

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
from numpy.typing import NDArray

# Centralized preview limit for error-message diagnostics to prevent
# massive stdout dumps on join failures.
_PREVIEW: Final[int] = 10


def _as_c_contig_f64(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.float64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)


def _as_c_contig_f32(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.float32]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.float32)


def _as_c_contig_i64(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int64]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int64)


def _as_c_contig_i32(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int32]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int32)


def _as_c_contig_i16(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int16]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int16)


def _as_c_contig_i8(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.int8]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.int8)


def _as_c_contig_bool(arr: NDArray) -> NDArray[np.bool_]:
    return np.ascontiguousarray(arr, dtype=np.bool_)


def unique_first_seen(
    arr: npt.NDArray,
) -> tuple[npt.NDArray, npt.NDArray[np.int32]]:
    """
    First-seen dedup: dtype-preserving in the values, int32 in the inverse.

    `np.unique` sorts; we need first-seen order so shock-compute /
    proxy-resolution plans are deterministic across reruns of identical inputs.

    Returns
    -------
    unique_first_seen
        (K,) — unique values in first-seen order, same dtype as ``arr``.

    inverse_first_seen
        (N,) int32 — for each input position, its index in ``unique_first_seen``.
    """
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr[:0].copy(), np.empty(0, dtype=np.int32)

    _, first_idx, inverse_sorted = np.unique(
        arr, return_index=True, return_inverse=True
    )
    # Reorder sorted-unique into first-seen order.
    order = np.argsort(first_idx, kind="mergesort")
    out_unique = arr[first_idx[order]]

    # Build sorted_idx -> first_seen_idx remap, then apply to the inverse.
    sorted_to_first_seen = np.empty(order.size, dtype=np.int32)
    sorted_to_first_seen[order] = np.arange(order.size, dtype=np.int32)
    inverse_first_seen = sorted_to_first_seen[inverse_sorted].astype(
        np.int32, copy=False
    )
    return out_unique, inverse_first_seen


# =========================================================================
# VECTORIZED JOIN PRIMITIVES
# =========================================================================
#
# `universe` MUST be sorted ascending and unique (caller's invariant).
# These do NOT validate that invariant; they trust it, exactly as the
# hand-rolled sites they replace did.


def searchsorted_membership(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
    """
    EXISTENCE CHECK: Per-element membership of `raw` in sorted-unique `universe`.

    Returns a `(raw.size,)` bool mask, ``True`` where the value is present.
    No sentinel handling — callers that treat ``raw < 0`` as "not required"
    should mask those out before calling, or ignore the corresponding mask entries.
    """
    if raw.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.bool_)
    if universe.size == 0:
        return np.zeros(raw.shape[0], dtype=np.bool_)

    idx = np.searchsorted(universe, raw)
    in_bounds = idx < universe.size
    found = np.zeros(raw.shape[0], dtype=np.bool_)
    found[in_bounds] = universe[idx[in_bounds]] == raw[in_bounds]
    return found


def searchsorted_or_raise(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
    ctx: str = "ids",
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int64]:
    """
    STRICT INNER JOIN: Locate every `raw` value in sorted-unique `universe`.
    Raises ValueError on any missing target.

    Returns the `(raw.size,)` int64 index array (the natural `np.searchsorted`
    dtype — callers gather with it, no downcast needed).
    """
    if raw.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.int64)

    n = universe.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: Universe is empty, {raw.shape[0]} target ID(s) cannot be resolved."
        )

    pos = np.searchsorted(universe, raw)
    np.clip(pos, 0, n - 1, out=pos)
    matched = universe[pos] == raw

    if not matched.all():
        bad = np.unique(raw[~matched])[:_PREVIEW].tolist()
        n_bad = int((~matched).sum())
        raise ValueError(
            f"{ctx}: {n_bad} ID(s) absent from the universe. First {len(bad)}: {bad}"
        )

    return pos


def searchsorted_soft_join(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
    sentinel: np.int32,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    SAFE LEFT JOIN: Probes existence. Maps ANY missing `raw` value to `sentinel`.

    Use when absent target IDs are a valid, expected runtime case (e.g.,
    a netting set whose obligor is not modelled in the target portfolio).

    Returns `(raw.size,)` int32 indices. Caller filters via ``keep = pos >= 0``
    (assuming sentinel is negative).
    """
    n = universe.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.full(raw.shape[0], sentinel, dtype=np.int32)

    pos = np.searchsorted(universe, raw)
    np.clip(pos, 0, n - 1, out=pos)
    hit = universe[pos] == raw
    return np.where(hit, pos, sentinel).astype(np.int32, copy=False)


def searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
    *,
    universe: npt.NDArray,
    raw: npt.NDArray,
    sentinel: np.int32,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
    """
    UNSAFE LEFT JOIN: High-performance ID translation with sentinel preservation.

    `raw < 0` rows are explicitly treated as sentinels and mapped to `sentinel`.

    CRITICAL: All other IDs (>= 0) are BLINDLY ASSUMED present in `universe`.
    This primitive does NOT probe membership (no equality check, no bounds clip).
    If a missing positive ID is passed, it will silently map to the next-largest
    element's index. Only use this when upstream referential integrity is 100%
    guaranteed by prior validation phases.
    """
    uix = np.searchsorted(universe, raw).astype(np.int32, copy=False)
    uix[raw < 0] = sentinel
    return uix
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/enums/discount_basis.py
"""
Discount basis taxonomy for IR discount curves.

A single currency carries multiple discount curves: one per OIS / IBOR
tenor / CSA collateral choice. The basis enum is the per-(currency,
basis) discriminator the ingest layer uses to resolve a trade's
discount-curve linkage when it specifies a non-default basis.

Sentinel:
  NOT_A_DISCOUNT_CURVE - explicitly marks a curve as non-discount. All
  curve_meta rows carry a basis; non-discount curves carry this value.
  The validator rejects any attempt to use a non-discount curve as a
  discount-curve linkage.

Standard convention: the firm-wide default discount basis is OIS. The
static-config validator enforces exactly one is_default_discount=True
curve per currency that has any discount curve, and that the default's
basis is OIS.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum


class DiscountBasis(IntEnum):
    NOT_A_DISCOUNT_CURVE = 0

    OIS = 1

    IBOR_1M = 11
    IBOR_3M = 13
    IBOR_6M = 16

    CSA_USD = 30
    CSA_EUR = 31
    CSA_GBP = 32
    CSA_CHF = 33
    CSA_JPY = 34

    INTERNAL_FVA = 90


# Firm-wide standard. The default discount curve per currency must use this basis.
FIRM_DEFAULT_DISCOUNT_BASIS: DiscountBasis = DiscountBasis.OIS


VALID_DISCOUNT_BASES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(b.name for b in DiscountBasis)

DISCOUNT_BASES_EXCLUDING_SENTINEL: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    b.name for b in DiscountBasis if b is not DiscountBasis.NOT_A_DISCOUNT_CURVE
)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/enums/financial_conventions.py
"""
Standard financial convention enumerations.

These are system-defined constants — not user configuration.
Users reference these string values in instrument definitions and
curve configurations.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from enum import Enum

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Day count conventions
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class DayCount(str, Enum):
    ACT_365F = "ACT_365F"
    ACT_360 = "ACT_360"
    DC_30_360 = "30_360"


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Business day conventions
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class BusinessDayConvention(str, Enum):
    FOLLOWING = "FOLLOWING"
    MOD_FOLLOWING = "MOD_FOLLOWING"
    PRECEDING = "PRECEDING"
    MOD_PRECEDING = "MOD_PRECEDING"
    NONE = "NONE"


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Compounding conventions
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


class CompoundingType(str, Enum):
    SIMPLE = "SIMPLE"
    CONTINUOUS = "CONTINUOUS"
    PERIODIC = "PERIODIC"


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class CompoundingConvention:
    name: str
    compounding_type: CompoundingType
    # None for SIMPLE and CONTINUOUS — frequency is not applicable.
    frequency_per_year: int | None


COMPOUNDING_CONVENTIONS: dict[str, CompoundingConvention] = {
    "SIMPLE": CompoundingConvention("SIMPLE", CompoundingType.SIMPLE, None),
    "CONTINUOUS": CompoundingConvention("CONTINUOUS", CompoundingType.CONTINUOUS, None),
    "ANNUAL": CompoundingConvention("ANNUAL", CompoundingType.PERIODIC, 1),
    "SEMI_ANNUAL": CompoundingConvention("SEMI_ANNUAL", CompoundingType.PERIODIC, 2),
    "QUARTERLY": CompoundingConvention("QUARTERLY", CompoundingType.PERIODIC, 4),
    "MONTHLY": CompoundingConvention("MONTHLY", CompoundingType.PERIODIC, 12),
    "BIWEEKLY": CompoundingConvention("BIWEEKLY", CompoundingType.PERIODIC, 26),
    "WEEKLY": CompoundingConvention("WEEKLY", CompoundingType.PERIODIC, 52),
}

# Flat sets for O(1) membership checks in validation.
VALID_DAY_COUNTS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(e.value for e in DayCount)
VALID_BUSINESS_DAY_CONVENTIONS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    e.value for e in BusinessDayConvention
)
VALID_COMPOUNDING_CONVENTIONS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    COMPOUNDING_CONVENTIONS.keys()
)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("=" * 70)
    print(" RISKQUANT FINANCIAL CONVENTIONS REVIEW ".center(70, "#"))
    print("=" * 70)

    # 1. Print Day Count Conventions
    print("\n--- Day Count Conventions ---")
    for dc in DayCount:
        print(f"  ├── {dc.value}")

    # 2. Print Business Day Conventions
    print("\n--- Business Day Conventions ---")
    for bdc in BusinessDayConvention:
        print(f"  ├── {bdc.value}")

    # 3. Print Compounding Conventions Map
    print("\n--- Compounding Conventions Summary ---")
    print(f"{'Key':<15} | {'Type':<12} | {'Frequency / Year':<16}")
    print("-" * 50)
    for key, conv in COMPOUNDING_CONVENTIONS.items():
        freq_str = (
            str(conv.frequency_per_year)
            if conv.frequency_per_year is not None
            else "N/A"
        )
        print(f"{key:<15} | {conv.compounding_type.value:<12} | {freq_str:<16}")

    # 4. Integrity Checks
    print("\n--- Static Set Validation Checks ---")
    print(f"✓ Day Count Lookups         : {len(VALID_DAY_COUNTS)} values loaded.")
    print(
        f"✓ Business Day Lookups      : {len(VALID_BUSINESS_DAY_CONVENTIONS)} values loaded."
    )
    print(
        f"✓ Compounding Type Lookups  : {len(VALID_COMPOUNDING_CONVENTIONS)} keys loaded."
    )

    # Assert that all keys in the lookups match expectation
    assert len(VALID_COMPOUNDING_CONVENTIONS) == len(COMPOUNDING_CONVENTIONS)
    print("✓ Validation Integrity      : Pass")
    print("=" * 70)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/enums/roll_methods.py
"""
Roll method taxonomy for spot roll rules.

Replaces the prior two ad-hoc declarations: the string-keyed
`_VALID_SPOT_ROLL_METHODS` frozenset in validate.py and the
`_SpotRollMethod` constant class in rf_resolution.py.

A roll method specifies how a relative tenor token (e.g. [M+1], [Y+0],
[HY+0]) resolves to an absolute risk-factor name for a given as-of date.
The (XAxisKind, RollMethod) pair determines both the token grammar and
the absolute name format; allowed pairs are enumerated explicitly in
ALLOWED_X_AXIS_KIND_ROLL_METHOD_PAIRS so the validator and the
resolution dispatch cannot drift.

Absolute name formats by combination:
  (OFFSET_M,  CALENDAR_PERIOD)     -> "<curve>_YYYYMM"
  (OFFSET_M,  HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE)   -> "<curve>_YYYYMMDD"
  (OFFSET_Y,  CALENDAR_PERIOD)     -> "<curve>_YYYY"
  (OFFSET_Y,  ROLL_ON_MMDD)        -> "<curve>_YYYY"
  (OFFSET_HY, HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE)   -> "<curve>_YYYYMMDD"
  (OFFSET_HY, HALF_YEAR_BY_LABEL)  -> "<curve>_YYYY_FH" or "<curve>_YYYY_BH"
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum

from riskquant.config.enums.x_axis_kinds import XAxisKind


class RollMethod(IntEnum):
    CALENDAR_PERIOD = 0
    ROLL_ON_MMDD = 1
    HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE = 2
    HALF_YEAR_BY_LABEL = 3


VALID_ROLL_METHODS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(m.name for m in RollMethod)


ALLOWED_X_AXIS_KIND_ROLL_METHOD_PAIRS: frozenset[tuple[XAxisKind, RollMethod]] = frozenset(
    {
        (XAxisKind.OFFSET_M, RollMethod.CALENDAR_PERIOD),
        (XAxisKind.OFFSET_M, RollMethod.HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE),
        (XAxisKind.OFFSET_Y, RollMethod.CALENDAR_PERIOD),
        (XAxisKind.OFFSET_Y, RollMethod.ROLL_ON_MMDD),
        (XAxisKind.OFFSET_HY, RollMethod.HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE),
        (XAxisKind.OFFSET_HY, RollMethod.HALF_YEAR_BY_LABEL),
    }
)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/config/enums/x_axis_kinds.py
"""
X-axis kind taxonomy for curves and surfaces.

Replaces the legacy (TenorType, AliasType, AliasKind) triple. A single
XAxisKind tells the resolution layer exactly how a curve / surface point
addresses its position on the time axis:

  LABEL      - fixed, as-of-independent string labels (e.g. [ON], [1W], [1M]).
               Used by IR conventional curves.
  OFFSET_M   - monthly offsets from a roll-determined spot month (e.g. [M+0],
               [M-1], [M+12]). Used by monthly commodity contracts.
  OFFSET_Y   - annual offsets from a roll-determined spot year (e.g. [Y+0],
               [Y-2]). Used by annual commodity contracts.
  OFFSET_HY  - half-year offsets from a roll-determined spot half (e.g.
               [HY+0], [HY+3]). Used by half-year power contracts.

The X-axis kind defines token grammar only. How an offset resolves to an
absolute date label (calendar period, roll-on-MMDD, half-year boundary)
is independent and lives on the spot roll rule via RollMethod. Allowed
(XAxisKind, RollMethod) pairs are enumerated explicitly in
ALLOWED_X_AXIS_KIND_ROLL_METHOD_PAIRS.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from enum import IntEnum
from re import Pattern


class XAxisKind(IntEnum):
    LABEL = 0
    OFFSET_M = 1
    OFFSET_Y = 2
    OFFSET_HY = 3


# Token grammars by kind. LABEL matches the standard IR conventions plus the
# generic [\d+(D|W|M|Y)] form. OFFSET kinds match a strictly signed integer
# inside brackets prefixed by the unit (M, Y, HY).
X_AXIS_TOKEN_REGEX: dict[XAxisKind, Pattern[str]] = {
    XAxisKind.LABEL: re.compile(r"^\[(?:ON|SN|TN|\d+D|\d+W|\d+M|\d+Y)\]$"),
    XAxisKind.OFFSET_M: re.compile(r"^\[M[+-]\d+\]$"),
    XAxisKind.OFFSET_Y: re.compile(r"^\[Y[+-]\d+\]$"),
    XAxisKind.OFFSET_HY: re.compile(r"^\[HY[+-]\d+\]$"),
}

X_AXIS_TOKEN_EXAMPLE: dict[XAxisKind, str] = {
    XAxisKind.LABEL: "[3M]",
    XAxisKind.OFFSET_M: "[M+1]",
    XAxisKind.OFFSET_Y: "[Y-2]",
    XAxisKind.OFFSET_HY: "[HY+0]",
}

VALID_X_AXIS_KINDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(k.name for k in XAxisKind)

# OFFSET kinds require a spot roll rule. LABEL does not (and must not).
X_AXIS_KINDS_REQUIRING_ROLL: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    k.name for k in (XAxisKind.OFFSET_M, XAxisKind.OFFSET_Y, XAxisKind.OFFSET_HY)
)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/enums/y_axis_kinds.py
"""
Y-axis kind taxonomy for surfaces.

Replaces surface_y_axis_types.py. A Y-axis kind tells the surface
interpolator how to interpret a surface point's y coordinate.

Forward-relative coordinates (LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD, MONEYNESS_FWD, DELTA_CALL)
depend on the underlying forward at the relevant expiry; the surface
itself does not store the forward — the pricer derives it from the
underlying curve at runtime.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum


class YAxisKind(IntEnum):
    STRIKE = 0
    LOG_STRIKE = 1
    MONEYNESS_SPOT = 2
    MONEYNESS_FWD = 3
    LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT = 4
    LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD = 5
    DELTA_CALL = 6


VALID_Y_AXIS_KINDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(k.name for k in YAxisKind)
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\config\namespace.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/config/namespace.py
"""
Global ID Namespace definitions and validation contracts.
"""

from typing import NamedTuple

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.exceptions import ConfigurationError

_BLOCK_SIZE: int = 250_000_000  # one block

_NODE_MIN_ID = 0  # node id 0 stays valid
_NODE_MAX_ID = _BLOCK_SIZE - 1  # [0,           249_999_999]
_CURVE_MIN_ID = _BLOCK_SIZE  # [250_000_000, 499_999_999]
_CURVE_MAX_ID = 2 * _BLOCK_SIZE - 1
_SURFACE_MIN_ID = 2 * _BLOCK_SIZE  # [500_000_000, 749_999_999]
_SURFACE_MAX_ID = 3 * _BLOCK_SIZE - 1


class IdNamespaceBounds(NamedTuple):
    min_id: int
    max_id: int
    name: str


assert (
    _NODE_MIN_ID
    < _NODE_MAX_ID
    < _CURVE_MIN_ID
    < _CURVE_MAX_ID
    < _SURFACE_MIN_ID
    < _SURFACE_MAX_ID
), "ID namespace boundaries must be strictly increasing and non-overlapping."


assert max(_NODE_MAX_ID, _CURVE_MAX_ID, _SURFACE_MAX_ID) <= np.iinfo(np.int32).max


class GlobalIdNamespace:
    """Global ID boundaries for RiskQuant execution mapping."""

    NODES = IdNamespaceBounds(
        min_id=_NODE_MIN_ID,
        max_id=_NODE_MAX_ID,
        name="Nodes (Risk Factors, dimension = 0)",
    )
    CURVES = IdNamespaceBounds(
        min_id=_CURVE_MIN_ID,
        max_id=_CURVE_MAX_ID,
        name="Curves (Containers, dimension = 1)",
    )
    SURFACES = IdNamespaceBounds(
        min_id=_SURFACE_MIN_ID,
        max_id=_SURFACE_MAX_ID,
        name="Surfaces (Containers, dimension = 2)",
    )


def enforce_id_namespace(
    *,
    ids: npt.NDArray[np.integer],
    bounds: IdNamespaceBounds,
) -> None:
    """
    Vectorized assertion that all IDs fall within their allowed global block.
    Raises ConfigurationError immediately on violation.
    """
    out_of_bounds = ids[(ids < bounds.min_id) | (ids > bounds.max_id)]

    if out_of_bounds.size > 0:
        bad_examples = np.unique(out_of_bounds)[:20]
        raise ConfigurationError(
            f"CRITICAL: {bounds.name} ID Namespace Violation.\n"
            f"Expected IDs between {bounds.min_id:1_.0f} and {bounds.max_id:1_.0f}.\n"
            f"Found {out_of_bounds.size:1,.0f} violations. Examples: {bad_examples}"
        )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # Quick sanity check of namespace boundaries
    print("Global ID Namespace Boundaries:")
    for namespace in [
        GlobalIdNamespace.NODES,
        GlobalIdNamespace.CURVES,
        GlobalIdNamespace.SURFACES,
    ]:
        print(f"{namespace.name}: {namespace.min_id:1_.0f} to {namespace.max_id:1_.0f}")
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/proxy_config/audit.py
"""
Tabular audit of a compiled ProxyTopology.

Imports Pandas deliberately — auditing is an edge-only diagnostic, not a
runtime path. Never imported by valuation, simlab, or any kernel.

This module also hosts the consolidated name-resolved DataFrame view
(`to_df`) that used to live as a method on ProxyTopology. Keeping it
here ensures the runtime container itself stays Pandas-free.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.config.proxy_config.proxy_topology import ProxyTopology
from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig

_SECTION = "=" * 72
_SUB = "-" * 72


def _header(title: str) -> None:
    print(_SECTION)
    print(title)
    print(_SECTION)


def _sub(title: str) -> None:
    print(f"\n{_SUB}")
    print(title)
    print(_SUB)


# ============================================================
# Name-resolved DataFrame projection
# ============================================================


def to_df(topology: ProxyTopology, static_config: RiskStaticConfig) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Consolidate container and rf proxies into a single name+id DataFrame.

    Mirrors the legacy `ProxyTopology.to_df()` output. Lives here rather
    than on the runtime container so the runtime stays Pandas-free.

    Columns
    -------
        proxying_level, required_name, required_id,
        proxy_name, proxy_id, vol_scaling_factor
    """
    name_to_cid = static_config.container_name_to_id
    name_to_rfid = static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id

    records: list[dict[str, object]] = []

    for pm in topology.container_proxies.values():
        records.append(
            {
                "proxying_level": "CONTAINER",
                "required_name": pm.required_name,
                "required_id": int(name_to_cid[pm.required_name]),
                "proxy_name": pm.proxy_name,
                "proxy_id": int(name_to_cid[pm.proxy_name]),
                "vol_scaling_factor": float(pm.vol_scaling_factor),
            }
        )

    for pm in topology.rf_proxies.values():
        records.append(
            {
                "proxying_level": "RISK_FACTOR",
                "required_name": pm.required_name,
                "required_id": int(name_to_rfid[pm.required_name]),
                "proxy_name": pm.proxy_name,
                "proxy_id": int(name_to_rfid[pm.proxy_name]),
                "vol_scaling_factor": float(pm.vol_scaling_factor),
            }
        )

    return pd.DataFrame(
        records,
        columns=[
            "proxying_level",
            "required_name",
            "required_id",
            "proxy_name",
            "proxy_id",
            "vol_scaling_factor",
        ],
    )


# ============================================================
# Section renderers
# ============================================================


def _audit_summary(topology: ProxyTopology) -> None:
    _header("ProxyTopology Audit")
    print(f"  Container proxies   : {topology.num_container_proxies}")
    print(f"  RF proxies          : {topology.num_rf_proxies}")
    print(f"  Total               : {topology.num_total_proxies}")
    print(f"  c_proxy array size  : {topology.container_proxy_id.size}")
    print(f"  rf_proxy array size : {topology.rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id.size}")
    arr = topology.container_proxy_id
    n_set_c = int((arr >= 0).sum())
    arr = topology.rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id
    n_set_rf = int((arr >= 0).sum())
    print(f"  c_proxy slots set   : {n_set_c}")
    print(f"  rf_proxy slots set  : {n_set_rf}")


def _audit_proxy_table(
    topology: ProxyTopology, static_config: RiskStaticConfig, n: int
) -> None:
    _sub("1. Name-resolved proxy table")
    df = to_df(topology, static_config)
    if df.empty:
        print("(no proxies registered)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))


def _audit_vol_scale_distribution(topology: ProxyTopology, n: int) -> None:
    _sub("2. vol_scaling_factor distribution")
    scales = [pm.vol_scaling_factor for pm in topology.container_proxies.values()] + [
        pm.vol_scaling_factor for pm in topology.rf_proxies.values()
    ]
    if not scales:
        print("(no proxies)")
        return
    arr = np.asarray(scales, dtype=np.float64)
    print(f"  count = {arr.size}")
    print(f"  min   = {arr.min():.4f}")
    print(f"  max   = {arr.max():.4f}")
    print(f"  mean  = {arr.mean():.4f}")
    print(f"  != 1  = {int((arr != 1.0).sum())} entries differ from identity")


def _audit_proxied_containers_by_kind(
    topology: ProxyTopology, static_config: RiskStaticConfig, n: int
) -> None:
    _sub("3. Container proxies — by container kind (curve / surface)")
    if not topology.container_proxies:
        print("(none)")
        return
    name_to_cid = static_config.container_name_to_id
    kind_arr = static_config.container_topology_type
    rows = []
    for pm in topology.container_proxies.values():
        req_id = int(name_to_cid[pm.required_name])
        prx_id = int(name_to_cid[pm.proxy_name])
        kind_label = {1: "CURVE", 2: "SURFACE"}.get(
            int(kind_arr[static_config.container_slot(req_id)]), "?"
        )
        rows.append(
            {
                "kind": kind_label,
                "required_name": pm.required_name,
                "required_id": req_id,
                "proxy_name": pm.proxy_name,
                "proxy_id": prx_id,
                "vol_scale": float(pm.vol_scaling_factor),
            }
        )
    df = (
        pd.DataFrame(rows).sort_values(["kind", "required_name"]).reset_index(drop=True)
    )
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))


def _audit_proxied_rfs_by_parent(
    topology: ProxyTopology, static_config: RiskStaticConfig, n: int
) -> None:
    _sub("4. RF proxies — by parent kind (node / curve point / surface point)")
    if not topology.rf_proxies:
        print("(none)")
        return
    name_to_rfid = static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id
    parent_kind = static_config.rf_id_to_parent_topology_type
    rows = []
    for pm in topology.rf_proxies.values():
        rfid = int(name_to_rfid[pm.required_name])
        prx_id = int(name_to_rfid[pm.proxy_name])
        kind_label = {0: "NODE", 1: "CURVE_POINT", 2: "SURFACE_POINT"}.get(
            int(parent_kind[rfid]), "?"
        )
        rows.append(
            {
                "parent_kind": kind_label,
                "required_name": pm.required_name,
                "required_id": rfid,
                "proxy_name": pm.proxy_name,
                "proxy_id": prx_id,
                "vol_scale": float(pm.vol_scaling_factor),
            }
        )
    df = (
        pd.DataFrame(rows)
        .sort_values(["parent_kind", "required_name"])
        .reset_index(drop=True)
    )
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))


def _audit_chain_check(topology: ProxyTopology) -> None:
    _sub("5. Chain check — every proxy must point at a terminal name")
    for level_label, proxies in (
        ("container", topology.container_proxies),
        ("risk_factor", topology.rf_proxies),
    ):
        required_set = set(proxies.keys())
        chained = [
            (pm.required_name, pm.proxy_name)
            for pm in proxies.values()
            if pm.proxy_name in required_set
        ]
        if chained:
            # validate_proxy_maps would have already rejected this; this
            # branch only triggers if someone built ProxyTopology directly
            # without calling the validator.
            print(f"  [{level_label}] CHAINED  ({len(chained)} pair(s))")
            for req, prx in chained[:5]:
                print(f"    {req!r} -> {prx!r}  (but {prx!r} is also proxied)")
        else:
            print(f"  [{level_label}] OK")


# ============================================================
# Public API
# ============================================================


def audit_proxy_topology(
    topology: ProxyTopology,
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
    n: int = 20,
) -> None:
    """
    Print a tabular audit of every section of the compiled topology.

    Parameters
    ----------
    topology
        Compiled topology to audit.
    static_config
        Source of name <-> id mappings for human-readable rendering.
    n
        Row cap per section. Use a large number for full dumps.
    """
    _audit_summary(topology)
    _audit_proxy_table(topology, static_config, n)
    _audit_vol_scale_distribution(topology, n)
    _audit_proxied_containers_by_kind(topology, static_config, n)
    _audit_proxied_rfs_by_parent(topology, static_config, n)
    _audit_chain_check(topology)
    print("\n" + _SECTION)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    from riskquant.config.proxy_config.compile import (
        compile_proxy_topology_from_xlsx,
    )
    from riskquant.config.static_config.compile import (
        compile_risk_static_config_from_xlsx,
    )
    from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_success_message

    print_calling_file()

    DEV_DATA_DIR: str = "C:/Users/mharvey/Documents/code/riskquant/data/canonical_examples"

    rsc = compile_risk_static_config_from_xlsx(
        path=f"{DEV_DATA_DIR}/canonical_risk_static_config.xlsx"
    )
    topology = compile_proxy_topology_from_xlsx(
        path=f"{DEV_DATA_DIR}/canonical_proxy_config.xlsx",
        static_config=rsc,
    )

    topology.summary()
    audit_proxy_topology(topology, rsc, n=200)

    print_success_message()
```

```python
# src/riskquant/config/proxy_config/compile.py
"""
Single entry-point for building a ProxyTopology from source data.

Pipeline:
    xlsx  ->  load  ->  validate_df  ->  partition  ->  validate_maps  ->  build  ->  ProxyTopology

The compile step is the only place the raw DataFrame is consumed. After
this layer no caller reads strings, levels, or vol-scaling factors as
named columns: the runtime container exposes only dense int arrays.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.config.proxy_config.loader import load_proxy_config_xlsx_into_df
from riskquant.config.proxy_config.proxy_topology import ProxyTopology
from riskquant.config.proxy_config.schema_objects import ProxyMap
from riskquant.config.proxy_config.validate import (
    validate_proxy_config_df,
    validate_proxy_maps,
)
from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig

# ============================================================
# Internal helpers
# ============================================================


def _build_proxy_map_dict(level_df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[str, ProxyMap]:
    """
    Vectorized: extract typed columns from the level-partitioned df and
    materialize one ProxyMap per row. No iterrows.

    `level_df` has been validated upstream — required_name uniqueness
    per level is guaranteed, so the dict keyed by required_name has no
    collisions.
    """
    if level_df.empty:
        return {}

    required_names = level_df["required_name"].astype(str).str.strip().to_numpy()
    proxy_names = level_df["proxy_name"].astype(str).str.strip().to_numpy()
    vol_scales = level_df["vol_scaling_factor"].astype(np.float64).to_numpy()

    return {
        str(req): ProxyMap(
            required_name=str(req),
            proxy_name=str(prx),
            vol_scaling_factor=float(scl),
        )
        for req, prx, scl in zip(required_names, proxy_names, vol_scales, strict=True)
    }


# ============================================================
# Public API
# ============================================================


def compile_proxy_topology_from_df(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
) -> ProxyTopology:
    """
    Validate and compile an active-filtered proxy DataFrame into a
    ProxyTopology.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df
        Active-filtered proxy DataFrame from the loader. Must have
        columns: proxying_level, required_name, proxy_name,
        vol_scaling_factor.
    static_config
        Compiled static config used to resolve names to ids and to
        validate cross-level conflicts via the static config's
        parent-kind / parent-id arrays.

    Returns
    -------
    ProxyTopology
        Fully compiled, Pandas-free runtime topology with dense int32
        lookup arrays already materialized.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        On any validation failure — Stage 1 (DataFrame schema/type/range),
        Stage 2 (FK / chain / cross-level conflict).
    """
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Stage 1: DataFrame-tier validation                                 #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    validate_proxy_config_df(df)

    if df.empty:
        # Build a no-op topology: all lookups pass through unchanged.
        return ProxyTopology.build(
            container_proxies={},
            rf_proxies={},
            static_config=static_config,
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Partition by level (vectorized; no row loop)                       #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    level_norm = df["proxying_level"].astype(str).str.upper()
    container_df = df.loc[level_norm == "CONTAINER"]
    rf_df = df.loc[level_norm == "RISK_FACTOR"]

    container_proxies = _build_proxy_map_dict(container_df)
    rf_proxies = _build_proxy_map_dict(rf_df)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Stage 2: cross-table validation                                    #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    validate_proxy_maps(
        container_proxies=container_proxies,
        rf_proxies=rf_proxies,
        static_config=static_config,
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Assemble: build dense arrays inside ProxyTopology.build            #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    return ProxyTopology.build(
        container_proxies=container_proxies,
        rf_proxies=rf_proxies,
        static_config=static_config,
    )


def compile_proxy_topology_from_xlsx(
    path: str | Path,
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
    sheet_name: str | None = "proxy_config",
) -> ProxyTopology:
    """
    Load, validate, and compile a proxy-config workbook into a ProxyTopology.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path
        Path to the Excel workbook.
    static_config
        Compiled static config for name validation and ID resolution.
    sheet_name
        Sheet to read. Defaults to "proxy_config".
    """
    df = load_proxy_config_xlsx_into_df(path=path, sheet_name=sheet_name)
    return compile_proxy_topology_from_df(df=df, static_config=static_config)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    from riskquant.config.static_config.compile import (
        compile_risk_static_config_from_xlsx,
    )
    from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_success_message

    print_calling_file()

    DEV_DATA_DIR: str = "C:/Users/mharvey/Documents/code/riskquant/data/dev"

    rsc = compile_risk_static_config_from_xlsx(
        path=f"{DEV_DATA_DIR}/dev_risk_static_data_config_v01.xlsx"
    )

    topology = compile_proxy_topology_from_xlsx(
        path=f"{DEV_DATA_DIR}/dev_risk_base_proxy_config_v01.xlsx",
        static_config=rsc,
    )

    topology.summary()
    print_success_message()
```

```python
# src/riskquant/config/proxy_config/loader.py
"""
Loads proxy configuration from an Excel workbook into a clean DataFrame.

The workbook MUST carry an `active` column. Rows with active=True are
returned; the `active` column is dropped from the result. If `active`
is absent the loader raises — silently treating an absent column as
"all rows active" is a real production foot-gun (drafts and historical
entries get compiled).

This loader only handles the human-facing xlsx convention. The in-memory
canonical fixture is post-filtered (no active column) and goes through
compile_proxy_topology_from_df directly.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

# "active" column is not a hard requirement.
_REQUIRED_COLS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {
        "proxying_level",
        "required_name",
        "proxy_name",
        "vol_scaling_factor",
    }
)

# Columns that survive the active-filter and are returned to compile.py.
_POST_FILTER_COLS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "proxying_level",
    "required_name",
    "proxy_name",
    "vol_scaling_factor",
)


def load_proxy_config_xlsx_into_df(
    *,
    path: str | Path,
    sheet_name: str | None = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Load the proxy-config sheet and return active rows only.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path
        Path to the Excel workbook.
    sheet_name
        Sheet to read. Defaults to the first sheet if None.

    Returns
    -------
    pd.DataFrame
        Columns: proxying_level, required_name, proxy_name,
        vol_scaling_factor. `active` is consumed and dropped.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If a required column is missing, or if the sheet name does not
        exist in the workbook.
    """
    path_str = str(path)

    with pd.ExcelFile(path_str, engine="openpyxl") as xls:
        available = list(xls.sheet_names)
        if sheet_name is not None and sheet_name not in available:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Missing required sheet {sheet_name!r} in {path_str!r}. "
                f"Available sheets: {available}"
            )
        _sheet: str = str(sheet_name) if sheet_name is not None else str(available[0])
        df: pd.DataFrame = pd.read_excel(xls, sheet_name=_sheet)

    # --- required columns: explicit (no silent active fallback) ---
    missing = _REQUIRED_COLS - set(df.columns)
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Missing required column(s) in sheet {_sheet!r} of {path_str!r}: "
            f"{sorted(missing)}.\n"
            f"Required: {sorted(_REQUIRED_COLS)}"
        )

    # --- active filter ---
    if "active" in df.columns:
        mask = df["active"].fillna(False).astype(bool)
        df = df.loc[mask, list(_POST_FILTER_COLS)].reset_index(drop=True)
    else:
        df = df[list(_POST_FILTER_COLS)].reset_index(drop=True)

    # --- type coercions ---
    # String columns: strip whitespace, leave nulls as nulls (validator
    # rejects both null and blank; loader does NOT fillna to "").
    for col in ("proxying_level", "required_name", "proxy_name"):
        df[col] = df[col].astype("string").str.strip()

    # vol_scaling_factor: float64 with missing -> 1.0 (identity).
    df["vol_scaling_factor"] = df["vol_scaling_factor"].fillna(1.0).astype(np.float64)

    return df


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import pathlib  # Add this at the top of your file

    from riskquant.utils.printer import (
        print_calling_file,
        print_success_message,
    )

    print_calling_file()

    # 1. Get the absolute path of the directory containing THIS script
    current_dir = pathlib.Path(__file__).parent.resolve()

    # Project directory is 4 levels up from the current script
    project_dir = current_dir.parent.parent.parent.parent
    print(f"Project directory: {project_dir}")

    # 2. Build the path dynamically relative to the current script.
    # NOTE: You may need to add .parent if this script is nested deeper.
    # For example: current_dir.parent / "data" / "dev"
    DEV_DATA_DIR = project_dir / "data" / "dev"
    DEV_DATA_FILE_NAME = "dev_risk_base_proxy_config_v01.xlsx"

    # Combine them using the / operator (a handy feature of pathlib)
    full_path = DEV_DATA_DIR / DEV_DATA_FILE_NAME
    print(f"\nFull path to Excel file: {full_path}")

    # Convert the Path object back to a string just in case
    # load_proxy_config_xlsx_into_df strictly requires a string
    df = load_proxy_config_xlsx_into_df(path=str(full_path))

    print(f"\nLoaded proxy config DataFrame (shape: {df.shape}):")
    print(df.to_string(index=False))

    print_success_message()
```
