<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\config\proxy_config\proxy_topology.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/config/proxy_config/proxy_topology.py
"""
Runtime proxy-topology container.

Pandas-free. Hot-path lookups are dense int32 / float64 NumPy arrays
indexed by required-id; sentinel -1 means "no proxy registered for this
id, pass through unchanged."

Built once at compile time. Immutable thereafter; the `with_overrides`
factory returns a brand-new instance.

Resolution semantics (single-hop, enforced by the validator)
------------------------------------------------------------
For any required_id `r`:
    slot        = searchsorted(container_ids, r)         (container level)
    proxy_id    = container_proxy_id[slot]               (or rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id[r])
    proxy_scale = container_proxy_vol_scale[slot]        (or rf_id_to_proxy_vol_scale[r])

If proxy_id < 0, no proxy is registered — pass through.
If proxy_id >= 0, the proxy must NOT itself be proxied (the validator
forbids chains). The kernel performs a single gather, no iteration.

The validator also forbids container-level proxies whose children are
rf-level proxied (cross-level conflict) — they are mutually exclusive
policies and coexisting them silently mis-applies the user's intent.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.config.proxy_config.schema_objects import ProxyMap

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig


# Sentinel for "no proxy registered for this id" in the dense arrays.
# Scoped to ProxyTopology — not in riskquant.constants because no
# consumer outside this module compares against it directly (the kernel
# uses `proxy_id >= 0` branchless masking instead).
_NO_PROXY_ID: int = -1


# Default vol scale for ids with no proxy. 1.0 is the identity
# multiplier; using NaN here would propagate into downstream math.
_DEFAULT_VOL_SCALE: float = 1.0


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ProxyTopology:
    """
    Compiled proxy topology.

    Init fields are supplied by `ProxyTopology.build` (or by
    `with_overrides`, which re-routes through `build`); they are not
    intended to be constructed directly by user code.

    Hot-path readers consume only the dense arrays:
        container_proxy_id        (slot-indexed, parallel to container_ids)
        container_proxy_vol_scale (slot-indexed, parallel to container_ids)
        rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id      (raw-rf-id-indexed)
        rf_id_to_proxy_vol_scale  (raw-rf-id-indexed)

    The two dicts (container_proxies, rf_proxies) are retained for
    audit and override purposes and are never touched on the hot path.
    """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Human-readable layer (audit + override path)                       #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #

    container_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap] = field(repr=False)
    rf_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap] = field(repr=False)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Hot-path dense arrays                                              #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # `container_ids` is the sorted-unique container axis (mirrors
    # static_config.container_ids). The two container_proxy_* arrays are
    # parallel to it — index by SLOT (searchsorted on container_ids), not
    # raw container_id. Container ids live at 250M+, so a dense-by-raw-id
    # layout would allocate multi-GB arrays.
    container_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(repr=False)
    container_proxy_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(repr=False)
    container_proxy_vol_scale: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(repr=False)

    # Length = static_config.max_rf_id + 1. Indexed by required_rf_id.
    # rf_ids are kept small (<= a few hundred K), so the dense-by-id
    # layout is retained on the rf side.
    rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id: npt.NDArray[np.int32] = field(repr=False)
    rf_id_to_proxy_vol_scale: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(repr=False)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Factories                                                          #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #

    @classmethod
    def build(
        cls,
        *,
        container_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap],
        rf_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap],
        static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
    ) -> ProxyTopology:
        """
        Build a ProxyTopology by resolving names against the static config
        and emitting the dense lookup arrays.

        Parameters
        ----------
        container_proxies, rf_proxies
            Already validated by `validate_proxy_maps`. Required name and
            proxy name are guaranteed to resolve in static_config; no
            chains, no cycles, no cross-level conflicts.
        static_config
            Source of truth for name -> id resolution and id-space sizes.
        """
        container_ids = np.asarray(static_config.container_ids, dtype=np.int32)
        n_container = int(container_ids.shape[0])
        n_rf = int(static_config.max_rf_id) + 1

        c_proxy = np.full(n_container, _NO_PROXY_ID, dtype=np.int32)
        c_scale = np.full(n_container, _DEFAULT_VOL_SCALE, dtype=np.float64)
        r_proxy = np.full(n_rf, _NO_PROXY_ID, dtype=np.int32)
        r_scale = np.full(n_rf, _DEFAULT_VOL_SCALE, dtype=np.float64)

        # Container arrays are SLOT-indexed: the required container's slot
        # is its position in container_ids. The stored VALUE is the proxy's
        # raw container id (consumers gather it as a downstream id).
        name_to_cid = static_config.container_name_to_id
        for pm in container_proxies.values():
            req_slot = static_config.container_slot(int(name_to_cid[pm.required_name]))
            proxy_id = int(name_to_cid[pm.proxy_name])
            c_proxy[req_slot] = np.int32(proxy_id)
            c_scale[req_slot] = float(pm.vol_scaling_factor)

        name_to_rfid = static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id
        for pm in rf_proxies.values():
            req_id = int(name_to_rfid[pm.required_name])
            proxy_id = int(name_to_rfid[pm.proxy_name])
            r_proxy[req_id] = np.int32(proxy_id)
            r_scale[req_id] = float(pm.vol_scaling_factor)

        return cls(
            container_proxies=container_proxies,
            rf_proxies=rf_proxies,
            container_ids=container_ids,
            container_proxy_id=c_proxy,
            container_proxy_vol_scale=c_scale,
            rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id=r_proxy,
            rf_id_to_proxy_vol_scale=r_scale,
        )

    def with_overrides(
        self,
        *,
        static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
        container_overrides: dict[str, ProxyMap] | None = None,
        rf_overrides: dict[str, ProxyMap] | None = None,
    ) -> ProxyTopology:
        """
        Return a new topology with overrides layered on top of self.

        Overrides are full-replace, keyed by required_name: any
        required_name appearing in the overrides supersedes the base
        topology's mapping for that name (or adds a new one).

        Re-runs the full proxy-map validation against the supplied
        static_config so override-induced chains, cycles, or cross-level
        conflicts surface immediately.
        """
        # Local import to avoid a cycle: validate imports schema_objects,
        # which proxy_topology already imports; validate also imports the
        # module that ends up being this one transitively via compile.
        from riskquant.config.proxy_config.validate import validate_proxy_maps

        merged_containers = dict(self.container_proxies)
        if container_overrides:
            merged_containers.update(container_overrides)

        merged_rfs = dict(self.rf_proxies)
        if rf_overrides:
            merged_rfs.update(rf_overrides)

        validate_proxy_maps(
            container_proxies=merged_containers,
            rf_proxies=merged_rfs,
            static_config=static_config,
        )

        return ProxyTopology.build(
            container_proxies=merged_containers,
            rf_proxies=merged_rfs,
            static_config=static_config,
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Single-id helpers (debug / unit-test only — not for hot path)      #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #

    def resolve_container(self, required_container_id: int) -> tuple[int, float]:
        """
        Return (proxy_container_id, vol_scale) for a single required id.

        If no proxy is registered (or the id is not a known container),
        returns (required_container_id, 1.0) — the identity mapping the
        kernel applies via branchless masking. Hot paths must use the
        dense arrays directly (after resolving id->slot in batch); this is
        a convenience for tests and notebook sessions.
        """
        cid = int(required_container_id)
        slot = int(np.searchsorted(self.container_ids, cid))
        if slot >= self.container_ids.size or int(self.container_ids[slot]) != cid:
            return (cid, _DEFAULT_VOL_SCALE)
        proxy_id = int(self.container_proxy_id[slot])
        if proxy_id < 0:
            return (cid, _DEFAULT_VOL_SCALE)
        return (proxy_id, float(self.container_proxy_vol_scale[slot]))

    def resolve_rf(self, required_rf_id: int) -> tuple[int, float]:
        """Same as resolve_container, but for the rf-id space."""
        i = int(required_rf_id)
        proxy_id = int(self.rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id[i])
        if proxy_id < 0:
            return (i, _DEFAULT_VOL_SCALE)
        return (proxy_id, float(self.rf_id_to_proxy_vol_scale[i]))

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #
    # Scalar accessors                                                   #
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ #

    @property
    def num_container_proxies(self) -> int:
        return len(self.container_proxies)

    @property
    def num_rf_proxies(self) -> int:
        return len(self.rf_proxies)

    @property
    def num_total_proxies(self) -> int:
        return self.num_container_proxies + self.num_rf_proxies

    def summary(self) -> None:
        """Print a console-friendly overview of the compiled topology."""
        lines = [
            "================================",
            "===   ProxyTopology Summary  ===",
            "================================",
            f"  Container proxies   : {self.num_container_proxies}",
            f"  RF proxies          : {self.num_rf_proxies}",
            f"  Total               : {self.num_total_proxies}",
            f"  c_proxy array size  : {self.container_proxy_id.size}",
            f"  rf_proxy array size : {self.rf_id_to_proxy_rf_id.size}",
            "================================",
        ]
        print("\n".join(lines))
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/proxy_config/schema_objects.py
"""
Immutable value object for one proxy mapping.

The proxying-level discriminator (container vs risk-factor) is carried by
the partition the ProxyMap lives in (ProxyTopology.container_proxies or
.rf_proxies), not by a field on the dataclass. Strings die at the edge;
the runtime never reads a level string.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class ProxyMap:
    """
    A single proxy mapping entry.

    The required name's historical data is unavailable or insufficient
    for risk estimation; the proxy name's data is used in its place,
    optionally scaled by `vol_scaling_factor` to account for known
    relative-vol differences between the two series.

    Attributes
    ----------
    required_name
        The name whose data is being substituted (the "missing" one).
        A container name when used in container_proxies, a relative
        risk-factor name when used in rf_proxies.
    proxy_name
        The name supplying the substitute data. Must be a peer of
        required_name (same level — container or rf — same namespace).
    vol_scaling_factor
        Strictly positive multiplier applied to the proxy's vol/shocks
        before substitution. Loader defaults missing cells to 1.0
        (identity). The validator rejects values <= 0 or > 10.0.
    """

    required_name: str
    proxy_name: str
    vol_scaling_factor: float
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/proxy_config/validate.py
"""
Two-stage validation for proxy configuration.

Stage 1: `validate_proxy_config_df`
    Runs against the active-filtered DataFrame. Vectorized — no row
    iteration. Catches schema, type, null, blank, and domain-range
    issues before any name resolution is attempted.

Stage 2: `validate_proxy_maps`
    Runs against the typed ProxyMap dicts (after compile partitions them
    by level) and the compiled RiskStaticConfig. Catches:
      - foreign-key drift against the static config,
      - self-references,
      - chained proxies (A->B->C is forbidden — multi-hop is not allowed),
      - cross-level conflicts (a container is proxied AND one of its
        child risk factors is also proxied).

The override path on ProxyTopology calls Stage 2 directly without
roundtripping through a DataFrame.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.config.enums.container_topology import ContainerTopologyType
from riskquant.config.proxy_config.schema_objects import ProxyMap

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig


# ============================================================
# Constants
# ============================================================


VALID_PROXYING_LEVELS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"CONTAINER", "RISK_FACTOR"})

# vol_scaling_factor sanity bounds. The lower bound is strict (a zero
# scale silently zeros the proxy's vol, which is a class-A modelling bug);
# the upper bound is conservative but catches typos like missing decimal
# point ("15" intended as "1.5").
_VOL_SCALE_MIN_EXCLUSIVE: float = 0.0
_VOL_SCALE_MAX_INCLUSIVE: float = 10.0


REQUIRED_COLUMNS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {"proxying_level", "required_name", "proxy_name", "vol_scaling_factor"}
)


# ============================================================
# Generic helpers
# ============================================================


def _check_no_blank_strings(df: pd.DataFrame, col: str) -> None:
    if col not in df.columns:
        return
    blanks = df[col].astype(str).str.strip() == ""
    if blanks.any():
        idx = df.index[blanks].tolist()[:10]
        raise ValueError(
            f"proxy_config.{col}: blank string at rows {idx}. "
            "Loader fills null cells with the empty string; the validator "
            "treats both as invalid."
        )


# ============================================================
# Stage 1 — DataFrame-tier validation
# ============================================================


def validate_proxy_config_df(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """
    Validate an active-filtered proxy DataFrame.

    Tiers:
      1. Required columns present.
      2. No null values in required columns.
      3. No blank strings in string columns.
      4. proxying_level is in VALID_PROXYING_LEVELS.
      5. vol_scaling_factor in (0, 10].
      6. (proxying_level, required_name) is unique — a required name
         can only be proxied once per level.
      7. required_name != proxy_name (self-reference).

    Cross-table checks (FK against static_config, chains, cycles,
    cross-level conflicts) are deferred to `validate_proxy_maps`,
    which runs after the DataFrame has been partitioned by level.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        On the first violation encountered, with a descriptive message.
    """
    if df.empty:
        return  # An empty proxy map is valid: no proxies applied.

    # --- 1. required columns ---
    missing = REQUIRED_COLUMNS - set(df.columns)
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"proxy_config missing required column(s): {sorted(missing)}.\n"
            f"Found: {list(df.columns)}"
        )

    # --- 2. no nulls in required columns ---
    null_mask = df[list(REQUIRED_COLUMNS)].isnull().any()
    if null_mask.any():
        null_cols = null_mask[null_mask].index.tolist()
        raise ValueError(
            f"proxy_config has null values in required column(s): {null_cols}."
        )

    # --- 3. no blank strings (loader fills nulls with "" which would slip past .isnull) ---
    for col in ("proxying_level", "required_name", "proxy_name"):
        _check_no_blank_strings(df, col)

    # --- 4. proxying_level domain ---
    # Coerce to uppercase before set membership (loader leaves case as-is).
    level_norm = df["proxying_level"].astype(str).str.upper()
    invalid = ~level_norm.isin(VALID_PROXYING_LEVELS)
    if invalid.any():
        bad = sorted(level_norm[invalid].unique().tolist())
        raise ValueError(
            f"proxy_config has invalid proxying_level value(s): {bad}.\n"
            f"Must be one of: {sorted(VALID_PROXYING_LEVELS)} (case-insensitive)."
        )

    # --- 5. vol_scaling_factor range ---
    scale = df["vol_scaling_factor"].astype(np.float64)
    bad_scale = (
        ~np.isfinite(scale.to_numpy())
        | (scale <= _VOL_SCALE_MIN_EXCLUSIVE)
        | (scale > _VOL_SCALE_MAX_INCLUSIVE)
    )
    if bad_scale.any():
        offending = df.loc[
            bad_scale, ["required_name", "proxy_name", "vol_scaling_factor"]
        ]
        raise ValueError(
            f"proxy_config has vol_scaling_factor outside "
            f"({_VOL_SCALE_MIN_EXCLUSIVE}, {_VOL_SCALE_MAX_INCLUSIVE}]:\n"
            f"{offending.to_string(index=False)}"
        )

    # --- 6. (proxying_level, required_name) composite uniqueness ---
    work = df.assign(
        _level=level_norm,
        _required=df["required_name"].astype(str).str.strip(),
    )
    dupes_mask = work.duplicated(subset=["_level", "_required"], keep=False)
    if dupes_mask.any():
        offending = df.loc[
            dupes_mask, ["proxying_level", "required_name", "proxy_name"]
        ]
        raise ValueError(
            "proxy_config has duplicate (proxying_level, required_name) rows. "
            "A required_name can be proxied at most once per level.\n"
            f"Conflicting rows:\n{offending.to_string(index=False)}"
        )

    # --- 7. self-reference ---
    self_ref = (
        df["required_name"].astype(str).str.strip()
        == df["proxy_name"].astype(str).str.strip()
    )
    if self_ref.any():
        offending = df.loc[self_ref, ["proxying_level", "required_name"]]
        raise ValueError(
            "proxy_config has self-referencing entries "
            "(required_name == proxy_name):\n"
            f"{offending.to_string(index=False)}"
        )


# ============================================================
# Stage 2 — ProxyMap-tier validation (FK + chain + cross-level)
# ============================================================


def _check_chain(proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap], level_label: str) -> None:
    """
    Forbid multi-hop proxies within a single level.

    A chain is any (A -> B) where B is itself a required_name in another
    (B -> C) entry. Multi-hop is banned: every proxy must point at a
    terminal node. Cycles are a special case of chains where some B == A,
    so this single check covers cycles too.
    """
    required_set: set[str] = set(proxies.keys())
    proxy_targets: list[tuple[str, str]] = [
        (pm.required_name, pm.proxy_name) for pm in proxies.values()
    ]

    chained: list[tuple[str, str]] = [
        (req, prx) for req, prx in proxy_targets if prx in required_set
    ]
    if chained:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Chained {level_label} proxies are not allowed (multi-hop is "
            f"banned — every proxy must point at a terminal name).\n"
            f"The proxy targets below are themselves required_names in other "
            f"rows:\n"
            + "\n".join(
                f"  {req!r} -> {prx!r}  (but {prx!r} is also proxied)"
                for req, prx in chained
            )
            + f"\nResolve by pointing both {level_label} mappings at a "
            "common terminal name."
        )


def _check_fk(
    proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap],
    name_to_id: dict[str, int | np.int32] | Mapping[str, int | np.int32],
    namespace_label: str,
) -> None:
    """
    Every required_name and proxy_name must resolve in name_to_id.

    Bulk set-difference: one error message lists every unknown name,
    not one PrettyKeyError per row.
    """
    referenced: set[str] = set()
    for pm in proxies.values():
        referenced.add(pm.required_name)
        referenced.add(pm.proxy_name)

    unknown = referenced - set(name_to_id)
    if unknown:
        raise ValueError(
            f"proxy_config references {len(unknown)} unknown {namespace_label} "
            f"name(s): {sorted(unknown)[:25]}.\n"
            f"Every required_name and proxy_name must exist in the static "
            f"config's {namespace_label} namespace."
        )


def _check_cross_level_conflict(
    container_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap],
    rf_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap],
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
) -> None:
    """
    Forbid a container being proxied while any of its child risk factors
    is ALSO proxied.

    Rationale: container-level "use the whole proxy container's curve"
    and rf-level "use this specific other point" are competing policies;
    coexisting them silently mis-applies the user's intent.
    """
    if not container_proxies or not rf_proxies:
        return

    # Resolve each proxied container to its container_id.
    name_to_cid = static_config.container_name_to_id
    topology_type_arr = (
        static_config.container_topology_type
    )  # slot-indexed: 0=node, 1=curve, 2=surface

    proxied_curve_ids: set[int] = set()
    proxied_surface_ids: set[int] = set()
    for pm in container_proxies.values():
        cid = int(name_to_cid[pm.required_name])
        topology_type = int(topology_type_arr[static_config.container_slot(cid)])
        if topology_type == ContainerTopologyType.CURVE.value:
            proxied_curve_ids.add(cid)
        elif topology_type == ContainerTopologyType.SURFACE.value:
            proxied_surface_ids.add(cid)

    if not (proxied_curve_ids or proxied_surface_ids):
        # FK passed but no proxied container resolved to curve or surface.
        # This should be unreachable; if we get here, container_topology_type
        # has a bug or container_proxies contains a node name (forbidden).
        raise ValueError(
            "internal: cross-level conflict check found proxied containers "
            "with topology_type not in {curve, surface}."
        )

    # Walk every rf-level proxy. The required_name is a relative
    # risk-factor name; its parent_id is on the dense rf_id_to_parent_id
    # array. If parent_id is in any proxied container set, conflict.
    name_to_rfid = static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id
    parent_topology_type_arr = static_config.rf_id_to_parent_topology_type
    parent_id_arr = static_config.rf_id_to_parent_id

    conflicts: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    for pm in rf_proxies.values():
        rfid = int(name_to_rfid[pm.required_name])
        topology_type = int(parent_topology_type_arr[rfid])
        if topology_type <= ContainerTopologyType.NODE.value:
            continue  # Topology type is NODE (0) or unoccupied (-1) - skip.
        pid = int(parent_id_arr[rfid])
        if (
            topology_type == ContainerTopologyType.CURVE.value
            and pid in proxied_curve_ids
        ):
            conflicts.append((pm.required_name, _curve_name(pid, static_config)))
        elif (
            topology_type == ContainerTopologyType.SURFACE.value
            and pid in proxied_surface_ids
        ):
            conflicts.append((pm.required_name, _surface_name(pid, static_config)))

    if conflicts:
        lines = [
            f"  {rf_name!r} (parent={parent_name!r})"
            for rf_name, parent_name in conflicts
        ]
        raise ValueError(
            "proxy_config has cross-level conflicts: the rf-level proxies "
            "below belong to containers that are ALSO container-level "
            "proxied.\n"
            "Container-level proxy and rf-level proxy are competing "
            "policies; choose ONE per affected container.\n" + "\n".join(lines)
        )


def _curve_name(curve_id: int, sc: RiskStaticConfig) -> str:
    cm = sc.curve_meta_dict.get(curve_id)
    return cm.curve_name if cm is not None else f"<curve_id={curve_id}>"


def _surface_name(surface_id: int, sc: RiskStaticConfig) -> str:
    sm = sc.surface_meta_dict.get(surface_id)
    return sm.surface_name if sm is not None else f"<surface_id={surface_id}>"


def validate_proxy_maps(
    *,
    container_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap],
    rf_proxies: dict[str, ProxyMap],
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
) -> None:
    """
    Validate partitioned proxy maps against the static config.

    Runs after compile.py has split the DataFrame by level (or after
    overrides have been merged in `ProxyTopology.with_overrides`).

    Checks
    ------
      A. Foreign-key: every name in container_proxies resolves in
         `static_config.container_name_to_id`; every name in rf_proxies
         resolves in `static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id`.
      B. Chain ban: no proxy target is itself a required name (per level).
         Implicitly catches cycles, since a cycle requires the target to
         be a required name.
      C. Cross-level: no container is both container-proxied and has any
         of its child risk factors rf-proxied.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        On the first violation, with a descriptive message naming every
        offending pair / name found.
    """
    _check_fk(container_proxies, static_config.container_name_to_id, "container")
    _check_fk(rf_proxies, static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id, "risk-factor")
    _check_chain(container_proxies, "container")
    _check_chain(rf_proxies, "risk-factor")
    _check_cross_level_conflict(container_proxies, rf_proxies, static_config)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/static_config/audit.py
"""
Tabular audit of a compiled RiskStaticConfig.

Imports Pandas deliberately — auditing is an edge-only diagnostic, not a
runtime path. Never imported by valuation, simlab, or any kernel.

Layout: one DataFrame per logical table the new 8-table schema exposes,
plus reverse-mapped derived structures (FX spot lookup, discount-curve
lookup, dense per-rf attribute arrays). Each section prints `head(n)`
with a configurable row cap.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import asdict

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.config.enums.container_topology import ContainerTopologyType
from riskquant.config.enums.discount_basis import DiscountBasis
from riskquant.config.enums.x_axis_kinds import XAxisKind
from riskquant.config.static_config.compile import (
    compile_risk_static_config_from_xlsx,
)
from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig


def _print_title(title: str) -> None:
    len_title = len(title) + 4
    ruler = "#" * len_title
    print(f"\n{ruler}")
    print(f"# {title} #")
    print(f"{ruler}")


def _print_subheader(title: str) -> None:
    ruler = "-" * (len(title) + 0)
    print("\n")
    print(f"{title}")
    print(f"{ruler}")


def _df_from_dataclass_values(values) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Build a DataFrame from an iterable of frozen dataclasses."""
    rows = [asdict(v) for v in values]
    return pd.DataFrame(rows) if rows else pd.DataFrame()


# ============================================================
# Per-table renderers
# ============================================================


def _audit_ccy(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("1. Currencies (ccy)")
    df = _df_from_dataclass_values(rsc.ccy_dict.values())
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Total: {rsc.num_ccys}")


def _audit_holiday_calendars(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("2. Holiday calendars (counts per calendar)")
    rows = [
        {
            "holiday_calendar_name": name,
            "n_holidays": int(cal.holiday_dates_ord.size),
            "first_ord": int(cal.holiday_dates_ord[0])
            if cal.holiday_dates_ord.size
            else None,
            "last_ord": int(cal.holiday_dates_ord[-1])
            if cal.holiday_dates_ord.size
            else None,
        }
        for name, cal in rsc.holiday_calendars_dict.items()
    ]
    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Total calendars: {len(rsc.holiday_calendars_dict)}")


def _audit_spot_roll_rules(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("3. Spot roll rules")
    df = _df_from_dataclass_values(rsc.spot_roll_rule_dict.values())
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Total: {len(rsc.spot_roll_rule_dict)}")


def _audit_node_meta(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader(
        "4. Node meta (scalar risk factors; FX + equity + credit single names)"
    )
    df = _df_from_dataclass_values(rsc.node_meta_dict.values())
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(
        f"  Total nodes: {rsc.num_nodes}  (FX: {sum(1 for n_ in rsc.node_meta_dict.values() if n_.fx_base_ccy is not None)})"
    )


def _audit_curve_meta(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("5. Curve meta (1D functions; discount + non-discount)")
    df = _df_from_dataclass_values(rsc.curve_meta_dict.values())
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    is_disc = df["discount_basis"] != DiscountBasis.NOT_A_DISCOUNT_CURVE.name
    print(
        f"  Total curves: {rsc.num_curves}  "
        f"(discount: {int(is_disc.sum())}, "
        f"default-flagged: {int(df['is_default_discount_for_ccy'].sum())})"
    )


def _audit_curve_point_meta(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("6. Curve point counts (per parent curve)")
    counts: dict[int, int] = {}
    for pt in rsc.curve_point_meta_dict.values():
        counts[pt.curve_id] = counts.get(pt.curve_id, 0) + 1
    rows = []
    for cid, cm in rsc.curve_meta_dict.items():
        rows.append(
            {
                "curve_id": cid,
                "curve_name": cm.curve_name,
                "x_axis_kind": cm.x_axis_kind,
                "n_points": counts.get(cid, 0),
            }
        )
    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Total curve points: {rsc.num_curve_points}")


def _audit_surface_meta(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("7. Surface meta (2D functions; vol surfaces)")
    df = _df_from_dataclass_values(rsc.surface_meta_dict.values())
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Total surfaces: {rsc.num_surfaces}")


def _audit_surface_point_meta(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("8. Surface point counts (per parent surface)")
    counts: dict[int, int] = {}
    for pt in rsc.surface_point_meta_dict.values():
        counts[pt.surface_id] = counts.get(pt.surface_id, 0) + 1
    rows = []
    for sid, sm in rsc.surface_meta_dict.items():
        rows.append(
            {
                "surface_id": sid,
                "surface_name": sm.surface_name,
                "x_axis_kind": sm.x_axis_kind,
                "y_axis_kind": sm.y_axis_kind,
                "n_points": counts.get(sid, 0),
            }
        )
    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Total surface points: {rsc.num_surface_points}")


# ============================================================
# Derived structures
# ============================================================


def _audit_fx_spot_lookup(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("9. FX spot lookup ((base_ccy_id, quote_ccy_id) -> rf_id)")
    if not rsc.fx_spot_lookup:
        print("(no FX nodes)")
        return
    rows = []
    for (base_id, quote_id), rf_id in rsc.fx_spot_lookup.items():
        base_name = (
            rsc.ccy_id_to_name[int(base_id)]
            if int(base_id) < len(rsc.ccy_id_to_name)
            else "?"
        )
        quote_name = (
            rsc.ccy_id_to_name[int(quote_id)]
            if int(quote_id) < len(rsc.ccy_id_to_name)
            else "?"
        )
        node = rsc.node_meta_dict.get(int(rf_id))
        rows.append(
            {
                "base_ccy_id": int(base_id),
                "base_ccy": base_name,
                "quote_ccy_id": int(quote_id),
                "quote_ccy": quote_name,
                "rf_id": int(rf_id),
                "node_name": node.node_name if node else "?",
            }
        )
    df = (
        pd.DataFrame(rows).sort_values(["base_ccy", "quote_ccy"]).reset_index(drop=True)
    )
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Total FX pairs: {rsc.num_fx_pairs}")


def _audit_discount_curves(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("10. Discount-curve lookups")

    # 10a — per-(ccy, basis) full table
    print("\n  (a) (ccy_id, basis_id) -> curve_id")
    rows = []
    for (ccy_id, basis_id), curve_id in rsc.discount_curve_lookup.items():
        cm = rsc.curve_meta_dict[int(curve_id)]
        ccy_name = rsc.ccy_id_to_name[int(ccy_id)]
        basis_name = DiscountBasis(int(basis_id)).name
        rows.append(
            {
                "ccy_id": int(ccy_id),
                "ccy": ccy_name,
                "basis_id": int(basis_id),
                "basis": basis_name,
                "curve_id": int(curve_id),
                "curve_name": cm.curve_name,
                "is_default": cm.is_default_discount_for_ccy,
            }
        )
    if rows:
        df = pd.DataFrame(rows).sort_values(["ccy", "basis"]).reset_index(drop=True)
        print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    else:
        print("  (no discount curves)")

    # 10b — per-ccy default
    print("\n  (b) ccy_id -> default discount curve_id")
    rows = []
    for ccy_id_int, curve_id in enumerate(rsc.ccy_id_to_default_discount_curve_id):
        cid = int(curve_id)
        if cid < 0:
            continue
        ccy_name = rsc.ccy_id_to_name[ccy_id_int]
        cm = rsc.curve_meta_dict[cid]
        rows.append(
            {
                "ccy_id": ccy_id_int,
                "ccy": ccy_name,
                "curve_id": cid,
                "curve_name": cm.curve_name,
                "basis": cm.discount_basis,
            }
        )
    if rows:
        df = pd.DataFrame(rows).sort_values("ccy").reset_index(drop=True)
        print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    else:
        print("  (no default-flagged discount curves)")


def _audit_dense_rf_arrays(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("11. Dense per-rf attribute arrays (head)")
    n_total = rsc.max_rf_id + 1
    # Mask out empty slots (rf_id not occupied).
    occupied = rsc.rf_id_to_parent_topology_type >= ContainerTopologyType.NODE.value
    if not occupied.any():
        print("(no risk factors)")
        return

    df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "rf_id": np.arange(n_total, dtype=np.int32),
            "parent_topology_type": rsc.rf_id_to_parent_topology_type,
            "parent_id": rsc.rf_id_to_parent_id,
            "ccy_id": rsc.rf_id_to_ccy_id,
            "shift_type_id": rsc.rf_id_to_shift_type_id,
            "unit_scale_x": rsc.rf_id_to_unit_scale_factor,
        }
    )
    df = df.loc[occupied].reset_index(drop=True)
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Occupied rf_id slots: {int(occupied.sum())} / {n_total} array length")


def _audit_dense_container_arrays(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("12. Per-container attribute arrays (slot-indexed; head)")
    n_total = rsc.num_containers
    occupied = rsc.container_topology_type > ContainerTopologyType.NODE.value
    if not occupied.any():
        print("(no containers)")
        return

    x_kind_names = [
        XAxisKind(int(k)).name if int(k) >= 0 else "-"
        for k in rsc.container_x_axis_kind
    ]
    df = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "container_id": rsc.container_ids,
            "topology_type": rsc.container_topology_type,  # 1=curve, 2=surface
            "ccy_id": rsc.container_ccy_id,
            "interp_x_id": rsc.container_interp_x_id,
            "extrap_x_id": rsc.container_extrap_x_id,
            "interp_y_id": rsc.container_interp_y_id,
            "extrap_y_id": rsc.container_extrap_y_id,
            "x_axis_kind": x_kind_names,
            "spot_roll_rule": rsc.container_spot_roll_rule_name,
        }
    )
    df = df.loc[occupied].reset_index(drop=True)
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))
    print(f"  Containers: {int(occupied.sum())} / {n_total} slots")


def _audit_tenor_index(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int) -> None:
    _print_subheader("13. Per-curve tenor index summary")
    rows = []
    for curve_id, idx in rsc.curve_id_to_tenor_index.items():
        cm = rsc.curve_meta_dict[curve_id]
        rows.append(
            {
                "curve_id": curve_id,
                "curve_name": cm.curve_name,
                "x_axis_kind": cm.x_axis_kind,
                "n_offsets": int(idx.sorted_offsets.size),
                "n_labels": len(idx.label_to_rf_id),
                "min_offset": int(idx.sorted_offsets[0])
                if idx.sorted_offsets.size
                else None,
                "max_offset": int(idx.sorted_offsets[-1])
                if idx.sorted_offsets.size
                else None,
            }
        )
    df = pd.DataFrame(rows)
    if df.empty:
        print("(empty)")
        return
    print(df.head(n).to_string(index=False))


# ============================================================
# Public API
# ============================================================


def audit_risk_static_config(rsc: RiskStaticConfig, n: int = 10) -> None:
    """
    Print a tabular audit of every table and derived structure on `rsc`.

    Parameters
    ----------
    rsc
        Compiled static config to audit.
    n
        Row cap per section. Use a large number for full dumps.
    """
    _print_title("RiskStaticConfig Audit")

    print("Summary:")
    print(f"  Currencies          : {rsc.num_ccys}")
    print(f"  Holiday calendars   : {len(rsc.holiday_calendars_dict)}")
    print(f"  Spot roll rules     : {len(rsc.spot_roll_rule_dict)}")
    print(f"  Nodes               : {rsc.num_nodes}")
    print(f"  Curves              : {rsc.num_curves}")
    print(f"  Curve points        : {rsc.num_curve_points}")
    print(f"  Surfaces            : {rsc.num_surfaces}")
    print(f"  Surface points      : {rsc.num_surface_points}")
    print(f"  Risk factors total  : {rsc.num_risk_factors}")
    print(f"  Containers total    : {rsc.num_containers}")
    print(f"  FX pairs            : {rsc.num_fx_pairs}")
    print(f"  Max rf_id           : {rsc.max_rf_id}")
    print(f"  Max container_id    : {rsc.max_container_id}")

    _audit_ccy(rsc, n)
    _audit_holiday_calendars(rsc, n)
    _audit_spot_roll_rules(rsc, n)
    _audit_node_meta(rsc, n)
    _audit_curve_meta(rsc, n)
    _audit_curve_point_meta(rsc, n)
    _audit_surface_meta(rsc, n)
    _audit_surface_point_meta(rsc, n)
    _audit_fx_spot_lookup(rsc, n)
    _audit_discount_curves(rsc, n)
    _audit_dense_rf_arrays(rsc, n)
    _audit_dense_container_arrays(rsc, n)
    _audit_tenor_index(rsc, n)

    _print_title("End of RiskStaticConfig Audit")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    from riskquant.utils.printer import print_calling_file, print_success_message

    print_calling_file()

    DEV_DATA_DIR: str = "C:/Users/mharvey/Documents/code/riskquant/data/canonical_examples"

        path=f"{DEV_DATA_DIR}/{DEV_DATA_FILE_NAME_STATIC_CONFIG}"
    )

    rsc.summary()
    audit_risk_static_config(rsc=rsc, n=200)

    print_success_message()
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\config\static_config\compile.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/config/static_config/compile.py
"""
Single entry-point for building a RiskStaticConfig from source data.

Pipeline:
    dict_of_dfs  ->  validate  ->  compile (typed scalars)  ->  RiskStaticConfig

The 7 (+ ccy + spot_roll_rule + holiday_calendars = 10) required sheets are:
    ccy, holiday_calendars, spot_roll_rule,
    node_meta, curve_meta, curve_point_meta,
    surface_meta, surface_point_meta.

All system-defined taxonomy (asset class, unit, shift, interp/extrap,
x/y axis kind, roll method, discount basis) lives in
riskquant.config.enums / riskquant.market_data and is resolved by the
RiskStaticConfig constructor.

Nothing in this module is imported by the valuation or simlab packages.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.config.static_config.loader import (
    load_risk_static_config_xlsx_into_dict,
)
from riskquant.config.static_config.schema_objects import (
    Ccy,
    CurveMeta,
    CurvePointMeta,
    HolidayCalendar,
    NodeMeta,
    SpotRollRule,
    SurfaceMeta,
    SurfacePointMeta,
)
from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig
from riskquant.config.static_config.validate import validate_static_config_dfs
from riskquant.constants import DATE_EPOCH

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Cell extraction primitives
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The only place raw DataFrame cell values become typed Python scalars.
# Deliberately thin — no business logic, just safe type coercion.


def _iter(df: pd.DataFrame) -> list[dict]:
    """
    Materialize a DataFrame into plain dicts with None for missing values.

    Per-column conversion avoids .where(..., None), which would force
    every column to object dtype.
    """
    if df is None or df.empty:
        return []

    cols: dict[str, list] = {}
    for col in df.columns:
        lst = df[col].tolist()
        cols[col] = [
            None if (v is pd.NA or (isinstance(v, float) and v != v)) else v
            for v in lst
        ]
    keys = list(cols.keys())
    return [
        dict(zip(keys, row, strict=True)) for row in zip(*cols.values(), strict=True)
    ]


def _str(v: object, *, upper: bool = False) -> str:
    if v is None:
        raise ValueError("Expected a non-null string cell, got None.")
    s = str(v).strip()
    return s.upper() if upper else s


def _opt_str(v: object, *, upper: bool = False) -> str | None:
    if v is None:
        return None
    s = str(v).strip()
    if s == "":
        return None
    return s.upper() if upper else s


def _int(v: object) -> int:
    if v is None:
        raise ValueError("Expected a non-null int cell, got None.")
    return int(v)  # type: ignore[arg-type]


def _opt_int(v: object) -> int | None:
    if v is None:
        return None
    if isinstance(v, float) and math.isnan(v):
        return None
    return int(v)  # type: ignore[arg-type]


def _bool(v: object) -> bool:
    if v is None or (isinstance(v, float) and math.isnan(v)):
        return False
    if isinstance(v, bool):
        return v
    if isinstance(v, (int, np.integer)):
        return bool(int(v))
    s = str(v).strip().upper()
    if s in ("TRUE", "1", "Y", "YES"):
        return True
    if s in ("FALSE", "0", "N", "NO", ""):
        return False
    raise ValueError(f"Cannot interpret {v!r} as boolean.")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Per-table compilers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def _compile_ccy(df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[str, Ccy]:
    out: dict[str, Ccy] = {}
    for r in _iter(df):
        name = _str(r["ccy_name"], upper=True)
        out[name] = Ccy(ccy_name=name, ccy_id=_int(r["ccy_id"]))
    return out


def _compile_holiday_calendars(df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[str, HolidayCalendar]:
    out: dict[str, HolidayCalendar] = {}
    if df is None or df.empty:
        return out

    epoch_ts = pd.Timestamp(DATE_EPOCH)
    dt_series = pd.to_datetime(df["holiday_date"], format="%Y-%m-%d", errors="raise")
    work = df.copy()
    work["_ord"] = (dt_series - epoch_ts).dt.days.astype(np.int32)

    for cal_name, group in work.groupby("holiday_calendar_name"):
        out[str(cal_name)] = HolidayCalendar(
            holiday_calendar_name=str(cal_name),
            holiday_dates_ord=np.sort(group["_ord"].unique().astype(np.int32)),
        )
    return out


def _compile_spot_roll_rule(df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[str, SpotRollRule]:
    out: dict[str, SpotRollRule] = {}
    for r in _iter(df):
        name = _str(r["rule_name"], upper=True)
        out[name] = SpotRollRule(
            rule_name=name,
            roll_method=_str(r["roll_method"], upper=True),
            offset_in_tenor_units=_int(r["offset_in_tenor_units"]),
            roll_month=_opt_int(r.get("roll_month")),
            roll_day=_opt_int(r.get("roll_day")),
            fh_bh_mmdd=_opt_str(r.get("fh_bh_mmdd")),
            post_roll_shift=_int(r["post_roll_shift"]),
        )
    return out


def _compile_node_meta(df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[int, NodeMeta]:
    out: dict[int, NodeMeta] = {}
    for r in _iter(df):
        node_id = _int(r["node_id"])
        out[node_id] = NodeMeta(
            node_id=node_id,
            node_name=_str(r["node_name"], upper=True),
            quote_ccy=_str(r["quote_ccy"], upper=True),
            asset_class=_str(r["asset_class"], upper=True),
            asset_subclass=_str(r["asset_subclass"], upper=True),
            quote_type=_str(r["quote_type"], upper=True),
            unit_scale=_str(r["unit_scale"], upper=True),
            shift_type=_str(r["shift_type"], upper=True),
            fx_base_ccy=_opt_str(r.get("fx_base_ccy"), upper=True),
        )
    return out


def _compile_curve_meta(df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[int, CurveMeta]:
    out: dict[int, CurveMeta] = {}
    for r in _iter(df):
        curve_id = _int(r["curve_id"])
        out[curve_id] = CurveMeta(
            curve_id=curve_id,
            curve_name=_str(r["curve_name"], upper=True),
            quote_ccy=_str(r["quote_ccy"], upper=True),
            asset_class=_str(r["asset_class"], upper=True),
            asset_subclass=_str(r["asset_subclass"], upper=True),
            quote_type=_str(r["quote_type"], upper=True),
            unit_scale=_str(r["unit_scale"], upper=True),
            shift_type=_str(r["shift_type"], upper=True),
            x_axis_kind=_str(r["x_axis_kind"], upper=True),
            x_interp_method=_str(r["x_interp_method"], upper=True),
            x_extrap_method=_str(r["x_extrap_method"], upper=True),
            spot_roll_rule_name=_opt_str(r.get("spot_roll_rule_name"), upper=True),
            discount_basis=_str(r["discount_basis"], upper=True),
            is_default_discount_for_ccy=_bool(r.get("is_default_discount_for_ccy")),
        )
    return out


def _compile_curve_point_meta(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    curve_meta: dict[int, CurveMeta],
) -> dict[int, CurvePointMeta]:
    out: dict[int, CurvePointMeta] = {}
    for r in _iter(df):
        curve_id = _int(r["curve_id"])
        rf_id = _int(r["rf_id"])
        out[rf_id] = CurvePointMeta(
            rf_id=rf_id,
            curve_id=curve_id,
            curve_name=curve_meta[curve_id].curve_name,
            tenor_token=_str(r["tenor_token"], upper=True),
        )
    return out


def _compile_surface_meta(df: pd.DataFrame) -> dict[int, SurfaceMeta]:
    out: dict[int, SurfaceMeta] = {}
    for r in _iter(df):
        surface_id = _int(r["surface_id"])
        out[surface_id] = SurfaceMeta(
            surface_id=surface_id,
            surface_name=_str(r["surface_name"], upper=True),
            quote_ccy=_str(r["quote_ccy"], upper=True),
            asset_class=_str(r["asset_class"], upper=True),
            asset_subclass=_str(r["asset_subclass"], upper=True),
            quote_type=_str(r["quote_type"], upper=True),
            unit_scale=_str(r["unit_scale"], upper=True),
            shift_type=_str(r["shift_type"], upper=True),
            x_axis_kind=_str(r["x_axis_kind"], upper=True),
            x_interp_method=_str(r["x_interp_method"], upper=True),
            x_extrap_method=_str(r["x_extrap_method"], upper=True),
            spot_roll_rule_name=_opt_str(r.get("spot_roll_rule_name"), upper=True),
            y_axis_kind=_str(r["y_axis_kind"], upper=True),
            y_interp_method=_str(r["y_interp_method"], upper=True),
            y_extrap_method=_str(r["y_extrap_method"], upper=True),
        )
    return out


def _compile_surface_point_meta(
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    surface_meta: dict[int, SurfaceMeta],
) -> dict[int, SurfacePointMeta]:
    out: dict[int, SurfacePointMeta] = {}
    for r in _iter(df):
        surface_id = _int(r["surface_id"])
        rf_id = _int(r["rf_id"])
        out[rf_id] = SurfacePointMeta(
            rf_id=rf_id,
            surface_id=surface_id,
            surface_name=surface_meta[surface_id].surface_name,
            x_tenor_token=_str(r["x_tenor_token"], upper=True),
            y_label=_str(r["y_label"], upper=False),
        )
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public API
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


def compile_risk_static_config_from_dict_of_dfs(
    dict_of_dfs: dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
) -> RiskStaticConfig:
    """
    Validate and compile a dict of DataFrames into a RiskStaticConfig.

    Parameters
    ----------
    dict_of_dfs
        Sheet-name -> DataFrame produced by the loader. All 8 required
        sheets must be present and well-formed; see REQUIRED_SHEETS in
        validate.py.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskStaticConfig
        Fully compiled, Pandas-free runtime config with every derived
        index already built.
    """
    validate_static_config_dfs(dict_of_dfs)

    ccy_dict = _compile_ccy(dict_of_dfs["ccy"])
    holiday_calendars_dict = _compile_holiday_calendars(
        dict_of_dfs["holiday_calendars"]
    )
    spot_roll_rule_dict = _compile_spot_roll_rule(dict_of_dfs["spot_roll_rule"])
    node_meta_dict = _compile_node_meta(dict_of_dfs["node_meta"])
    curve_meta_dict = _compile_curve_meta(dict_of_dfs["curve_meta"])
    curve_point_meta_dict = _compile_curve_point_meta(
        dict_of_dfs["curve_point_meta"], curve_meta_dict
    )
    surface_meta_dict = _compile_surface_meta(dict_of_dfs["surface_meta"])
    surface_point_meta_dict = _compile_surface_point_meta(
        dict_of_dfs["surface_point_meta"], surface_meta_dict
    )

    return RiskStaticConfig(
        ccy_dict=ccy_dict,
        holiday_calendars_dict=holiday_calendars_dict,
        spot_roll_rule_dict=spot_roll_rule_dict,
        node_meta_dict=node_meta_dict,
        curve_meta_dict=curve_meta_dict,
        curve_point_meta_dict=curve_point_meta_dict,
        surface_meta_dict=surface_meta_dict,
        surface_point_meta_dict=surface_point_meta_dict,
    )


def compile_risk_static_config_from_xlsx(path: str | Path) -> RiskStaticConfig:
    """
    Load, validate, and compile an .xlsx workbook into a RiskStaticConfig.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path
        Path to the Excel workbook with the 8 required sheets.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskStaticConfig
        Compiled runtime configuration.
    """
    dict_of_dfs = load_risk_static_config_xlsx_into_dict(path=path)
    return compile_risk_static_config_from_dict_of_dfs(dict_of_dfs)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/config/static_config/loader.py
"""
Thin xlsx loader for the static-config workbook.

The loader does not validate anything beyond Excel parseability; all
schema / semantic validation lives in `validate_static_config_dfs`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

import pandas as pd

from riskquant.config.static_config.validate import REQUIRED_SHEETS


def load_risk_static_config_xlsx_into_dict(
    *,
    path: str | Path,
) -> dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    """
    Read every required sheet from an .xlsx workbook into a dict of DataFrames.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path
        Path to the Excel workbook.

    Returns
    -------
    dict[str, pd.DataFrame]
        Sheet-name -> DataFrame for every name in `REQUIRED_SHEETS`.

    Raises
    ------
    FileNotFoundError
        If the workbook does not exist.
    ValueError
        If a required sheet is missing from the workbook.
    """
    p = Path(path)
    if not p.is_file():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"Workbook not found: {p}")

    excel = pd.ExcelFile(p)
    available: set[str] = {str(name) for name in excel.sheet_names}
    missing = REQUIRED_SHEETS - available
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Workbook {p.name} is missing required sheet(s): {sorted(missing)}.\n"
            f"Sheets present: {sorted(available)}."
        )

    return {name: excel.parse(name) for name in REQUIRED_SHEETS}
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/static_config/rf_resolution.py
"""
Date-sensitive resolution of relative risk-factor names to absolute names.

Examples for as-of=2026-05-23:
    BRENT[M+0]          ->   BRENT_202607        (NEXT_MONTH, CALENDAR_PERIOD)
    HYDRO[Y+0]          ->   HYDRO_2026          (ANNUAL_ROLL_APRIL_BGN, ROLL_ON_MMDD)
    ERCOT[HY+0]         ->   ERCOT_2026_BH       (HALF_YEAR_LABEL, HALF_YEAR_BY_LABEL)

The public entry point is `build_risk_factor_name_mapping_for_as_of_date`
which returns a frozen, bidirectionally indexed `RiskFactorAbsToRelNameIdMap`.

Dispatch is a registry keyed by (XAxisKind, RollMethod) — never a
string-equality ladder. The validator guarantees only allowed pairs
ever reach the resolver.

Nodes do not resolve: their absolute name equals their declared name
(node_name). Surface point names follow the same model as curve points
with an additional bracketed y label.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from bisect import bisect_left
from collections.abc import Callable
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from datetime import date
from typing import overload

from riskquant.config.enums.roll_methods import RollMethod
from riskquant.config.enums.x_axis_kinds import XAxisKind
from riskquant.config.static_config.schema_objects import (
    SpotRollRule,
)
from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig
from riskquant.utils.tenors import (
    add_months_to_date_and_return_yyyymm01,
    fmt_date_as_str_yyyymm,
    get_spot_year,
    parse_bracket_token,
)

# ============================================================
# Resolver type
# ============================================================

ResolverFn = Callable[
    ["_ResolverContext"],
    str,  # absolute date suffix, e.g. "202607" / "2026" / "2026_FH" / "20260630"
]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class _ResolverContext:
    """Per-token resolution context. Holds everything the dispatch fn needs."""

    container_name: str
    relative_name: str
    rule: SpotRollRule
    token_offset: int
    as_of_date: date


# ============================================================
# Per-(XAxisKind, RollMethod) resolvers
# ============================================================


def _resolve_offset_m_calendar_period(ctx: _ResolverContext) -> str:
    """OFFSET_M + CALENDAR_PERIOD  ->  '<curve>_YYYYMM'."""
    spot = add_months_to_date_and_return_yyyymm01(
        d=date(ctx.as_of_date.year, ctx.as_of_date.month, 1),
        months_to_add=ctx.rule.offset_in_tenor_units,
    )
    target = add_months_to_date_and_return_yyyymm01(
        d=spot, months_to_add=ctx.token_offset
    )
    return fmt_date_as_str_yyyymm(target)


def _resolve_offset_m_half_year_by_date(ctx: _ResolverContext) -> str:
    """OFFSET_M + HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE  ->  '<curve>_YYYYMMDD' on the next FH/BH boundary."""
    fh_bh_mmdd = _require_fh_bh_mmdd(ctx)
    abs_dt = _half_year_boundary_by_date(
        as_of_date=ctx.as_of_date,
        fh_bh_mmdd=fh_bh_mmdd,
        token_offset=ctx.token_offset,
        relative_name=ctx.relative_name,
        container_name=ctx.container_name,
    )
    return f"{abs_dt.year:04d}{abs_dt.month:02d}{abs_dt.day:02d}"


def _resolve_offset_y_calendar_period(ctx: _ResolverContext) -> str:
    """OFFSET_Y + CALENDAR_PERIOD  ->  '<curve>_YYYY'."""
    target_year = (
        ctx.as_of_date.year + ctx.rule.offset_in_tenor_units + ctx.token_offset
    )
    return f"{target_year:04d}"


def _resolve_offset_y_roll_on_mmdd(ctx: _ResolverContext) -> str:
    """OFFSET_Y + ROLL_ON_MMDD  ->  '<curve>_YYYY' with year picked by roll boundary."""
    spot_year = get_spot_year(
        as_of_date=ctx.as_of_date,
        roll_month=int(ctx.rule.roll_month or 0),
        roll_day=int(ctx.rule.roll_day or 0),
        post_roll_shift=ctx.rule.post_roll_shift,
    )
    return f"{spot_year + ctx.token_offset:04d}"


def _resolve_offset_hy_half_year_by_date(ctx: _ResolverContext) -> str:
    """OFFSET_HY + HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE  ->  '<curve>_YYYYMMDD'."""
    fh_bh_mmdd = _require_fh_bh_mmdd(ctx)
    abs_dt = _half_year_boundary_by_date(
        as_of_date=ctx.as_of_date,
        fh_bh_mmdd=fh_bh_mmdd,
        token_offset=ctx.token_offset,
        relative_name=ctx.relative_name,
        container_name=ctx.container_name,
    )
    return f"{abs_dt.year:04d}{abs_dt.month:02d}{abs_dt.day:02d}"


def _resolve_offset_hy_half_year_by_label(ctx: _ResolverContext) -> str:
    """OFFSET_HY + HALF_YEAR_BY_LABEL  ->  '<curve>_YYYY_FH' / '<curve>_YYYY_BH'."""
    fh_bh_mmdd = _require_fh_bh_mmdd(ctx)
    return _half_year_label_suffix(
        as_of_date=ctx.as_of_date,
        fh_bh_mmdd=fh_bh_mmdd,
        token_offset=ctx.token_offset,
    )


# Dense registry: (XAxisKind, RollMethod) -> resolver. Branch-free dispatch.
_OFFSET_RESOLVERS: dict[tuple[XAxisKind, RollMethod], ResolverFn] = {
    (XAxisKind.OFFSET_M, RollMethod.CALENDAR_PERIOD): _resolve_offset_m_calendar_period,
    (
        XAxisKind.OFFSET_M,
        RollMethod.HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE,
    ): _resolve_offset_m_half_year_by_date,
    (XAxisKind.OFFSET_Y, RollMethod.CALENDAR_PERIOD): _resolve_offset_y_calendar_period,
    (XAxisKind.OFFSET_Y, RollMethod.ROLL_ON_MMDD): _resolve_offset_y_roll_on_mmdd,
    (
        XAxisKind.OFFSET_HY,
        RollMethod.HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE,
    ): _resolve_offset_hy_half_year_by_date,
    (
        XAxisKind.OFFSET_HY,
        RollMethod.HALF_YEAR_BY_LABEL,
    ): _resolve_offset_hy_half_year_by_label,
}


# ============================================================
# Half-year helpers
# ============================================================


def _require_fh_bh_mmdd(ctx: _ResolverContext) -> str:
    """Half-year roll methods require fh_bh_mmdd to be populated."""
    if not ctx.rule.fh_bh_mmdd:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Spot roll rule {ctx.rule.rule_name!r} requires fh_bh_mmdd "
            f"but it is absent.\n"
            f"relative={ctx.relative_name!r}, container={ctx.container_name!r}"
        )
    return ctx.rule.fh_bh_mmdd


def _parse_fh_bh_mmdd(fh_bh_mmdd: str) -> tuple[int, int, int, int]:
    """Parse 'MMDD|MMDD' into (fh_month, fh_day, bh_month, bh_day)."""
    parts = fh_bh_mmdd.split("|")
    if len(parts) != 2 or len(parts[0]) != 4 or len(parts[1]) != 4:
        raise ValueError(f"fh_bh_mmdd must be 'MMDD|MMDD', got {fh_bh_mmdd!r}")
    fh_mm, fh_dd = int(parts[0][:2]), int(parts[0][2:])
    bh_mm, bh_dd = int(parts[1][:2]), int(parts[1][2:])
    return fh_mm, fh_dd, bh_mm, bh_dd


def _half_year_boundary_by_date(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    fh_bh_mmdd: str,
    token_offset: int,
    relative_name: str,
    container_name: str,
) -> date:
    """
    Resolve a half-year boundary date.

    Builds a sorted list of FH/BH boundary dates spanning a window around
    as_of_date, then picks the boundary at (bisect_left index + token_offset).
    """
    fh_mm, fh_dd, bh_mm, bh_dd = _parse_fh_bh_mmdd(fh_bh_mmdd)
    as_of_year = as_of_date.year
    look = max(3, abs(token_offset) + 1)

    boundaries: list[date] = []
    for y in range(as_of_year - look, as_of_year + look + 1):
        boundaries.append(date(y, fh_mm, fh_dd))
        boundaries.append(date(y, bh_mm, bh_dd))
    if fh_mm > bh_mm or (fh_mm == bh_mm and fh_dd > bh_dd):
        boundaries.sort()

    idx_target = bisect_left(boundaries, as_of_date) + token_offset
    if idx_target < 0 or idx_target >= len(boundaries):
        raise ValueError(
            f"HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE offset {token_offset} out of range.\n"
            f"relative={relative_name!r}, container={container_name!r}"
        )
    return boundaries[idx_target]


def _half_year_label_suffix(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    fh_bh_mmdd: str,
    token_offset: int,
) -> str:
    """Resolve a half-year label suffix (YYYY_FH / YYYY_BH)."""
    fh_mm, fh_dd, _, _ = _parse_fh_bh_mmdd(fh_bh_mmdd)
    current_half_idx = 0 if as_of_date <= date(as_of_date.year, fh_mm, fh_dd) else 1
    total = (as_of_date.year * 2) + current_half_idx + token_offset
    target_year = total // 2
    target_label = "FH" if (total % 2 == 0) else "BH"
    return f"{target_year:04d}_{target_label}"


# ============================================================
# RiskFactorAbsToRelNameIdMap
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class RiskFactorAbsToRelNameIdMap:
    """
    Bidirectionally indexed mapping between absolute and relative
    risk-factor names for one as-of date.

    Conceptual model
    ----------------
    Relative names (from config):
        BRENT[M+0]                 -- curve point
        VOL_BRENT[M+0][STRIKE=50]  -- surface point
        USDEUR                     -- node (same as absolute)

    Absolute names (as-of-resolved):
        BRENT_202607
        VOL_BRENT_202607[STRIKE=50]
        USDEUR

    Lookup contract
    ---------------
    O(1) lookup for both absolute and relative forms via the unified
    `lookup` dict. Hot-path callers should use `get_rf_id(name)` and
    treat it as the only lookup function they need.
    """

    as_of_date: date
    abs_to_rf_id: dict[str, int]
    abs_to_rel_name: dict[str, str]

    _rel_to_abs: dict[str, str] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _rel_to_id: dict[str, int] = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _lookup: dict[str, int] = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        abs_keys = set(self.abs_to_rf_id)
        rel_keys = set(self.abs_to_rel_name)
        if abs_keys != rel_keys:
            sym_diff = sorted(abs_keys ^ rel_keys)[:25]
            raise ValueError(
                f"abs_to_rf_id and abs_to_rel_name keys disagree: {sym_diff}"
            )

        rel_to_abs: dict[str, str] = {}
        rel_to_id: dict[str, int] = {}
        for abs_name, rel_name in self.abs_to_rel_name.items():
            rf_id = self.abs_to_rf_id[abs_name]
            if rel_name in rel_to_abs and rel_to_abs[rel_name] != abs_name:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Duplicate relative name {rel_name!r} maps to both "
                    f"{rel_to_abs[rel_name]!r} and {abs_name!r}"
                )
            rel_to_abs[rel_name] = abs_name
            rel_to_id[rel_name] = rf_id

        # Defensive disjointness check.
        # The static_config validator already enforces (a) global name uniqueness
        # across all five namespaces and (b) that the only legitimate overlap is
        # nodes, where abs_name == rel_name == node_name AND abs_id == rel_id.
        # This loop only fires if those invariants are broken by a future change
        # to validate.py or schema_objects.py — at which point the loud error here
        # is exactly what we want.
        for name in abs_keys & set(rel_to_id):
            if self.abs_to_rf_id[name] != rel_to_id[name]:
                raise ValueError(
                    f"Name {name!r} appears as both absolute and relative "
                    f"with different rf_ids "
                    f"(abs={self.abs_to_rf_id[name]}, rel={rel_to_id[name]})"
                )

        lookup = {**self.abs_to_rf_id, **rel_to_id}

        object.__setattr__(self, "_rel_to_abs", rel_to_abs)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_rel_to_id", rel_to_id)
        object.__setattr__(self, "_lookup", lookup)

    # ----- Single hot-path lookup -----

    def get_rf_id(self, name: str) -> int:
        """Resolve either an absolute or relative name -> rf_id."""
        return self._lookup[name]

    # ----- Explicit lookups (clarity / validation paths) -----

    def get_rf_id_for_abs_name(self, abs_name: str) -> int:
        return self.abs_to_rf_id[abs_name]

    @overload
    def get_rf_id_for_rel_name(self, rel_name: str) -> int: ...
    @overload
    def get_rf_id_for_rel_name(self, rel_name: list[str]) -> list[int]: ...

    def get_rf_id_for_rel_name(self, rel_name: str | list[str]) -> int | list[int]:
        m = self._rel_to_id
        if isinstance(rel_name, str):
            return m[rel_name]
        return [m[r] for r in rel_name]

    def get_abs_name_for_rel_name(self, rel_name: str | list[str]) -> str | list[str]:
        m = self._rel_to_abs
        if isinstance(rel_name, str):
            return m[rel_name]
        return [m[r] for r in rel_name]

    def get_rel_name_for_abs_name(self, abs_name: str) -> str:
        return self.abs_to_rel_name[abs_name]

    # ----- Properties -----

    @property
    def num_risk_factors(self) -> int:
        return len(self.abs_to_rf_id)

    @property
    def rel_name_to_rf_id(self) -> dict[str, int]:
        return self._rel_to_id

    @property
    def rel_name_to_abs_name(self) -> dict[str, str]:
        return self._rel_to_abs

    @property
    def lookup(self) -> dict[str, int]:
        return self._lookup


# ============================================================
# Resolution engine
# ============================================================


def build_risk_factor_name_mapping_for_as_of_date(
    *,
    risk_static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
    as_of_date: date | str,
) -> RiskFactorAbsToRelNameIdMap:
    """
    Resolve every relative risk-factor name to its absolute name for one as_of_date.

    Three sources are merged:

      1. Nodes — abs_name == rel_name == node_name. No resolution.
      2. Curve points — abs_name = f"{curve_name}_{resolved_suffix}".
         LABEL curves: resolved_suffix is the bare label (e.g. "ON", "3M");
         OFFSET curves: resolved_suffix is computed via the registry.
      3. Surface points — abs_name = f"{surface_name}_{resolved_suffix}[{y_label}]".
         Same resolution rules as curves for the x axis.

    Parameters
    ----------
    risk_static_config
        Compiled static configuration.
    as_of_date
        Valuation date used to resolve OFFSET tokens. ISO string accepted.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorAbsToRelNameIdMap
        Bidirectional abs<->rel<->rf_id mapping.
    """
    if as_of_date is None:
        raise ValueError("as_of_date is required.")
    if isinstance(as_of_date, str):
        as_of_date = date.fromisoformat(as_of_date)

    abs_to_rf_id: dict[str, int] = {}
    abs_to_rel_name: dict[str, str] = {}

    # ---- Nodes ----
    for node in risk_static_config.node_meta_dict.values():
        abs_to_rf_id[node.node_name] = int(node.node_id)
        abs_to_rel_name[node.node_name] = node.node_name

    # ---- Curve points ----
    for pt in risk_static_config.curve_point_meta_dict.values():
        cm = risk_static_config.curve_meta_dict[pt.curve_id]
        rel_name = f"{cm.curve_name}{pt.tenor_token}"
        suffix = _resolve_curve_or_surface_suffix(
            container_name=cm.curve_name,
            container_kind="curve",
            x_axis_kind_name=cm.x_axis_kind,
            spot_roll_rule_name=cm.spot_roll_rule_name,
            spot_roll_rule_dict=risk_static_config.spot_roll_rule_dict,
            tenor_token=pt.tenor_token,
            relative_name=rel_name,
            as_of_date=as_of_date,
        )
        abs_name = f"{cm.curve_name}_{suffix}"
        abs_to_rf_id[abs_name] = int(pt.rf_id)
        abs_to_rel_name[abs_name] = rel_name

    # ---- Surface points ----
    for pt in risk_static_config.surface_point_meta_dict.values():
        sm = risk_static_config.surface_meta_dict[pt.surface_id]
        rel_name = f"{sm.surface_name}{pt.x_tenor_token}[{pt.y_label}]"
        suffix = _resolve_curve_or_surface_suffix(
            container_name=sm.surface_name,
            container_kind="surface",
            x_axis_kind_name=sm.x_axis_kind,
            spot_roll_rule_name=sm.spot_roll_rule_name,
            spot_roll_rule_dict=risk_static_config.spot_roll_rule_dict,
            tenor_token=pt.x_tenor_token,
            relative_name=rel_name,
            as_of_date=as_of_date,
        )
        abs_name = f"{sm.surface_name}_{suffix}[{pt.y_label}]"
        abs_to_rf_id[abs_name] = int(pt.rf_id)
        abs_to_rel_name[abs_name] = rel_name

    return RiskFactorAbsToRelNameIdMap(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        abs_to_rf_id=abs_to_rf_id,
        abs_to_rel_name=abs_to_rel_name,
    )


def _resolve_curve_or_surface_suffix(
    *,
    container_name: str,
    container_kind: str,
    x_axis_kind_name: str,
    spot_roll_rule_name: str | None,
    spot_roll_rule_dict: dict[str, SpotRollRule],
    tenor_token: str,
    relative_name: str,
    as_of_date: date,
) -> str:
    """Resolve a single curve/surface point's absolute-name suffix."""
    x_kind = XAxisKind[x_axis_kind_name]

    if x_kind == XAxisKind.LABEL:
        label, signed_offset = parse_bracket_token(tenor_token)
        if signed_offset is not None:
            raise ValueError(
                f"LABEL {container_kind} {container_name!r} has offset token "
                f"{tenor_token!r}; LABEL containers accept label-only tokens."
            )
        return label

    if spot_roll_rule_name is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"OFFSET {container_kind} {container_name!r} has no spot_roll_rule_name."
        )
    rule = spot_roll_rule_dict.get(spot_roll_rule_name)
    if rule is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Unknown spot_roll_rule_name={spot_roll_rule_name!r} for "
            f"{container_kind} {container_name!r}."
        )

    _, token_offset = parse_bracket_token(tenor_token)
    if token_offset is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"OFFSET {container_kind} {container_name!r} requires a signed "
            f"integer offset token, got {tenor_token!r}."
        )

    method = RollMethod[rule.roll_method]
    resolver = _OFFSET_RESOLVERS.get((x_kind, method))
    if resolver is None:
        raise ValueError(
            f"Unsupported (x_axis_kind, roll_method) pair: "
            f"({x_kind.name}, {method.name})."
        )

    return resolver(
        _ResolverContext(
            container_name=container_name,
            relative_name=relative_name,
            rule=rule,
            token_offset=token_offset,
            as_of_date=as_of_date,
        )
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/config/static_config/schema_objects.py
"""
Immutable value objects for the god-tier static config pipeline.

One dataclass per input table. Every field is non-null in its own table:
no nullability cascades, no "must be null when this is null" rules. The
three first-class object kinds (Node, Curve, Surface) each get their own
metadata table and child point table.

  NodeMeta              - scalar observable. A node IS its own risk factor.
                          FX rate nodes additionally carry fx_base_ccy.
  CurveMeta             - 1D function metadata. Optional discount-basis tag
                          marks IR discount curves.
  CurvePointMeta        - one row per point on a curve. The point's
                          tenor_token is parsed using the parent's
                          x_axis_kind.
  SurfaceMeta           - 2D function metadata (expiry x strike-axis).
  SurfacePointMeta      - one row per point on a surface; x_tenor_token
                          is parsed using the surface's x_axis_kind,
                          y_label using its y_axis_kind.
  SpotRollRule          - one named rule. PK is the rule name.
  HolidayCalendar       - one calendar with sorted int32 holiday ordinals.
  Ccy                   - one currency with its dense int8 id.

All system-defined taxonomies (asset class, unit, shift, interp/extrap,
x/y axis kind, roll method, discount basis) live in riskquant.config.enums
and riskquant.market_data — never inside these dataclasses as derived state.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

# ============================================================
# CURRENCY
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class Ccy:
    """A currency in the organisation's active universe."""

    ccy_name: str
    ccy_id: int


# ============================================================
# HOLIDAY CALENDAR
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class HolidayCalendar:
    """
    Holiday dates for one named calendar, stored as sorted int32
    ordinals (days since 1970-01-01) for O(log N) np.searchsorted
    bisection in the business-day engine.
    """

    holiday_calendar_name: str
    holiday_dates_ord: npt.NDArray[np.int32]


# ============================================================
# SPOT ROLL RULE
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class SpotRollRule:
    """
    Named spot-roll rule.

    PK is `rule_name`. The rule's `roll_method` determines which
    parameters are meaningful — the validator checks each parameter
    against the method:

      CALENDAR_PERIOD   -> uses offset_in_tenor_units
      ROLL_ON_MMDD      -> uses roll_month, roll_day, post_roll_shift
      HALF_YEAR_BY_DATE -> uses fh_bh_mmdd
      HALF_YEAR_BY_LABEL-> uses fh_bh_mmdd

    Unused parameters carry sentinel zeros (offset, post_roll_shift)
    or None (roll_month, roll_day, fh_bh_mmdd) — these are never read by
    the resolution code for a non-applicable method.
    """

    rule_name: str
    roll_method: str
    offset_in_tenor_units: int
    roll_month: int | None
    roll_day: int | None
    fh_bh_mmdd: str | None
    post_roll_shift: int


# ============================================================
# NODE  (scalar; IS its own risk factor)
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class NodeMeta:
    """
    A scalar observable. Every node is both a container (for ingest
    classification) and a risk factor (for market-data lookup). The
    node_id IS the rf_id; there is no child point table.

    fx_base_ccy contract:
      asset_class == "FX"  <=> fx_base_ccy is not None
      For FX nodes, node_name == fx_base_ccy + quote_ccy is enforced.
    """

    node_id: int
    node_name: str

    quote_ccy: str
    asset_class: str
    asset_subclass: str

    quote_type: str
    unit_scale: str
    shift_type: str

    fx_base_ccy: str | None


# ============================================================
# CURVE
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CurveMeta:
    """
    A 1D function metadata row.

    x_axis_kind contract:
      LABEL                    -> spot_roll_rule_name is None
      OFFSET_M / OFFSET_Y /
      OFFSET_HY                -> spot_roll_rule_name is non-None

    discount_basis contract:
      "NOT_A_DISCOUNT_CURVE"   -> non-discount curve (e.g. commodity forward).
                                  is_default_discount_for_ccy must be False.
      any other value          -> discount curve. asset_class must be "IR".
                                  Exactly one discount curve per (ccy) is
                                  flagged is_default_discount_for_ccy=True
                                  and its basis must be the firm default.
    """

    curve_id: int
    curve_name: str

    quote_ccy: str
    asset_class: str
    asset_subclass: str

    quote_type: str
    unit_scale: str
    shift_type: str

    x_axis_kind: str
    x_interp_method: str
    x_extrap_method: str
    spot_roll_rule_name: str | None

    discount_basis: str
    is_default_discount_for_ccy: bool


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class CurvePointMeta:
    """One addressable point on a curve."""

    rf_id: int
    curve_id: int
    curve_name: str
    tenor_token: str


# ============================================================
# SURFACE
# ============================================================


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class SurfaceMeta:
    """
    A 2D function metadata row. Same x_axis_kind / spot_roll_rule
    contract as CurveMeta. y_axis_kind has no roll-method coupling: the
    y coordinate is value-typed (a strike, log-strike, moneyness, delta).
    """

    surface_id: int
    surface_name: str

    quote_ccy: str
    asset_class: str
    asset_subclass: str

    quote_type: str
    unit_scale: str
    shift_type: str

    x_axis_kind: str
    x_interp_method: str
    x_extrap_method: str
    spot_roll_rule_name: str | None

    y_axis_kind: str
    y_interp_method: str
    y_extrap_method: str


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class SurfacePointMeta:
    """One addressable point on a surface."""

    rf_id: int
    surface_id: int
    surface_name: str
    x_tenor_token: str
    y_label: str
```
</file>
