<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\builder.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/builder.py
"""
Market-data matrix builder.

Sits on the producer side of the valuation contract. Takes raw
scenario-shocked levels plus the `UixMapper` describing the run's
global universe, and emits a frozen `MarketDataMatrix` whose every
axis is aligned with the per-trade `_uix` coordinates carried by the
compiled blocks.

Key responsibilities
--------------------
1. FX effective matrix. Reads `UixMapper.fx_effective_pairs` to learn
   which `(fx_uix, invert)` pairs are needed across the portfolio,
   then materialises `fx_spots_effective: (K, S)` from the raw
   `(F, S)` direct-quote scenario matrix. Inverted rows are resolved
   via vectorised `np.reciprocal(out=...)`; direct rows are a single
   block copy. The pricers see zero inversion math.

2. Discount-factor grid. Log-linear interpolation in time, flat
   extrapolation. Routed through the zero-allocation Numba kernel
   with the pre-allocated output buffer.

3. Price-curve grid. Same shape as discount, but interpolation
   method varies per curve and is dispatched via `CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY`
   on `(interp_method, extrap_method)` group masks.

4. Time-to-expiry grid. Act/365.25 day count from `as_of_date` to
   each unique portfolio date, floored at 1e-9 to keep `sqrt(tte)`
   well-defined for same-day expiries.

5. Vol surfaces. Two-step pipeline:
   (a) DELTA -> MONEYNESS_FWD calibration for DELTA-quoted surfaces
       via `market_data.calibrate.delta_to_moneyness`. Per-surface
       (T_REF, σ_REF) scalars produce expiry/scenario-independent
       moneyness pillars. The conversion is F-independent so the
       calibrator does not need FX or discount-curve context.
   (b) Time-slice interpolation onto the unique-date universe.
       Output layout is `[surface, expiry, scenario, pillar]` so
       the strike-axis interpolation in the option-curve kernel
       can walk a contiguous pillar run per (trade, scenario).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from datetime import date

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import DAYS_PER_YR_ACT_365_25, TTE_FLOOR
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_defs import (
    interp_linear_on_log_values_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
)
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_dispatch import CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY
from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
from riskquant.market_data.transformers.delta_to_moneyness import (
    calibrate_delta_surfaces_to_moneyness,
)
from riskquant.universe import UixMapper
from riskquant.utils.dates import date_to_ord32

# ==============================================================================
# FX EFFECTIVE
# ==============================================================================


def _build_fx_spots_effective(
    *,
    raw_fx_spots: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    fx_effective_pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Materialise the `(K, S)` effective FX matrix from the dedup pair table.

    `fx_effective_pairs[k] == (fx_uix, invert)` — `fx_uix` is a row index
    into `raw_fx_spots` (or `< 0` for an identity-row sentinel), `invert`
    is `0` for direct and `1` for inverted (i.e. `1 / rate`).

    Scatter contract (CRITICAL)
    ---------------------------
    `out[mask, :] = src` is the NumPy SETITEM form: it scatters `src`
    back into `out` via fancy/boolean-index assignment. This is
    in-place.

    `np.copyto(out[mask, :], src)` is a different code path. `out[mask,
    :]` here is a GETITEM expression; boolean-mask indexing returns a
    COPY of the masked rows, not a view. `np.copyto` then writes into
    that throw-away copy and the actual `out` is never touched. Same
    trap as the original `_build_price_curve_grid` bug — see scatter
    comment in that function.

    The fix is to use `__setitem__` directly:
        out[direct_mask, :] = raw_fx_spots[fx_uixs[direct_mask]]
        out[invert_mask, :] = 1.0 / raw_fx_spots[fx_uixs[invert_mask]]

    Both forms are scatter-assignment back into `out`. The invert path
    is one fused float64 division per scenario per inverted row — the
    `1.0 / x` expression allocates the result, but the allocation is
    K_invert*S which is the size of the final scatter target anyway,
    so no extra peak memory vs the broken `np.reciprocal(..., out=...)`
    form.
    """
    k_pairs = fx_effective_pairs.shape[0]
    out = np.empty((k_pairs, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)

    if k_pairs == 0:
        return out

    if fx_effective_pairs.shape[1] != 2:
        raise ValueError(
            f"fx_effective_pairs must have shape (K, 2); got {fx_effective_pairs.shape}"
        )

    fx_uixs = fx_effective_pairs[:, 0]
    invs = fx_effective_pairs[:, 1]

    sentinel_mask = fx_uixs < 0
    direct_mask = (~sentinel_mask) & (invs == 0)
    invert_mask = (~sentinel_mask) & (invs == 1)

    if sentinel_mask.any():
        out[sentinel_mask, :] = 1.0

    if direct_mask.any():
        out[direct_mask, :] = raw_fx_spots[fx_uixs[direct_mask]]

    if invert_mask.any():
        out[invert_mask, :] = 1.0 / raw_fx_spots[fx_uixs[invert_mask]]

    return out


# ==============================================================================
# SCALAR NODES (UNIVERSE-ALIGNED — item C, option A1)
# ==============================================================================


def _build_scalar_node_levels(
    *,
    raw_scalar_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    scalar_node_uix_in_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_market_ids: int,
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Scatter dense scalar-node levels into one `market_id_universe`-aligned
    `(num_market_ids, num_scenarios)` matrix.

    The compilers index `prices` / `forward_nodes` / `vol_nodes` with a
    `market_id_universe` UIX (`map_market_ids_to_uix`), so axis-0 of the
    returned array MUST be `len(market_id_universe)`. The dense input rows
    (the price / fwd-node / vol-node sub-block of `flat_levels`, in that
    order) carry their universe position in `scalar_node_uix_in_universe`;
    we scatter each into its row.

    Option A1: one backing array shared by all three pricer-facing scalar
    fields. The price / fwd / vol id sets are disjoint, so no row is written
    twice and each pricer reads only the rows of its own class. Rows of the
    universe that are NOT scalar nodes (FX-spot ids, etc.) are never read by
    a scalar pricer, so they are left as `np.empty` garbage — touching them
    would be a wiring bug the validator already rejects, not a silent
    miss-read.

    Scatter contract: `out[idx_rows, :] = src` is fancy-index SETITEM —
    in-place. Same trap documented in `_build_fx_spots_effective`.
    """
    out = np.empty((num_market_ids, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)
    if raw_scalar_nodes.shape[0] == 0:
        return out
    if raw_scalar_nodes.shape[0] != scalar_node_uix_in_universe.shape[0]:
        raise ValueError(
            f"scalar-node row count {raw_scalar_nodes.shape[0]} != universe-index "
            f"count {scalar_node_uix_in_universe.shape[0]}"
        )
    out[scalar_node_uix_in_universe, :] = raw_scalar_nodes
    return out


def _scatter_price_curve_grid_to_universe(
    *,
    dense_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    price_curve_uix_in_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_market_ids: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Scatter the dense `(C, D, S)` price-curve grid into a
    `(num_market_ids, D, S)` array aligned to `market_id_universe`.

    Forward-curve underlyings resolve to a curve CONTAINER id that lands in
    `market_id_universe`; `compile_vanilla_forward_curve` sets
    `price_curve_uix = map_market_ids_to_uix(...)` and the kernel reads
    `price_curve_grid[price_curve_uix, delivery_uix, s]`. So axis-0 of the
    grid MUST be `len(market_id_universe)` — the exact A1 treatment one axis
    up. `price_curve_uix_in_universe[c]` is the universe row of dense curve
    `c`. Universe rows that are NOT price-curve containers are never read by
    the forward-curve kernel, so they remain `np.empty` garbage (a wiring
    bug there would be rejected by validation, not silently mis-read).
    """
    d_dates = dense_grid.shape[1] if dense_grid.ndim == 3 else 0
    num_scenarios = dense_grid.shape[2] if dense_grid.ndim == 3 else 0
    out = np.empty((num_market_ids, d_dates, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)
    if dense_grid.shape[0] == 0:
        return out
    if dense_grid.shape[0] != price_curve_uix_in_universe.shape[0]:
        raise ValueError(
            f"price-curve grid row count {dense_grid.shape[0]} != universe-index "
            f"count {price_curve_uix_in_universe.shape[0]}"
        )
    out[price_curve_uix_in_universe, :, :] = dense_grid
    return out


# ==============================================================================
# CURVE GRIDS
# ==============================================================================


def _build_price_curve_grid(
    *,
    raw_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    target_dates: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    interp_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    extrap_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Group-dispatched 3-D curve interpolation onto the unique-date grid.

    Curves sharing the same `(interp_method, extrap_method)` pair are
    gathered into a contiguous scratch buffer, fed to the dispatched
    kernel, then scatter-assigned back into `out_grid`.

    Scatter contract
    ----------------
    `out_grid[idx]` on the LHS of an assignment is NumPy fancy-index
    setitem and is in-place. `out_grid[idx]` on the RHS (the kernel's
    original signature) would have been a fresh COPY — the kernel
    would have written into the copy, the copy would have been
    discarded, and `out_grid` would have been left as `np.empty()`
    garbage. The scratch + scatter pattern is the correct fix.
    """
    c_curves = raw_values.shape[0]
    d_dates = target_dates.shape[0]

    out_grid = np.empty((c_curves, d_dates, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)

    if d_dates == 0 or c_curves == 0:
        return out_grid

    for (interp_id, extrap_id), kernel in CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY.items():
        mask = (interp_methods == interp_id) & (extrap_methods == extrap_id)
        if not mask.any():
            continue

        idx = np.where(mask)[0]
        n_in_group = idx.shape[0]

        # Gather to a contiguous scratch buffer the kernel can write to.
        scratch = np.empty(
            (n_in_group, d_dates, num_scenarios),
            dtype=np.float64,
        )
        kernel(
            np.ascontiguousarray(raw_tenors[idx]),
            np.ascontiguousarray(raw_values[idx]),
            target_dates,
            scratch,
        )
        # Scatter back. Fancy-indexed ASSIGNMENT is in-place.
        out_grid[idx] = scratch

    return out_grid


def _build_discount_factor_grid(
    *,
    raw_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_ln_dfs: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    target_dates: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Discount-factor grid: log-linear interpolation in time, flat extrap.

    Domain-convention: every discount curve in the system is quoted as
    log(DF) at pillar dates and interpolated linearly on log(DF), which
    is equivalent to linear interpolation on zero rates between pillars.
    The flat extrapolation prevents pathological exponentials at the long
    end and matches the front-office convention.
    """
    c_curves = raw_ln_dfs.shape[0]
    d_dates = target_dates.shape[0]

    out_grid = np.empty((c_curves, d_dates, num_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)

    if d_dates == 0 or c_curves == 0:
        return out_grid

    interp_linear_on_log_values_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
        raw_tenors,
        raw_ln_dfs,
        target_dates,
        out_grid,
    )
    return out_grid


# ==============================================================================
# VOL SURFACES
# ==============================================================================


def _build_vol_surfaces(
    *,
    raw_vol_surface_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    surface_interp_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    surface_extrap_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    target_dates: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    num_scenarios: int,
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Time-slice every vol surface onto the portfolio's unique-date axis
    and emit the canonical kernel-facing layout.

    Input  : `raw_vol_surface_values` is `(C_surf, M_tenors, K_strikes, S_scn)`.
    Output : `(C_surf, D_dates, S_scn, K_strikes)` — pillar in trailing axis.

    Pre-calibration contract
    ------------------------
    `build_market_data_matrix` calibrates DELTA-quoted surfaces to
    MONEYNESS_FWD upstream via `delta_to_moneyness.calibrate_delta_
    surfaces_to_moneyness`, which rewrites both `raw_vol_surface_
    strikes` and `surface_y_axis_type_ids`. By the time this function
    runs, no DELTA_CALL rows remain — the kernel's `_Y_KIND_MONEYNESS`
    dispatch path consumes them transparently.
    """
    c_surf = raw_vol_surface_tenors.shape[0] if raw_vol_surface_tenors.ndim > 0 else 0
    k_strikes = (
        raw_vol_surface_strikes.shape[1] if raw_vol_surface_strikes.ndim > 1 else 0
    )
    d_dates = target_dates.shape[0]

    if d_dates == 0 or raw_vol_surface_values.size == 0 or c_surf == 0:
        return np.empty((c_surf, d_dates, num_scenarios, k_strikes), dtype=np.float64)

    c_in, m_tenors, k_in, s_in = raw_vol_surface_values.shape
    if c_in != c_surf or k_in != k_strikes or s_in != num_scenarios:
        raise ValueError(
            f"raw_vol_surface_values shape {raw_vol_surface_values.shape} is "
            f"inconsistent with tenors/strikes/scenarios "
            f"({c_surf}, {m_tenors}, {k_strikes}, {num_scenarios})."
        )

    # Step 1. Fold (pillar, scenario) into one trailing axis and feed
    # the time-interp kernels as a 3-D (c, m_tenors, k * s) array.
    flat_in = raw_vol_surface_values.reshape(
        c_surf, m_tenors, k_strikes * num_scenarios
    )
    time_sliced_flat = _build_price_curve_grid(
        raw_tenors=raw_vol_surface_tenors,
        raw_values=np.ascontiguousarray(flat_in),
        target_dates=target_dates,
        interp_methods=surface_interp_x_methods,
        extrap_methods=surface_extrap_x_methods,
        num_scenarios=k_strikes * num_scenarios,
    )

    # Step 2. Reshape back to (c, d, k, s) and swap to land in the kernel-
    # facing (c, d, s, k) layout. Force C-contiguous because the kernel
    # signature pins `[:, :, :, ::1]`.
    canonical = time_sliced_flat.reshape(
        c_surf, d_dates, k_strikes, num_scenarios
    ).swapaxes(2, 3)
    return np.ascontiguousarray(canonical)


# ==============================================================================
# TOP-LEVEL ENTRY POINT
# ==============================================================================


def build_market_data_matrix(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    uix_mapper: UixMapper,
    num_scenarios: int,
    # ---- Raw scalar matrices (F/scalar-nodes, scenarios) ----
    raw_fx_spots: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_scalar_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    scalar_node_uix_in_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    # ---- Curve configs ----
    raw_discount_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_discount_ln_dfs: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_price_curve_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_price_curve_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    curve_interp_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    curve_extrap_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    price_curve_uix_in_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    # ---- Surface configs ----
    raw_vol_surface_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    surface_interp_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    surface_extrap_x_methods: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    surface_y_axis_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
) -> MarketDataMatrix:
    """
    Assemble a frozen `MarketDataMatrix` aligned to the run's UIX universes.

    Parameters
    ----------
    uix_mapper
        Source of truth for the FX-effective pair table and the unique-
        date axis. Built once per run by `build_uix_mapper`.

    Returns
    -------
    MarketDataMatrix
        Valuation-ready container; pricers consume directly.
    """
    unique_dates_ord32 = uix_mapper.date_ord32_universe
    d_dates = unique_dates_ord32.shape[0]

    # ---- 0. Scalar-node levels (universe-aligned, item C / A1) -----------
    # One backing array indexed by `market_id_universe`; prices / forward_
    # nodes / vol_nodes alias it (disjoint id classes, each pricer reads
    # only its own rows).
    scalar_levels = _build_scalar_node_levels(
        raw_scalar_nodes=raw_scalar_nodes,
        scalar_node_uix_in_universe=scalar_node_uix_in_universe,
        num_market_ids=uix_mapper.market_id_universe.shape[0],
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    # ---- 1. FX effective matrix ------------------------------------------
    fx_spots_effective = _build_fx_spots_effective(
        raw_fx_spots=raw_fx_spots,
        fx_effective_pairs=uix_mapper.fx_effective_pairs,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    # ---- 2. Discount factor grid -----------------------------------------
    discount_factor_grid = _build_discount_factor_grid(
        raw_tenors=raw_discount_tenors,
        raw_ln_dfs=raw_discount_ln_dfs,
        target_dates=unique_dates_ord32,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    # ---- 3. Price curve grid (universe-aligned, item C / A1 for curves) --
    dense_price_curve_grid = _build_price_curve_grid(
        raw_tenors=raw_price_curve_tenors,
        raw_values=raw_price_curve_values,
        target_dates=unique_dates_ord32,
        interp_methods=curve_interp_methods,
        extrap_methods=curve_extrap_methods,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )
    price_curve_grid = _scatter_price_curve_grid_to_universe(
        dense_grid=dense_price_curve_grid,
        price_curve_uix_in_universe=price_curve_uix_in_universe,
        num_market_ids=uix_mapper.market_id_universe.shape[0],
    )

    # ---- 4. TTE grid (act/365.25) ----------------------------------------
    as_of_ord32 = date_to_ord32(as_of_date)
    if d_dates == 0:
        tte_grid = np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        tte_grid = np.maximum(
            (unique_dates_ord32.astype(np.float64) - float(as_of_ord32))
            / DAYS_PER_YR_ACT_365_25,
            TTE_FLOOR,
        )

    # ---- 5. Vol surfaces ------------------------------------------------
    #
    # Step 5a: DELTA -> MONEYNESS_FWD calibration. The calibrator
    # rewrites `raw_vol_surface_strikes` rows whose y-axis-type is
    # DELTA_CALL; non-DELTA rows pass through untouched. The
    # `surface_y_axis_type_ids_calibrated` array is the post-
    # calibration per-surface view: DELTA_CALL slots are rewritten to
    # MONEYNESS_FWD, non-DELTA slots are unchanged. This is the
    # runtime per-surface diagnostic counterpart to the per-trade
    # view on IngestBatch (already MONEYNESS_FWD post-ingest via
    # `surface_y_axis_kind_for_kernel`). Both views agree by
    # construction.
    (
        calibrated_strikes,
        surface_y_axis_type_ids_calibrated,
    ) = calibrate_delta_surfaces_to_moneyness(
        raw_vol_surface_strikes=raw_vol_surface_strikes,
        raw_vol_surface_tenors=raw_vol_surface_tenors,
        raw_vol_surface_values=raw_vol_surface_values,
        surface_y_axis_type_ids=surface_y_axis_type_ids,
    )

    # Step 5b: time-slice onto the unique-date axis. By this point no
    # DELTA_CALL rows remain — `_build_vol_surfaces` no longer needs
    # the y-axis-type ids argument.
    vol_surfaces = _build_vol_surfaces(
        raw_vol_surface_tenors=raw_vol_surface_tenors,
        raw_vol_surface_strikes=calibrated_strikes,
        raw_vol_surface_values=raw_vol_surface_values,
        surface_interp_x_methods=surface_interp_x_methods,
        surface_extrap_x_methods=surface_extrap_x_methods,
        target_dates=unique_dates_ord32,
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
    )

    return MarketDataMatrix(
        num_scenarios=num_scenarios,
        fx_spots_effective=fx_spots_effective,
        prices=scalar_levels,
        forward_nodes=scalar_levels,
        vol_nodes=scalar_levels,
        discount_factor_grid=discount_factor_grid,
        price_curve_grid=price_curve_grid,
        tte_grid=tte_grid,
        vol_surface_strikes=calibrated_strikes,
        vol_surfaces=vol_surfaces,
        surface_y_axis_type_ids=surface_y_axis_type_ids_calibrated,
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/calibrate/implied_vol/black76/calibrate.py
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.kernels.black76_implied_vol_batch import (
    _kernel_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.master import (
    CalibratedOptionQuotes,
    CanonicalOptionQuotes,
)


def calibrate_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices(
    quotes: CanonicalOptionQuotes,
) -> CalibratedOptionQuotes:
    """
    Calibrate Black-76 implied volatilities from canonical (undiscounted-call)
    option quotes.

    Returns a full-length, index-aligned ``CalibratedOptionQuotes`` master.
    Failed inversions carry the ``IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL`` value and a
    ``False`` entry in ``success_mask``; ``vega`` carries the sensitivity at the
    solved iv (0.0 for failed and at-intrinsic solves) and acts as the natural
    quality weight. See that master for the full contract.
    """
    iv, vega = _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays(
        fwd_price=quotes.fwd_price,
        strike=quotes.strike,
        tte=quotes.tte,
        market_call_price_undiscounted=quotes.undiscounted_call_price,
    )

    # Branchless contract derivation: solved (>= 0.0, incl. the 0.0 at-intrinsic
    # case) vs the negative failure sentinel. Single source of truth is the iv
    # sign; the master re-checks this invariant on construction.
    success_mask = iv >= 0.0

    return CalibratedOptionQuotes(
        fwd_price=quotes.fwd_price,
        strike=quotes.strike,
        tte=quotes.tte,
        iv=iv,
        vega=vega,
        success_mask=success_mask,
    )


def _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays(
    fwd_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    tte: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    market_call_price_undiscounted: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> tuple[npt.NDArray[np.float64], npt.NDArray[np.float64]]:
    """
    Array-level calibration entry point.

    Returns ``(iv, vega)``, both length ``n_options``. Retained as the raw-array
    seam for isolated NumPy/SciPy parity tests of the kernel; production callers
    go through ``calibrate_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices``.
    """
    fwd_price = np.ascontiguousarray(fwd_price, dtype=np.float64)
    strike = np.ascontiguousarray(strike, dtype=np.float64)
    tte = np.ascontiguousarray(tte, dtype=np.float64)
    market_call_price_undiscounted = np.ascontiguousarray(
        market_call_price_undiscounted, dtype=np.float64
    )

    n_options = fwd_price.shape[0]
    iv_out = np.empty(n_options, dtype=np.float64)
    vega_out = np.empty(n_options, dtype=np.float64)

    _kernel_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices(
        fwd_price,
        strike,
        tte,
        market_call_price_undiscounted,
        iv_out,
        vega_out,
    )

    return iv_out, vega_out
```

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/calibrate/implied_vol/black76/kernels/black76_implied_vol_batch.py
"""
This module contains the batch-processing Numba-jitted kernels for solving for Black-76
implied volatilities from large arrays of option market data, including undiscounted calls
and mixed calls/puts via Put-Call parity. The core root-solving logic is implemented in
the `black76_implied_vol_scalar` module.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb
import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.kernels.black76_implied_vol_scalar import (
    _solver_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices_brent,
)
from riskquant.numba import _F64_1D, parallel_njit
from riskquant.valuation.pricing_models.black76.model import _black76_fv_vega


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_1D,  # forward price
        _F64_1D,  # strike
        _F64_1D,  # time to expiry
        _F64_1D,  # market price
        _F64_1D,  # output array for implied vol
        _F64_1D,  # output array for vega at the solved iv
    )
)
def _kernel_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices(
    fwd_price,  # forward price (n_options,)
    strike,  # strike price (n_options,)
    tte,  # time to expiry (n_options,)
    market_call_price_undiscounted,  # market price (n_options,)
    iv_out,  # output array for implied vol (n_options,)
    vega_out,  # output array for vega at solved iv (n_options,)
):
    n_options = fwd_price.shape[0]

    for i in nb.prange(n_options):
        # Solve using brent's method, which is robust and fast for 1D root-finding
        # (inline numba kernel with no call overhead).
        iv = _solver_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices_brent(
            fwd_price[i],
            strike[i],
            tte[i],
            market_call_price_undiscounted[i],
        )
        iv_out[i] = iv

        # Fused vega evaluation at the solved iv (reuses the same band of memory,
        # no second pass). The helper returns 0.0 for the failure sentinel
        # (iv < 0) and for at-intrinsic zero-vol solves (iv == 0).
        vega_out[i] = _black76_fv_vega(
            fwd=fwd_price[i],
            strike=strike[i],
            sqrt_tte=np.sqrt(tte[i]),
            half_tte=0.5 * tte[i],
            vol=iv,
        )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/calibrate/implied_vol/black76/kernels/black76_implied_vol_scalar.py
"""
This module contains the core logic for solving for Black-76 implied volatilities
for individual options, implemented as Numba-jitted functions for maximum performance.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

from riskquant.constants import IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL
from riskquant.numba import inline_njit
from riskquant.valuation.pricing_models.black76.model import _black76_fv_call

_EPSILON = np.finfo(np.float64).eps

_DEFAULT_MAX_ITER = 100

_DEFAULT_ROOT_TOL = 1e-8
_LOWER_VOL = 1e-8
_UPPER_VOL = 10.0


@inline_njit
def _solver_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices_brent(
    fwd: float,
    strike: float,
    tte: float,
    target_call_price_undiscounted: float,
) -> float:
    # -1. Bind
    _iv_solver_failed = IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL

    # 0. Fail fast
    fwd_price_tol = 1e-12 * max(fwd, 1.0)
    if target_call_price_undiscounted > fwd + fwd_price_tol:
        return _iv_solver_failed
    if tte <= 0.0:
        return _iv_solver_failed
    if fwd <= 0.0:
        return _iv_solver_failed
    if strike <= 0.0:
        return _iv_solver_failed

    sqrt_tte = np.sqrt(tte)
    half_tte = 0.5 * tte

    # 1. Intrinsic value check
    intrinsic = max(fwd - strike, 0.0)
    intrinsic_tol = 1e-12 * max(fwd, strike, 1.0)
    if target_call_price_undiscounted < intrinsic - intrinsic_tol:
        return _iv_solver_failed
    if target_call_price_undiscounted <= intrinsic + intrinsic_tol:
        return 0.0

    # 2. Bracket Initialization
    a = _LOWER_VOL
    b = _UPPER_VOL
    fa = (
        _black76_fv_call(
            fwd=fwd,
            strike=strike,
            sqrt_tte=sqrt_tte,
            half_tte=half_tte,
            vol=a,
        )
        - target_call_price_undiscounted
    )
    fb = (
        _black76_fv_call(
            fwd=fwd,
            strike=strike,
            sqrt_tte=sqrt_tte,
            half_tte=half_tte,
            vol=b,
        )
        - target_call_price_undiscounted
    )

    if fa == 0.0:
        return a
    if fb == 0.0:
        return b
    if fa * fb > 0.0:
        return _iv_solver_failed

    if abs(fa) < abs(fb):  # Ensure b is the best guess
        a, b = b, a
        fa, fb = fb, fa

    c = a
    fc = fa
    d = b - a
    e = d

    eps = _EPSILON

    max_iter = _DEFAULT_MAX_ITER
    vol_tol = _DEFAULT_ROOT_TOL

    # 3. Brent's Iteration Loop
    for _ in range(max_iter):
        # Always maintain 'b' as the best guess.
        #
        # NR zbrent rotation, executed sequentially: a=b, b=c, c=a. Because the
        # trailing ``c = a`` runs AFTER ``a = b``, it resolves to the OLD b, so
        # 'a' and 'c' both take the previous best point. Preserving the OLD a in
        # 'c' instead (the prior bug) silently aliases 'b' and 'c' in the
        # re-bracketed state, yielding xm == 0 and a false convergence that
        # returns a non-repricing vol (often the lower bracket bound).
        if abs(fc) < abs(fb):
            a = b
            fa = fb

            b = c
            fb = fc

            c = a
            fc = fa

        xm = 0.5 * (c - b)
        vol_tol1 = 2.0 * eps * abs(b) + 0.5 * vol_tol

        # Convergence check
        if abs(xm) <= vol_tol1 or fb == 0.0:
            return float(b)

        # Attempt Secant or Inverse Quadratic Interpolation
        if abs(e) >= vol_tol1 and abs(fa) > abs(fb):
            s = fb / fa
            if a == c:
                # Secant method (only two distinct points available)
                p = 2.0 * xm * s
                q = 1.0 - s
            else:
                # Inverse quadratic interpolation (three distinct points)
                q = fa / fc
                r = fb / fc
                p = s * (2.0 * xm * q * (q - r) - (b - a) * (r - 1.0))
                q = (q - 1.0) * (r - 1.0) * (s - 1.0)

            if p > 0.0:
                q = -q
            p = abs(p)

            # Check if interpolation is acceptable and within bounds
            min1 = 3.0 * xm * q - abs(vol_tol1 * q)
            min2 = abs(e * q)
            if 2.0 * p < (min1 if min1 < min2 else min2):
                e = d
                d = p / q
            else:
                # Interpolation failed, fallback to bisection
                d = xm
                e = d
        else:
            # Bounds tightening too slow, fallback to bisection
            d = xm
            e = d

        a = b
        fa = fb

        # Advance the best guess
        if abs(d) > vol_tol1:
            b += d
        else:
            b += vol_tol1 if xm >= 0.0 else -vol_tol1

        b = float(b)

        # Evaluate the new point
        fb = (
            _black76_fv_call(
                fwd=fwd,
                strike=strike,
                sqrt_tte=sqrt_tte,
                half_tte=half_tte,
                vol=b,
            )
            - target_call_price_undiscounted
        )

        # Re-bracket the root
        if (fb > 0.0 and fc > 0.0) or (fb < 0.0 and fc < 0.0):
            c = a
            fc = fa
            d = b - a
            e = d

    # Failed to converge within max_iter
    return _iv_solver_failed
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/calibrate/option_quotes/compile.py
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.kernels.compile_canonical_quotes import (
    _kernel_compute_undiscounted_call_price,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.master import (
    CanonicalOptionQuotes,
    ValidatedOptionQuotes,
)


def compile_canonical_option_quotes(
    quotes: ValidatedOptionQuotes,
) -> CanonicalOptionQuotes:
    """
    Compile validated option market quotes into the canonical
    calibration representation.

    Input
    -----
    ValidatedOptionQuotes

    Output
    ------
    CanonicalOptionQuotes (F, K, T, C_undiscounted_call) suitable for
    implied-volatility calibration.

    The master is already normalized (dtype + contiguity) and shape-checked by
    ``validate_option_quotes`` and ``ValidatedOptionQuotes.__post_init__``, so
    this path trusts it: no re-cast, no re-validation.
    """
    return _compile_to_canonical(
        fwd_price=quotes.fwd_price,
        strike=quotes.strike,
        tte=quotes.tte,
        call_put_flag=quotes.call_put_flag,
        discount_factor=quotes.discount_factor,
        option_price=quotes.option_price,
    )


def _compile_canonical_option_quotes_arrays(
    fwd_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    tte: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    call_put_flag: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    discount_factor: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    option_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> CanonicalOptionQuotes:
    """
    Raw-array compilation seam.

    Normalizes dtype/layout and shape-checks before compiling. Retained for
    direct/raw callers and isolated kernel tests; production callers go through
    ``compile_canonical_option_quotes``, which trusts an already-validated
    master and skips this normalization.
    """
    fwd_price = np.ascontiguousarray(fwd_price, dtype=np.float64)
    strike = np.ascontiguousarray(strike, dtype=np.float64)
    tte = np.ascontiguousarray(tte, dtype=np.float64)
    call_put_flag = np.ascontiguousarray(call_put_flag, dtype=np.int8)
    discount_factor = np.ascontiguousarray(discount_factor, dtype=np.float64)
    option_price = np.ascontiguousarray(option_price, dtype=np.float64)

    n_quotes = fwd_price.shape[0]

    for name, array in (
        ("strike", strike),
        ("tte", tte),
        ("call_put_flag", call_put_flag),
        ("discount_factor", discount_factor),
        ("option_price", option_price),
    ):
        if array.shape[0] != n_quotes:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{name} array length ({array.shape[0]}) does not match fwd_price ({n_quotes})"
            )

    return _compile_to_canonical(
        fwd_price=fwd_price,
        strike=strike,
        tte=tte,
        call_put_flag=call_put_flag,
        discount_factor=discount_factor,
        option_price=option_price,
    )


def _compile_to_canonical(
    fwd_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    tte: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    call_put_flag: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    discount_factor: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    option_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> CanonicalOptionQuotes:
    """
    Trusted compilation core: assumes inputs are already normalized and
    shape-consistent. Allocates the output band, runs the put-call-parity /
    undiscounting kernel, and constructs the canonical master.
    """
    n_quotes = fwd_price.shape[0]
    undiscounted_call_price = np.empty(n_quotes, dtype=np.float64)

    _kernel_compute_undiscounted_call_price(
        fwd_price,
        strike,
        call_put_flag,
        discount_factor,
        option_price,
        undiscounted_call_price,
    )

    return CanonicalOptionQuotes(
        fwd_price=fwd_price,
        strike=strike,
        tte=tte,
        undiscounted_call_price=undiscounted_call_price,
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\calibrate\option_quotes\kernels\compile_canonical_quotes.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/calibrate/option_quotes/kernels/compile_canonical_quotes.py

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb

from riskquant.numba import _F64_1D, _I8_1D, parallel_njit


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        _F64_1D,  # forward price
        _F64_1D,  # strike
        _I8_1D,  # call_put_flag (1=call, 0=put)
        _F64_1D,  # discount factor
        _F64_1D,  # option price
        _F64_1D,  # undiscounted call price out
    )
)
def _kernel_compute_undiscounted_call_price(
    fwd_price,
    strike,
    call_put_flag,
    discount_factor,
    option_price,
    undiscounted_call_price_out,
):
    """
    Compile validated market option quotes into canonical undiscounted
    call-price representation.

    Contract
    --------
    Inputs are assumed valid.

    call_put_flag:
        1 = call
        0 = put

    Output
    ------
    undiscounted_call_price_out[i]

    Transformations
    ---------------
    Call:
        C = option_price / df

    Put:
        C = option_price / df + F - K
    """

    n_quotes = fwd_price.shape[0]

    for i in nb.prange(n_quotes):
        undiscounted_price = option_price[i] / discount_factor[i]

        if call_put_flag[i]:
            undiscounted_call_price_out[i] = undiscounted_price
        else:
            undiscounted_call_price_out[i] = (
                undiscounted_price + fwd_price[i] - strike[i]
            )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/calibrate/option_quotes/master.py
"""
This module defines the contract for calibrating implied volatilities from raw option
market data."""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import MIN_RELIABLE_VEGA_OVER_FWD


@dataclass(slots=True, frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class ValidatedOptionQuotes:
    fwd_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    tte: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    call_put_flag: npt.NDArray[np.int8]
    discount_factor: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    option_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        n = self.fwd_price.shape[0]

        if self.strike.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("strike length mismatch")

        if self.tte.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("tte length mismatch")

        if self.call_put_flag.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("call_put_flag length mismatch")

        if self.discount_factor.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("discount_factor length mismatch")

        if self.option_price.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("option_price length mismatch")

    @property
    def n_quotes(self) -> int:
        return self.fwd_price.shape[0]

    def __repr__(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.__class__.__name__}(n_quotes={self.n_quotes:,})"

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.to_string()

    def to_string(self, max_rows: int = 10) -> str:
        """
        Return a human-readable preview of the validated option quotes.

        Only the first ``max_rows`` rows are displayed.
        """

        n_quotes = self.n_quotes
        n_display = min(n_quotes, max_rows)

        lines: list[str] = [
            (f"{self.__class__.__name__}(n_quotes={n_quotes:,})"),
            "",
            (
                f"{'Row':>8} | {'Fwd':>14} | {'Strike':>14} | {'TTE':>12} | "
                f"{'C/P':>4} | {'DF':>10} | {'Price':>14}"
            ),
            "-" * 94,
        ]

        for i in range(n_display):
            cp = "C" if self.call_put_flag[i] == 1 else "P"
            lines.append(
                f"{i:8d} | "
                f"{self.fwd_price[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{self.strike[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{self.tte[i]:12.6f} | "
                f"{cp:>4} | "
                f"{self.discount_factor[i]:10.6f} | "
                f"{self.option_price[i]:14.6f}"
            )

        if n_quotes > n_display:
            lines.append("-" * 94)
            lines.append(f"... {n_quotes - n_display:,} additional rows not shown")

        return "\n".join(lines)


@dataclass(slots=True, frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class CanonicalOptionQuotes:
    fwd_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    tte: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    undiscounted_call_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        n = self.fwd_price.shape[0]

        if self.strike.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("strike length mismatch")

        if self.tte.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("tte length mismatch")

        if self.undiscounted_call_price.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("undiscounted_call_price length mismatch")

    @property
    def n_quotes(self) -> int:
        return self.fwd_price.shape[0]

    def __repr__(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.__class__.__name__}(n_quotes={self.fwd_price.shape[0]:,})"

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.to_string()

    def to_string(self, max_rows: int = 10) -> str:
        """
        Return a human-readable preview of the canonical option quotes.

        Only the first ``max_rows`` rows are displayed.
        """

        n_quotes = self.fwd_price.shape[0]
        n_display = min(n_quotes, max_rows)

        lines: list[str] = [
            (f"{self.__class__.__name__}(n_quotes={n_quotes:,})"),
            "",
            (
                f"{'Row':>8} | {'Fwd':>14} | {'Strike':>14} | {'TTE':>12} | {'Undisc Call':>14}"
            ),
            "-" * 74,
        ]

        for i in range(n_display):
            lines.append(
                f"{i:8d} | "
                f"{self.fwd_price[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{self.strike[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{self.tte[i]:12.6f} | "
                f"{self.undiscounted_call_price[i]:14.6f}"
            )

        if n_quotes > n_display:
            lines.append("-" * 74)
            lines.append(f"... {n_quotes - n_display:,} additional rows not shown")

        return "\n".join(lines)


@dataclass(slots=True, frozen=True, kw_only=True)
class CalibratedOptionQuotes:
    """
    Calibrated Black-76 implied volatilities, index-aligned to the canonical
    quotes they were solved from.

    Contract
    --------
    The master is *full-length* and never compacted: ``fwd_price``, ``strike``,
    ``tte``, ``iv``, ``vega`` and ``success_mask`` share one index, preserving
    the join back to the originating quotes (and, downstream, to instrument
    identity).

    Failure channel (decision (a)):
        - ``success_mask[i]`` is the contract surface. ``True`` => solved.
        - ``iv[i] == IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL`` (negative) marks a failed
          inversion.
        - ``iv[i] == 0.0`` is a *valid* at-intrinsic zero-vol solve, not a
          failure.
        - The invariant ``success_mask == (iv >= 0.0)`` is enforced at
          construction.

    Quality channel:
        - ``vega[i]`` is dC/dvol at the solved iv (undiscounted Black-76).
        - It is the natural quality weight: 0.0 for failed solves and for
          at-intrinsic zero-vol solves, tiny in flat-vega regions where the iv
          is under-determined, large where the inversion is well-conditioned.
        - A solved iv with near-zero vega reprices its target but does not pin
          down the vol; weight residuals by ``vega`` (or gate via
          ``reliable_mask``) when fitting a surface.

    The mask is intentionally denormalized against the iv sign so consumers gate
    with a branchless ``iv[success_mask]`` instead of re-deriving the convention.
    """

    fwd_price: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    strike: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    tte: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    iv: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    vega: npt.NDArray[np.float64]
    success_mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        n = self.fwd_price.shape[0]

        if self.strike.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("strike length mismatch")

        if self.tte.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("tte length mismatch")

        if self.iv.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("iv length mismatch")

        if self.vega.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("vega length mismatch")

        if self.success_mask.shape[0] != n:
            raise ValueError("success_mask length mismatch")

        # NaN must never enter the output: that is the whole point of rejecting
        # the in-band-NaN channel in favour of an explicit sentinel + mask.
        if np.isnan(self.iv).any():
            raise ValueError(
                "iv contains NaN; failed solves must use the "
                "IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL value, not NaN"
            )

        # success_mask is the named contract; enforce it against the iv sign so a
        # mask that disagrees with the sentinel can never be silently constructed.
        if not np.array_equal(self.success_mask, self.iv >= 0.0):
            raise ValueError(
                "success_mask inconsistent with iv sign contract "
                "(failed solves must be the negative sentinel)"
            )

        # Vega is a sensitivity magnitude: non-negative, finite, and exactly 0
        # wherever the solve failed. Note the converse does NOT hold: a *solved*
        # at-intrinsic (iv == 0.0) quote also carries zero vega, which is correct.
        if np.isnan(self.vega).any():
            raise ValueError("vega contains NaN")

        if np.any(self.vega < 0.0):
            raise ValueError("vega must be non-negative")

        if not np.all(self.vega[~self.success_mask] == 0.0):
            raise ValueError("failed solves must carry zero vega")

    @property
    def n_quotes(self) -> int:
        return self.fwd_price.shape[0]

    @property
    def n_solved(self) -> int:
        return int(np.count_nonzero(self.success_mask))

    @property
    def n_failed(self) -> int:
        return self.n_quotes - self.n_solved

    def reliable_mask(
        self, min_vega_over_fwd: float = MIN_RELIABLE_VEGA_OVER_FWD
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.bool_]:
        """
        Solved quotes whose vega is large enough for the iv to be well-determined.

        ``reliable = success_mask & (vega >= min_vega_over_fwd * fwd_price)``.

        The vega floor is scaled by forward so the gate is dimensionless across
        notionals. The default is a heuristic; pass an explicit floor to tune.
        Returns a fresh boolean array.
        """
        return self.success_mask & (self.vega >= min_vega_over_fwd * self.fwd_price)

    def __repr__(self) -> str:
        return (
            f"{self.__class__.__name__}("
            f"n_quotes={self.n_quotes:,}, "
            f"n_solved={self.n_solved:,}, "
            f"n_failed={self.n_failed:,})"
        )

    def __str__(self) -> str:
        return self.to_string()

    def to_string(self, max_rows: int = 10) -> str:
        """
        Return a human-readable preview of the calibrated quotes.

        Only the first ``max_rows`` rows are displayed.
        """

        n_quotes = self.n_quotes
        n_display = min(n_quotes, max_rows)

        lines: list[str] = [
            (
                f"{self.__class__.__name__}(n_quotes={n_quotes:,}, "
                f"n_solved={self.n_solved:,}, n_failed={self.n_failed:,})"
            ),
            "",
            (
                f"{'Row':>8} | {'Fwd':>14} | {'Strike':>14} | "
                f"{'TTE':>12} | {'IV':>14} | {'Vega':>14} | {'Status':>8}"
            ),
            "-" * 101,
        ]

        for i in range(n_display):
            status = "ok" if self.success_mask[i] else "FAILED"
            lines.append(
                f"{i:8d} | "
                f"{self.fwd_price[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{self.strike[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{self.tte[i]:12.6f} | "
                f"{self.iv[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{self.vega[i]:14.6f} | "
                f"{status:>8}"
            )

        if n_quotes > n_display:
            lines.append("-" * 101)
            lines.append(f"... {n_quotes - n_display:,} additional rows not shown")

        return "\n".join(lines)
```

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/calibrate/option_quotes/validate.py
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.master import (
    ValidatedOptionQuotes,
)

#: Max offending row indices shown in a validation error message.
_PREVIEW = 10

#: Relative tolerance on the no-arbitrage bounds. Strictly a float-noise guard
#: (it absorbs rounding in df * bound), not market bid/ask slack -- validate is
#: the hard edge; dirty feeds must be cleaned upstream.
_ARB_REL_TOL = 1e-9


def _format_violation(message: str, mask: npt.NDArray[np.bool_]) -> str:
    """Append a count and a bounded preview of offending row indices."""
    idx = np.flatnonzero(mask)
    head = ", ".join(str(int(i)) for i in idx[:_PREVIEW])
    more = "" if idx.size <= _PREVIEW else f", ... (+{idx.size - _PREVIEW} more)"
    return f"{message}: {idx.size} quote(s) at rows [{head}{more}]"


def validate_option_quotes(
    fwd_price: npt.ArrayLike,
    strike: npt.ArrayLike,
    tte: npt.ArrayLike,
    call_put_flag: npt.ArrayLike,
    discount_factor: npt.ArrayLike,
    option_price: npt.ArrayLike,
) -> ValidatedOptionQuotes:
    """
    Validate and normalize market option quotes.

    Parameters
    ----------
    fwd_price
        Forward prices.

    strike
        Option strikes.

    tte
        Time-to-expiry in years.

    call_put_flag
        Option type flags.

            1 = call
            0 = put

    discount_factor
        Discount factors.

    option_price
        Discounted market option prices.

    Returns
    -------
    ValidatedOptionQuotes

    Notes
    -----
    This function performs:

        - dtype normalization
        - contiguous array normalization
        - shape validation
        - domain validation
        - no-arbitrage bound validation

    It does NOT perform:

        - put-call parity transformations
        - undiscounting
        - implied volatility calibration
    """

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Normalize dtypes and memory layout
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    fwd_price = np.ascontiguousarray(fwd_price, dtype=np.float64)
    strike = np.ascontiguousarray(strike, dtype=np.float64)
    tte = np.ascontiguousarray(tte, dtype=np.float64)

    call_put_flag = np.ascontiguousarray(
        call_put_flag,
        dtype=np.int8,
    )

    discount_factor = np.ascontiguousarray(
        discount_factor,
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    option_price = np.ascontiguousarray(
        option_price,
        dtype=np.float64,
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Shape validation
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    n_quotes = fwd_price.shape[0]

    for name, array in (
        ("strike", strike),
        ("tte", tte),
        ("call_put_flag", call_put_flag),
        ("discount_factor", discount_factor),
        ("option_price", option_price),
    ):
        if array.shape[0] != n_quotes:
            raise ValueError(
                f"{name} length ({array.shape[0]}) "
                f"does not match fwd_price length ({n_quotes})"
            )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Domain validation
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    if np.any(fwd_price <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("fwd_price must be strictly positive")

    if np.any(strike <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("strike must be strictly positive")

    if np.any(tte <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("tte must be strictly positive")

    if np.any(discount_factor <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("discount_factor must be strictly positive")

    if np.any(option_price < 0.0):
        raise ValueError("option_price cannot be negative")

    invalid_flags = (call_put_flag != 0) & (call_put_flag != 1)

    if np.any(invalid_flags):
        raise ValueError("call_put_flag must contain only 0 (put) or 1 (call)")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # No-arbitrage bound validation
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    #
    # Model-free European bounds on the *discounted* price, per side:
    #     call:  df * max(F - K, 0) <= c <= df * F
    #     put:   df * max(K - F, 0) <= p <= df * K
    # (undiscounted call in [intrinsic, F]; undiscounted put in [intrinsic, K];
    # scale by df). A breach is a broken feed, not dirty data, so we reject
    # loudly here rather than letting it surface as an opaque failed solve.

    is_call = call_put_flag == 1

    intrinsic_undisc = np.where(
        is_call,
        np.maximum(fwd_price - strike, 0.0),
        np.maximum(strike - fwd_price, 0.0),
    )
    lower_bound = discount_factor * intrinsic_undisc
    upper_bound = discount_factor * np.where(is_call, fwd_price, strike)

    arb_tol = _ARB_REL_TOL * np.maximum(upper_bound, 1.0)

    below_lower = option_price < lower_bound - arb_tol
    if np.any(below_lower):
        raise ValueError(
            _format_violation(
                "option_price below discounted intrinsic (lower no-arbitrage bound)",
                below_lower,
            )
        )

    above_upper = option_price > upper_bound + arb_tol
    if np.any(above_upper):
        raise ValueError(
            _format_violation(
                "option_price above discounted forward/strike "
                "(upper no-arbitrage bound)",
                above_upper,
            )
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Contract construction
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    return ValidatedOptionQuotes(
        fwd_price=fwd_price,
        strike=strike,
        tte=tte,
        call_put_flag=call_put_flag,
        discount_factor=discount_factor,
        option_price=option_price,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\curves\extrap_methods.py

from __future__ import annotations

from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class ExtrapMethod(IntEnum):
    FLAT = 1
    LINEAR = 2


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
EXTRAP_FLAT: np.int8 = np.int8(ExtrapMethod.FLAT)
EXTRAP_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(ExtrapMethod.LINEAR)


# Extrap methods
EXTRAP_METHOD_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in ExtrapMethod
)

# Central registry
EXTRAP_METHOD_REGISTRY: dict[str, ExtrapMethod] = {
    "LINEAR": ExtrapMethod.LINEAR,
    "FLAT": ExtrapMethod.FLAT,
}


def validate_extrapolation_methods_dict(
    extrapolation_methods_dict: dict[str, int],
) -> None:
    expected = {
        name: int(member) for name, member in ExtrapMethod.__members__.items()
    }
    got = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in extrapolation_methods_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "extrapolation_methods sheet does not match riskquant ExtrapMethod enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel extrapolation_methods table or update riskquant.market_data.curves.extrap_methods.ExtrapMethod."
    )
```

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\curves\interp_defs.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/curves/interp_defs.py
"""
INTERP DEFS

Interpolation kernels used by RiskQuant.

Input conventions
-----------------

All kernels operate directly on the values supplied in the ``values``
array.

For kernels operating on log values:

    * ``log_values`` MUST already contain logarithms of the quantity
      being interpolated.
    * The kernel interpolates linearly in log-space.
    * The result is transformed back to original space via ``exp()``.

Example
-------

To interpolate discount factors log-linearly:

    logged_dfs = np.log(discount_factors)

    interp_linear_on_log_values_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
        tenors,
        logged_dfs,
        targets,
        out,
    )

Passing raw discount factors to a LOG kernel will produce incorrect
results.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

import numba as nb
import numpy as np

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _F64_3D,
    parallel_njit,
)

# --- HELPERS ---

_NB_VOID_3D = nb.void(
    _F64_2D,  # tenors
    _F64_3D,  # values
    _F64_1D,  # targets
    _F64_3D,  # out
)

_NB_VOID_2D = nb.void(
    _F64_2D,  # pillars
    _F64_3D,  # values
    _F64_2D,  # targets
    _F64_2D,  # out
)

# ==============================================================================
# --- LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Zero-allocation 3D Linear Interpolator with Flat Extrapolation."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt
            w = min(max(w, 0.0), 1.0)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = values[c, idx1, s]
                out[c, d, s] = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)


# ==============================================================================
# --- LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Zero-allocation 3D Linear Interpolator with Linear Extrapolation."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt
            # LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION: Deliberately do not clamp w

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = values[c, idx1, s]
                out[c, d, s] = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)


# ==============================================================================
# --- LINEAR INTERPOLATION ON LOG VALUES WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_linear_on_log_values_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    log_values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Log-linear interpolation of discount factors.

    Interpolates linearly in log-discount-factor space and then exponentiates.

    If discount factors are represented as

        D(T) = exp(-z(T) * T)

    this corresponds to piecewise-linear interpolation of the
    discount-factor exponent (-z(T)T), not necessarily piecewise-linear
    interpolation of zero rates themselves.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = log_values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt
            w = min(max(w, 0.0), 1.0)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = log_values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = log_values[c, idx1, s]
                ln_interp = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)
                out[c, d, s] = math.exp(ln_interp)


# ==============================================================================
# --- LOG-LINEAR INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_linear_on_log_values_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    log_values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 3D Log-Linear Interpolator with Linear Extrapolation.

    Same code shape as the flat-extrap sibling EXCEPT the weight `w =
    (target - t0) / dt` is not clamped to `[0, 1]`. On targets past
    the last pillar, `w > 1` extrapolates linearly in log-space (i.e.
    the implied zero-rate is extrapolated linearly off the last
    pillar segment, then exponentiated). Inside the pillar range the
    behaviour is identical to the flat-extrap kernel.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = log_values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]

            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), m_pillars - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            t0 = tenors[c, idx0]
            t1 = tenors[c, idx1]

            dt = t1 - t0
            if dt == 0.0:
                dt = 1.0

            w = (target - t0) / dt

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                y0 = log_values[c, idx0, s]
                y1 = log_values[c, idx1, s]
                ln_interp = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)
                out[c, d, s] = math.exp(ln_interp)


# ==============================================================================
# --- STEP LEFT (LOCF) INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_locf_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Zero-allocation 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolator."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]
            # side="right" ensures we find the first index > target.
            # Subtracting 1 gives the index <= target (Last Observation Carried Forward).
            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right") - 1
            idx = min(max(idx, 0), m_pillars - 1)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out[c, d, s] = values[c, idx, s]


# ==============================================================================
# --- STEP LEFT (LOCF) INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_locf_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 3D Step Left (LOCF) Interpolator with linear-extrap label.

    A step function has no segment slope to extrapolate along, so on
    a step function "linear extrapolation" reduces to flat
    extrapolation: the last observed value is carried forward (past
    the last pillar) or backward (before the first). The
    implementation is byte-identical to the flat-extrap kernel; both
    are registered separately in `CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY` for taxonomy
    completeness.

    If a future requirement asks for true slope-extrapolation of a
    piecewise-constant function (e.g. extrap using the slope of the
    last two pillar VALUES rather than the last segment), this is the
    place to fork the implementation.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]
            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="right") - 1
            idx = min(max(idx, 0), m_pillars - 1)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out[c, d, s] = values[c, idx, s]


# ==============================================================================
# --- STEP RIGHT (NOCB) INTERPOLATION WITH FLAT EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_nocb_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """Zero-allocation 3D Step Right (NOCB) Interpolator."""
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]
            # side="left" ensures we find the first index >= target (Next Observation Carried Backward).
            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="left")
            idx = min(max(idx, 0), m_pillars - 1)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out[c, d, s] = values[c, idx, s]


# ==============================================================================
# --- STEP RIGHT (NOCB) INTERPOLATION WITH LINEAR EXTRAPOLATION ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_3D)
def interp_nocb_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel(
    tenors: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 3D Step Right (NOCB) Interpolator with linear-extrap label.

    Mirror of `interp_step_left_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel` — see
    its docstring. A step function has no segment slope, so "linear
    extrapolation" reduces to flat extrapolation; this kernel is
    byte-identical to the flat-extrap step-right sibling and is
    registered separately in `CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY` for taxonomy
    completeness.
    """
    c_curves, m_pillars = tenors.shape
    d_target_dates = targets.shape[0]
    s_scenarios = values.shape[2]

    for c in nb.prange(c_curves):
        for d in range(d_target_dates):
            target = targets[d]
            idx = np.searchsorted(tenors[c], target, side="left")
            idx = min(max(idx, 0), m_pillars - 1)

            for s in range(s_scenarios):
                out[c, d, s] = values[c, idx, s]


# ==============================================================================
# --- 2D SURFACE STRIKE INTERPOLATOR ---
# ==============================================================================


@parallel_njit(_NB_VOID_2D)
def interp_strike_linear_flat_extrap_numba_kernel(
    pillars: np.ndarray,
    values: np.ndarray,
    targets: np.ndarray,
    out: np.ndarray,
) -> None:
    """
    Zero-allocation 2D Surface Strike Interpolator.
    Pre-allocates an (N_trades, S_scenarios) array and resolves exactly.
    """
    n_trades, k_strikes = pillars.shape
    s_scenarios = targets.shape[1]

    for n in nb.prange(n_trades):
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            target = targets[n, s]

            idx = np.searchsorted(pillars[n], target, side="right")
            idx1 = min(max(idx, 1), k_strikes - 1)
            idx0 = idx1 - 1

            x0 = pillars[n, idx0]
            x1 = pillars[n, idx1]

            dx = x1 - x0
            if dx == 0.0:
                dx = 1.0

            w = (target - x0) / dx
            w = min(max(w, 0.0), 1.0)

            y0 = values[n, idx0, s]
            y1 = values[n, idx1, s]

            out[n, s] = y0 + w * (y1 - y0)
```

```python
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Callable

import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.curves.extrap_methods import (
    EXTRAP_FLAT,
    EXTRAP_LINEAR,
)

# Import the fused Numba kernels
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_defs import (
    interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_linear_on_log_values_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,

    interp_locf_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_nocb_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    interp_nocb_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,

# Import your enum constants
from riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_methods import (
    INTERP_LINEAR,
    INTERP_LOCF,
    INTERP_LOG_LINEAR,
    INTERP_NOCB,
)

# ============================================================
# INTERPOLATION DISPATCH REGISTRY
# ============================================================

# Define the strict signature every curve kernel must follow.
# Signature: (tenors, values, targets, out) -> None
InterpKernelFn = Callable[[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray], None]

# Map (Interp Method ID, Extrap Method ID) -> Math Kernel
# We cast the np.int8 enums to pure Python ints for safe, fast dictionary hashing.
CURVE_MATH_REGISTRY: dict[tuple[int, int], InterpKernelFn] = {
    (
        int(INTERP_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_linear_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_linear_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_linear_on_log_values_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOG_LINEAR),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_linear_on_log_values_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOCF),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_locf_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_LOCF),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_locf_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_NOCB),
        int(EXTRAP_FLAT),
    ): interp_nocb_flat_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
    (
        int(INTERP_NOCB),
        int(EXTRAP_LINEAR),
    ): interp_nocb_linear_extrap_3d_numba_kernel,
}
```

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\curves\interp_methods.py

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class InterpMethod(IntEnum):
    LINEAR = 1
    LOG_LINEAR = 2
    LOCF = 3
    NOCB = 4
    CUBIC_SPLINE = 5


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
INTERP_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.LINEAR)
INTERP_LOG_LINEAR: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.LOG_LINEAR)
INTERP_LOCF: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.LOCF)
INTERP_NOCB: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.NOCB)
INTERP_CUBIC_SPLINE: np.int8 = np.int8(InterpMethod.CUBIC_SPLINE)

# Interp methods
INTERP_METHOD_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in InterpMethod
)

# Central registry
INTERP_METHOD_REGISTRY: dict[str, InterpMethod] = {
    "LINEAR": InterpMethod.LINEAR,
    "LOG_LINEAR": InterpMethod.LOG_LINEAR,
    "LOCF": InterpMethod.LOCF,
    "NOCB": InterpMethod.NOCB,
    "CUBIC_SPLINE": InterpMethod.CUBIC_SPLINE,
}


def validate_interpolation_methods_dict(
    interpolation_methods_dict: Mapping[str, int],
) -> None:
    expected = {name: int(member) for name, member in InterpMethod.__members__.items()}
    got = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in interpolation_methods_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "interpolation_methods sheet does not match riskquant InterpolationMethod enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel interpolation_methods table or update riskquant.market_data.curves.interp_methods.InterpMethod."
    )
```
