```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\builder_matrix.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from pandas.api.types import is_datetime64_any_dtype

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.builder_utils import (
    _require_mapping_key,
    _validate_mapping_dict,
)
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.matrix import RiskFactorMatrix
from riskquant.utils.dates import (
    coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp,
    datetime64D_to_ord32,
)

# --- BUILD A RISK FACTOR MATRIX FROM A DATAFRAME


def build_risk_factor_matrix_from_df(
    *,
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    use_int32_dates: bool = True,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
    """Construct a RiskFactorMatrix from a wide pandas DataFrame.

    Parameters
    ----------
    df : pd.DataFrame
        Wide DataFrame with dates on the index and one column per risk factor.
    name_to_id_map : Optional[Mapping[str, int]]
        Optional mapping from column name (string) to integer factor id. If
        omitted, columns are parsed as integers directly.
    use_int32_dates : bool
        If True convert dates to int32 ordinal representation; otherwise
        keep datetime64[D] dtype.
    float_dtype : numpy floating type
        dtype used for the `values` array (default: np.float32).

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorMatrix
        Canonicalized matrix with validated shapes, dtypes and contiguity.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If the index is not date-like, not monotonic increasing, or contains
        duplicates; or if factor id mapping contains errors.

    Notes
    -----
    The function intentionally does not auto-sort the DataFrame rows
    (dates). If the provided index is unsorted we raise an error because
    auto-sorting would require an expensive O(T*N) copy of the values.
    """
    if not isinstance(df, pd.DataFrame):
        raise ValueError("Expected a DataFrame!")

    # Accept a wider set of date-like index types; coerce if needed.
    if not is_datetime64_any_dtype(df.index):
        # Convert in-place where possible; this preserves row order and
        # avoids unnecessary copying of the data matrix.
        df = df.copy(deep=False)
        df.index = pd.to_datetime(df.index, errors="raise")

    # Enforce invariants early with clear errors (and avoid needless allocations)
    if df.index.has_duplicates:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame index (dates) must be unique!")
    if not df.index.is_monotonic_increasing:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame index (dates) must be sorted ascending!")

    # Check for duplicated column labels (factor names); this would cause silent data corruption
    if df.columns.has_duplicates:
        duplicated = df.columns[df.columns.duplicated(keep=False)]
        unique_dupes = duplicated.unique()
        sample = unique_dupes[:10]
        raise ValueError(
            f"DataFrame columns (factor names) must be unique.\n"
            f"Found {len(unique_dupes)} duplicated column(s).\n"
            f"Sample duplicates: {list(sample)}"
        )

    # Dates: obtain a datetime64[D] view and optionally convert to int32 ordinal
    dts_64D = df.index.to_numpy(dtype="datetime64[D]", copy=False)
    dates = datetime64D_to_ord32(dts_64D) if use_int32_dates else dts_64D

    # Columns -> factor_ids: either direct integer parsing or mapping lookup.
    cols = df.columns
    n = cols.size

    if name_to_id_map is None:
        # Parse column labels as integers; will raise on non-integer labels.
        factor_ids = np.fromiter(
            (int(c) for c in cols),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )
    else:
        # Validate mapping once (cheap for small config dicts) and then map.
        _validate_mapping_dict(name_to_id_map)
        factor_ids = np.fromiter(
            (_require_mapping_key(name_to_id_map, str(c)) for c in cols),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )

    # Canonicalize factor axis BEFORE allocating `values` (avoids extra copies).
    if n > 1 and np.any(factor_ids[1:] < factor_ids[:-1]):
        perm = np.argsort(factor_ids, kind="mergesort")
        factor_ids = factor_ids[perm]

        if np.any(factor_ids[1:] == factor_ids[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique!")

        # Reorder the DataFrame columns to match the canonical factor order.
        df = df.iloc[:, perm]
    else:
        if n > 1 and np.any(factor_ids[1:] == factor_ids[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique!")

    # Materialize the numeric matrix as a C-contiguous array with requested dtype.
    values = np.ascontiguousarray(
        df.to_numpy(dtype=float_dtype, copy=True, na_value=np.nan)
    )

    return RiskFactorMatrix(dates=dates, factor_ids=factor_ids, values=values)


# --- BUILD A RISK FACTOR MATRIX DIRECTLY FROM AN XLSX FILE


def build_risk_factor_matrix_from_xlsx(
    *,
    path: str | Path,
    sheet_name: str | int = 0,
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    use_int32_dates: bool = True,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
    as_of_date: pd.Timestamp | None = None,
) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
    """Load a RiskFactorMatrix from an Excel workbook/sheet.

    Parameters
    ----------
    path : str | Path
        Path to the Excel file.
    sheet_name : str | int
        Sheet name or index to read (forwarded to pandas.read_excel).
    name_to_id_map : Optional[Mapping[str, int]]
        Optional mapping for column name -> factor id (same semantics as
        `from_df`).
    use_int32_dates : bool
        If True convert dates to int32 ordinal representation.
    float_dtype : numpy floating type
        dtype used for the `values` array.
    as_of_date : Optional[pd.Timestamp]
        If provided, rows with date > as_of_date are filtered out.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorMatrix
        Canonicalized matrix constructed from the Excel sheet.
    """
    # Read sheet with the first column treated as the index (dates expected).
    df = pd.read_excel(
        str(path),
        sheet_name=sheet_name,
        index_col=0,
    )

    # If empty.
    if df.empty:
        raise ValueError("dataframe is empty - check: {path}")

    # Convert index to datetime if needed (common for Excel imports).
    if not is_datetime64_any_dtype(df.index):
        df.index = pd.to_datetime(df.index, errors="raise")

    # Optional filter by as_of_date; preserves monotonicity if present.
    if as_of_date is not None:
        cutoff = coerce_as_of_date_to_pd_timestamp(as_of_date)
        df = df.loc[df.index <= cutoff]

    # Enforce same sorted/unique contract as `from_df`.

    # Duplicated dates.
    if df.index.has_duplicates:
        duplicated = df.index[df.index.duplicated(keep=False)]
        unique_dupes = duplicated.unique()
        sample = unique_dupes[:10]
        raise ValueError(
            f"Excel sheet index (dates) must be unique.\n"
            f"Found {len(unique_dupes)} duplicated date(s).\n"
            f"Sample duplicates: {list(sample)}"
        )

    # Non-monotonic dates
    if not df.index.is_monotonic_increasing:
        raise ValueError("Excel sheet index (dates) must be sorted ascending!")

    # Reuse the robust `from_df` constructor for the remaining work.
    return build_risk_factor_matrix_from_df(
        df=df,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        use_int32_dates=use_int32_dates,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/builder_snapshot.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from datetime import date
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.builder_utils import (
    _require_mapping_key,
    _validate_mapping_dict,
)
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    levels_dict: Mapping[str, float],
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a dictionary mapping risk factor names to their levels.
    """
    if not levels_dict:
        raise ValueError("levels_dict cannot be empty.")

    n = len(levels_dict)

    if name_to_id_map is None:
        # Assume the keys in the dictionary are already integer IDs formatted as strings.
        ids = np.fromiter((int(k) for k in levels_dict.keys()), dtype=np.int32, count=n)
    else:
        # Resolve names to IDs
        _validate_mapping_dict(name_to_id_map)
        ids = np.fromiter(
            (_require_mapping_key(name_to_id_map, str(k)) for k in levels_dict.keys()),
            dtype=np.int32,
            count=n,
        )

    # Extract levels into a contiguous array
    levels = np.fromiter(levels_dict.values(), dtype=float_dtype, count=n)

    return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        factor_ids=ids,
        factor_levels=levels,
    )


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_df(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    df: pd.DataFrame,
    factor_col: str = "risk_factor_name",
    level_col: str = "level",
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a vertical pandas DataFrame.
    """
    if df.empty:
        raise ValueError("DataFrame is empty.")

    if factor_col not in df.columns or level_col not in df.columns:
        raise ValueError(
            f"DataFrame must contain columns '{factor_col}' and '{level_col}'."
        )

    # Drop any rows where the level is NaN
    df_clean = df.dropna(subset=[level_col])

    # There must not be duplicate factor names after dropping NaNs, as this would lead to ambiguity in the snapshot.
    if df_clean[factor_col].duplicated().any():
        duplicated_factors = df_clean[factor_col][
            df_clean[factor_col].duplicated()
        ].unique()
        raise ValueError(
            f"Duplicate factor names found in column '{factor_col}' after dropping NaNs: {duplicated_factors}"
        )

    # Extract as a dictionary to reuse the highly-optimized dictionary builder
    # zip is faster than df.set_index().to_dict() or df.itertuples()
    levels_dict = dict(zip(df_clean[factor_col].astype(str), df_clean[level_col]))

    return build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        levels_dict=levels_dict,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )


def build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_xlsx(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    path: str | Path,
    sheet_name: str | int = 0,
    factor_col: str = "risk_factor_name",
    level_col: str = "level",
    name_to_id_map: Mapping[str, int] | None = None,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float32,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Build a snapshot from a two-column Excel sheet.
    """
    df = pd.read_excel(str(path), sheet_name=sheet_name)

    return build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_df(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        df=df,
        factor_col=factor_col,
        level_col=level_col,
        name_to_id_map=name_to_id_map,
        float_dtype=float_dtype,
    )
```

```python
# src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\builder_utils.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import Any

import numpy as np


def _validate_mapping_dict(m: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None:
    """Validate a small static mapping of risk factor name -> integer id.

    Checks performed:
      - keys must be non-empty strings
      - values must be integer-like (reject bool, float, str)
      - no two keys map to the same integer id

    This helper is optimized for configuration-time validation (small dicts),
    not for per-row lookups in tight loops.
    """
    # Empty/blank key detection (single pass)
    for k in m.keys():
        if not isinstance(k, str) or not k.strip():
            raise ValueError(f"Mapping contains invalid/blank key: {k!r}")

    # Ensure values are integer-like and detect duplicates by tracking seen ids.
    seen: dict[int, str] = {}
    for k, v in m.items():
        # `bool` is a subclass of int in Python; explicitly reject it here because
        # booleans are not valid factor ids.
        if isinstance(v, (bool,)):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Mapping value for '{k}' must be an int id, got bool={v!r}"
            )

        # Accept Python ints and NumPy integer scalars; reject other types.
        if not isinstance(v, (int, np.integer)):
            raise ValueError(
                f"Mapping value for '{k}' must be an int id, got {type(v).__name__}={v!r}"
            )

        iv = int(v)
        prev = seen.get(iv)
        if prev is not None:
            # Clear, actionable error message when multiple names map to same id.
            raise ValueError(
                "Mapping contains duplicate ID assignment:\n"
                f"  '{prev}' and '{k}' both map to ID={iv}\n"
                "Each risk factor must have a unique risk_factor_name_id."
            )
        seen[iv] = k


def _require_mapping_key(
    m: Mapping[str, Any],
    key: str,
    *,
    preview_n: int = 25,
) -> Any:
    """Lookup `key` in mapping `m` and raise a detailed error if missing.

    This helper provides user-friendly diagnostics on configuration errors by
    showing a short preview of known keys and a suggested fix. Designed to be
    inexpensive on the success path (single hash lookup); the preview list is
    materialized only on error.

    Parameters
    ----------
    m : Mapping[str, Any]
        Mapping from risk factor name to id.
    key : str
        Key to lookup (column label converted to string prior to lookup).
    preview_n : int
        Maximum number of known keys to show in the error message.
    """
    try:
        return m[key]
    except KeyError:
        # Materialize a small preview of keys only when diagnosing a failure --
        # prevents huge error messages for very large config maps.
        keys = list(m.keys())
        n = len(keys)
        preview = keys[:preview_n]
        preview_str = ", ".join(preview) + (" ..." if n > preview_n else "")

        raise ValueError(
            "Risk factor ID mapping lookup failed.\n"
            f"Missing key: '{key}'\n\n"
            "Fix:\n"
            "  Add this risk factor to the STATIC config table that defines risk_factor_name_id.\n"
            "  Example row to add:\n"
            f"    risk_factor_name = {key}\n"
            "    risk_factor_name_id = <new unique integer>\n\n"
            f"Known keys preview ({min(preview_n, n)} of {n}):\n"
            f"  {preview_str}\n"
        ) from None
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\compile_snapshot.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/compile_snapshot.py
"""
Compile a flat `(rel_name, level)` levels dictionary into a typed
`RiskFactorLevelSnapshot`, with unit-scale conversion applied.

Public API mirror — analogous to `compile_risk_static_config_from_dict_of_dfs`:

  compile_risk_static_config_from_dict_of_dfs(dict_of_dfs) -> RiskStaticConfig
  compile_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_levels_dict(
      *, as_of_date, levels_dict, static_config
  ) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot

Contract
--------
- Every key in `levels_dict` is a relative risk-factor name (e.g.
  "USD_SOFR_OIS[1Y]", "BRENT_CRUDE_VOL_SURF[M+0][50.00]") that
  `static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id` resolves.
- Each value is the level *in the unit_scale declared by the static
  config for that rf*. This compiler applies the per-rf
  `unit_scale_factor` so the emitted `factor_levels` are always in
  base DECIMAL — e.g. PERCENT inputs (5.30) become decimal (0.0530),
  and BP inputs (50.0) become decimal (0.0050).
- The resolved `factor_ids` array is sorted strictly ascending and
  C-contiguous; `RiskFactorLevelSnapshot.__post_init__` enforces this
  invariant downstream.

Why a separate compiler rather than reusing `build_*_from_dict`?
----------------------------------------------------------------
The existing `build_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_dict` is layout-
agnostic and unit-agnostic — fine for already-canonical numeric inputs.
The compiler here adds two pieces the canonical fixture needs:
  1. Name resolution via the static config (rel_name -> rf_id).
  2. Unit-scale promotion to base DECIMAL.

Both belong to the ingest perimeter, not the hot-path snapshot
container, so they live here in a dedicated compile step.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from datetime import date

import numpy as np

from riskquant.config.static_config.static_config import RiskStaticConfig
from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot


def compile_risk_factor_level_snapshot_from_levels_dict(
    *,
    as_of_date: date,
    levels_dict: Mapping[str, float],
    static_config: RiskStaticConfig,
    float_dtype: type[np.floating] = np.float64,
) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """
    Resolve relative-name -> rf_id, apply unit_scale, and return a snapshot.

    Parameters
    ----------
    as_of_date
        Calendar date the snapshot applies to. Pure metadata on the
        returned object — no arithmetic uses it.
    levels_dict
        Flat mapping of relative-name -> level. Levels are in each rf's
        declared `unit_scale` (PERCENT for IR, DECIMAL for prices /
        FX / vols, BP if anything declares it).
    static_config
        Compiled static config. Source of truth for the rel-name -> rf_id
        index and the per-rf unit_scale_factor.
    float_dtype
        Output dtype for `factor_levels`. Default `float64` to preserve
        precision through the unit-scale multiplication; downstream
        scenario buffers narrow to `float32` at their own perimeter.

    Returns
    -------
    RiskFactorLevelSnapshot
        `factor_ids` int32 sorted-ascending unique; `factor_levels`
        in base DECIMAL, parallel to `factor_ids`.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        Empty `levels_dict`.
    KeyError
        A relative name in `levels_dict` does not resolve to any rf_id
        in the static config. The error names the offending key.
    """
    if not levels_dict:
        raise ValueError(
            "levels_dict cannot be empty; the snapshot must reference at "
            "least one rf_id."
        )

    n = len(levels_dict)
    rel_to_rf_id = static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id
    rf_id_to_unit_scale = static_config.rf_id_to_unit_scale_factor

    # Single-pass resolution: name -> rf_id + per-rf scale -> base level.
    # Pre-allocated int32 / float64 buffers; no Python list intermediates
    # past the dict iteration itself.
    ids_buf = np.empty(n, dtype=np.int32)
    levels_buf = np.empty(n, dtype=float_dtype)

    for i, (rel_name, raw_level) in enumerate(levels_dict.items()):
        rf_id = rel_to_rf_id.get(rel_name)
        if rf_id is None:
            raise KeyError(
                f"rel_name {rel_name!r} did not resolve through "
                f"static_config.rel_name_to_rf_id. The snapshot fixture is "
                f"out of sync with the static config."
            )
        ids_buf[i] = np.int32(rf_id)
        # rf_id_to_unit_scale_factor is keyed by rf_id (numpy array indexed
        # by id). float()-wrap because the result will be cast to
        # float_dtype anyway and we want native multiplication.
        levels_buf[i] = float_dtype(raw_level) * float_dtype(
            rf_id_to_unit_scale[int(rf_id)]
        )

    # The snapshot constructor enforces sorted-unique + contiguous; pass
    # the buffers in directly. If callers fed us duplicate rel-names that
    # map to the same rf_id, the snapshot constructor will raise.
    return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
        as_of_date=as_of_date,
        factor_ids=ids_buf,
        factor_levels=levels_buf,
    )
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\risk_factors\matrix.py">
```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/matrix.py
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

from riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.snapshot import RiskFactorLevelSnapshot
from riskquant.utils.dates import ordinal32_to_datetime64D


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RiskFactorMatrix:
    """
    Canonical RiskFactorMatrix with God-Tier memory layout for time-series operations.

    Invariants:
      - dates: 1D, sorted ascending, unique (int32 ordinal or datetime64[D])
      - factor_ids: 1D int32, sorted ascending, unique
      - values: 2D float32/64, shape (T, N), F-contiguous by default for column-major access
      - len(dates)=T, len(factor_ids)=N
    """

    # datetime64[D] or int32 ordinal
    dates: np.ndarray

    # int32
    factor_ids: np.ndarray

    # float32/64, shape (T, N) --> (observations, factors)
    values: np.ndarray

    # Default memory layout (F-contiguous for time-series)
    fortran_order: bool = True

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        # ---- Basic checks ----
        t_obs, n_factors = self.values.shape
        if self.dates.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError("dates must be 1D")
        if self.factor_ids.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be 1D")
        if self.values.ndim != 2:
            raise ValueError("values must be 2D")

        # ---- dtype checks ----
        if not np.issubdtype(self.values.dtype, np.floating):
            raise TypeError(f"values must be float dtype, got {self.values.dtype}")

        if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer):
            if self.dates.dtype != np.int32:
                raise TypeError(f"int dates must be int32, got {self.dates.dtype}")
        elif self.dates.dtype != np.dtype("datetime64[D]"):
            raise TypeError(
                f"datetime dates must be datetime64[D], got {self.dates.dtype}"
            )

        if self.factor_ids.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"factor_ids must be int32, got {self.factor_ids.dtype}")

        # ---- shape checks ----
        if self.dates.size != t_obs:
            raise ValueError(
                f"len(dates) ({self.dates.size}) != values.shape[0] ({t_obs})"
            )
        if self.factor_ids.size != n_factors:
            raise ValueError(
                f"len(factor_ids) ({self.factor_ids.size}) != values.shape[1] ({n_factors})"
            )

        # ---- memory layout ----
        if self.fortran_order:
            if not self.values.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
                vals = np.asfortranarray(self.values)
                object.__setattr__(self, "values", vals)
        else:
            if not self.values.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
                vals = np.ascontiguousarray(self.values)
                object.__setattr__(self, "values", vals)

        # ---- dates sorted + unique ----
        if t_obs > 1:
            if np.any(self.dates[1:] < self.dates[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("dates must be sorted ascending")
            if np.any(self.dates[1:] == self.dates[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("dates must be unique")

        # ---- factor_ids sorted + unique ----
        # Mirror the dates check above: always run the adjacent-equality
        # check, then sort only if needed. The previous form only entered
        # the duplicate guard on the unsorted path, so an already-sorted
        # input like [1, 2, 2, 3] would bypass uniqueness entirely.
        if n_factors > 1:
            is_unsorted = bool(np.any(self.factor_ids[1:] < self.factor_ids[:-1]))

            if is_unsorted:
                perm = np.argsort(self.factor_ids, kind="mergesort")
                sorted_ids = self.factor_ids[perm]
            else:
                perm = None
                sorted_ids = self.factor_ids

            if np.any(sorted_ids[1:] == sorted_ids[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")

            if perm is not None:
                sorted_vals = (
                    np.asfortranarray(self.values[:, perm])
                    if self.fortran_order
                    else np.ascontiguousarray(self.values[:, perm])
                )
                object.__setattr__(self, "factor_ids", sorted_ids)
                object.__setattr__(self, "values", sorted_vals)

    # ---------------------------- Convenience Methods ----------------------------
    def to_df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        index = pd.DatetimeIndex(
            ordinal32_to_datetime64D(self.dates)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else self.dates
        )
        df = pd.DataFrame(data=self.values, index=index, columns=self.factor_ids)
        df.index.name = "date"
        df.columns.name = "factor_id"
        return df

    def head(self, n: int = 5) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df().head(n=n)

    def tail(self, n: int = 5) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df().tail(n=n)

    def plot(self, **kwargs):
        return self.to_df().plot(**kwargs)

    # ---------------------------- Properties ----------------------------
    @property
    def index_dates(self) -> pd.DatetimeIndex:
        return pd.DatetimeIndex(
            ordinal32_to_datetime64D(self.dates)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else self.dates
        )

    @property
    def index_factors(self) -> pd.Index:
        return pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id")

    @property
    def df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        return self.to_df()

    @property
    def shape(self) -> tuple[int, ...]:
        return self.values.shape

    @property
    def num_dates(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_factors(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[1]

    # ---------------------------- Slicing Methods ----------------------------
    def slice_dates(
        self, start: int | None = None, end: int | None = None
    ) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
        start = 0 if start is None else start
        end = self.num_dates if end is None else end
        vals = self.values[start:end, :]
        if self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.asfortranarray(vals)
        elif not self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.ascontiguousarray(vals)
        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=self.dates[start:end],
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids,
            values=vals,
            fortran_order=self.fortran_order,
        )

    def slice_factors(self, factor_indices: np.ndarray) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
        vals = self.values[:, factor_indices]
        if self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["F_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.asfortranarray(vals)
        elif not self.fortran_order and not vals.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            vals = np.ascontiguousarray(vals)
        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=self.dates,
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids[factor_indices],
            values=vals,
            fortran_order=self.fortran_order,
        )

    # ---------------------------- Time-Series Optimization ----------------------------
    def to_time_series_optimized_array(self) -> np.ndarray:
        """Return F-contiguous array for column-major operations (Numba/Cython)."""
        return np.asfortranarray(self.values) if not self.fortran_order else self.values

    def get_factor_time_series(self, factor_id: int) -> np.ndarray:
        idx = np.searchsorted(self.factor_ids, factor_id)
        if idx >= self.factor_ids.size or self.factor_ids[idx] != factor_id:
            raise KeyError(f"factor_id {factor_id} not found")
        return np.ascontiguousarray(self.values[:, idx])

    # ---------------------------- Snapshot ----------------------------
    def get_snapshot(self, target_date: date) -> RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
        from riskquant.utils.dates import date_to_ord32

        target_val = (
            date_to_ord32(target_date)
            if np.issubdtype(self.dates.dtype, np.integer)
            else np.datetime64(target_date, "D")
        )
        idx = np.searchsorted(self.dates, target_val)
        if idx >= self.dates.size or self.dates[idx] != target_val:
            raise KeyError(f"Date {target_date} not found in matrix")

        levels = self.values[idx, :]
        valid_mask = ~np.isnan(levels)
        return RiskFactorLevelSnapshot(
            as_of_date=target_date,
            factor_ids=self.factor_ids[valid_mask],
            factor_levels=levels[valid_mask],
        )

    @classmethod
    def from_snapshot(
        cls,
        snapshot: RiskFactorLevelSnapshot,
        *,
        fortran_order: bool = True,
    ) -> RiskFactorMatrix:
        """
        Elevate this snapshot into a 1-row RiskFactorMatrix.

        S-Tier mechanics:
        - factor_ids: Passed by reference (zero-copy).
        - values: Reshaped via np.newaxis (zero-copy view).
        - dates: Minimal 1-element array allocation aligned to datetime64[D].
        """

        # 1. Align dates invariant: 1D array of datetime64[D]
        dates_arr = np.array([snapshot.as_of_date], dtype="datetime64[D]")

        # 2. Align values invariant: 2D array (T, N) -> (1, N)
        # Using np.newaxis creates a fast, zero-copy view of the 1D C-contiguous array.
        values_2d = snapshot.factor_levels[np.newaxis, :]

        return RiskFactorMatrix(
            dates=dates_arr,
            factor_ids=snapshot.factor_ids,  # Already validated, sorted, and unique
            values=values_2d,
            fortran_order=fortran_order,
        )
```
</file>

```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/shift_types.py
from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class ShiftType(IntEnum):
    NO_SHIFT = -1
    LOG_RETURN = 1
    SIMPLE_RETURN = 2
    DIFFERENCE = 3


# Kernel-friendly int8 constants (avoid repeated casts in hot code)
SHIFT_NO_SHIFT_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.NO_SHIFT)
SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.LOG_RETURN)
SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.SIMPLE_RETURN)
SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID: np.int8 = np.int8(ShiftType.DIFFERENCE)


# Names tuple
SHIFT_TYPE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(method.name.upper() for method in ShiftType)

# Registry
SHIFT_TYPE_NAME_TO_ID_REGISTRY: dict[str, np.int8] = {
    "NO_SHIFT": SHIFT_NO_SHIFT_ID,
    "LOG_RETURN": SHIFT_LOG_RETURN_ID,
    "SIMPLE_RETURN": SHIFT_SIMPLE_RETURN_ID,
    "DIFFERENCE": SHIFT_DIFFERENCE_ID,
}


def validate_shift_types_dict(shift_types_dict: Mapping[str, int]) -> None:
    """
    Validate that the Excel-provided mapping exactly matches the code enum.

    Rationale:
      - Code is the contract; Excel is data.
      - Kernels rely on stable integer codes.
    """
    # Expected mapping from enum
    expected: dict[str, int] = {
        name: int(member) for name, member in ShiftType.__members__.items()
    }

    # Normalize "got" mapping to plain ints for stable comparison
    got: dict[str, int] = {str(k): int(v) for k, v in shift_types_dict.items()}

    if got == expected:
        return

    missing = {k: expected[k] for k in expected.keys() - got.keys()}
    extra = {k: got[k] for k in got.keys() - expected.keys()}
    different = {
        k: {"expected": expected[k], "got": got[k]}
        for k in (expected.keys() & got.keys())
        if expected[k] != got[k]
    }

    raise ValueError(
        "shift_types sheet does not match riskquant ShiftType enum.\n"
        f"missing={missing}\n"
        f"extra={extra}\n"
        f"different={different}\n"
        "Fix the Excel shift_types table or update riskquant.market_data.risk_factors.shift_types.ShiftType."
    )
```
</file>

```python
# riskquant/market_data/risk_factors/snapshot.py

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class RiskFactorLevelSnapshot:
    """Immutable snapshot of risk factor levels at a single as-of date.

    Attributes
    ----------
    as_of_date : datetime.date
        The calendar date for which the snapshot applies.
    factor_ids : np.ndarray[int32]
        1-D array of unique risk-factor identifiers (dtype int32, C-contiguous).
        Must be sorted ascending; if not sorted, the constructor will re-order
        the ids and corresponding `factor_levels`.
    factor_levels : np.ndarray[float32|float64]
        1-D numeric array of factor levels aligned with `factor_ids`.
        Must be the same length as `factor_ids` and C-contiguous.

    Invariants (enforced on construction)
    -------------------------------------
    - `factor_ids` is 1D, dtype int32, C-contiguous, sorted ascending and unique
    - `factor_levels` is 1D, dtype float32 or float64, C-contiguous
    - lengths of `factor_ids` and `factor_levels` match

    The class is intentionally lightweight and validates eagerly so downstream
    numerical kernels can assume compact, contiguous, and deterministic
    memory layouts (fast gathers, stable hashing, etc.).
    """

    as_of_date: date
    factor_ids: np.ndarray  # (N0,) int32
    factor_levels: np.ndarray  # (N0,) float32/float64

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        """Validate and canonicalize snapshot arrays.

        The method performs cheap, early checks and performs the minimal amount
        of work required to restore canonical invariants (sorting + contiguity).
        It raises clear exceptions on invalid input rather than attempting to
        silently coerce data into a valid form.
        """
        # Local aliases to make subsequent checks concise and explicit.
        ids = self.factor_ids
        lv = self.factor_levels

        # ----- shape checks -----
        # Ensure both arrays are 1-D vectors.
        if ids.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"factor_ids must be 1D; got shape={ids.shape}")
        if lv.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"factor_levels must be 1D; got shape={lv.shape}")

        # Ensure arrays have the same length (alignment requirement).
        if ids.shape[0] != lv.shape[0]:
            raise ValueError(
                f"factor_ids and factor_levels length mismatch: "
                f"{ids.shape[0]} vs {lv.shape[0]}"
            )

        # ----- dtype checks -----
        # factor_ids must be 32-bit integers for compactness and predictable
        # behavior when used as indices or keys in mappings.
        if ids.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"factor_ids must be int32; got {ids.dtype}")

        # factor_levels must be floating point (single or double precision).
        if lv.dtype not in (np.float32, np.float64):
            raise TypeError(f"factor_levels must be float32 or float64; got {lv.dtype}")

        # ----- memory layout checks -----
        # Many numerical routines assume C-contiguous 1-D arrays; check early so
        # callers get an explicit error instead of subtle performance bugs.
        if not ids.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be C-contiguous")
        if not lv.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            raise ValueError("factor_levels must be C-contiguous")

        # Fast path: zero or one element requires no further sorting/uniqueness checks.
        n = ids.size
        if n <= 1:
            return

        # ----- sorted + unique enforcement -----
        # If ids are already non-decreasing, perform an adjacent-duplicate check
        # which is allocation-free. If not sorted, build a stable permutation and
        # reorder both arrays so the object is canonical afterwards.
        if not np.any(ids[1:] < ids[:-1]):
            # ids are non-decreasing — ensure strict uniqueness.
            if np.any(ids[1:] == ids[:-1]):
                raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")
            return

        # ids are unsorted: compute a stable sort permutation (mergesort).
        perm = np.argsort(ids, kind="mergesort")

        # Apply the permutation to ids (produces a new array view/copy).
        ids_sorted = ids[perm]

        # Check for duplicates after sorting (adjacent duplicates are easy to detect).
        if np.any(ids_sorted[1:] == ids_sorted[:-1]):
            raise ValueError("factor_ids must be unique")

        # Reorder factor levels to keep alignment with the now-sorted ids.
        lv_sorted = lv[perm]

        # Fancy indexing typically returns a contiguous array for 1-D, but
        # explicitly enforce C-contiguity to preserve the class invariant.
        if not lv_sorted.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]:
            lv_sorted = np.ascontiguousarray(lv_sorted)

        # Bypass dataclass immutability to install the canonicalized arrays.
        object.__setattr__(self, "factor_ids", ids_sorted)
        object.__setattr__(self, "factor_levels", lv_sorted)

    def to_df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Return the snapshot as a 1-row wide DataFrame."""
        # Reshape the 1D levels array into a 2D row vector (1, N)
        data_2d = self.factor_levels[np.newaxis, :]

        df = pd.DataFrame(
            data=data_2d,
            index=pd.DatetimeIndex([self.as_of_date], name="date"),
            columns=pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id"),
        )
        return df

    def to_series(self) -> pd.Series:
        """Return the snapshot as a pandas Series."""
        return pd.Series(
            data=self.factor_levels,
            index=pd.Index(self.factor_ids, name="factor_id"),
            name=self.as_of_date,
        )

    @property
    def df(self) -> pd.DataFrame:
        """Alias for to_df()."""
        return self.to_df()

    @property
    def sr(self) -> pd.Series:
        """Alias for to_series()."""
        return self.to_series()

    @property
    def num_factors(self) -> int:
        return len(self.factor_ids)

    def summary(self) -> None:
        """Print a console-friendly overview of the snapshot."""
        lines = [
            "=======================================",
            "=== RiskFactorLevelSnapshot Summary ===",
            "=======================================",
            f"  As of date          : {self.as_of_date}",
            f"  Number of factors   : {self.num_factors}",
            f"  Factor ID range     : {self.factor_ids.min()} to {self.factor_ids.max()}",
            f"  Factor level range  : {self.factor_levels.min()} to {self.factor_levels.max()}",
            "=======================================",
        ]
        print("\n".join(lines))
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\state.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/state.py
"""
Vectorized market data container for the valuation engine.

`MarketDataMatrix` is the frozen, valuation-ready SoA representation of all
market data required to price a `CompiledPortfolio` across `num_scenarios`
parallel scenarios.

Axis conventions
----------------
* Dimension 0 always corresponds to a `_uix` (price / fx_effective / vol /
  discount universe).
* For 2-D scalar grids the final dimension is the scenario index.
* 3-D grids (`discount_factor_grid`, `price_curve_grid`) carry a middle
  date-uix axis aligned with `CompiledPortfolio.unique_dates_ord32`, and
  scenario in the trailing axis.
* `vol_surfaces` is 4-D and arranged as `[surface, expiry, scenario, pillar]`
  so that the strike-axis pillars for one trade-scenario are a single
  contiguous run of memory — see Vol-surface layout below.

FX semantics
------------
`fx_spots_effective` holds the deduplicated, fully-resolved FX transform
matrix. Row `k` is the spot transform identified by row `k` of the
portfolio's `UixMapper.fx_effective_pairs`. Trades that need no FX carry
`INVALID_UIX` (< 0) in their per-trade `fx_*_effective_uix` field and skip
the FX gather entirely via a hoisted branch in the pricer.

Pricer contract for FX:
    out[i, s] *= fx_spots_effective[fx_effective_uix[i], s]

No inversion math, no `1.0 / rate`, no scenario-level branches. The raw
`fx_spots` matrix is producer-side only and never reaches the pricers.

Vol-surface layout (CRITICAL)
-----------------------------
`vol_surfaces` is `(num_vol_surfaces, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios,
max_vol_strikes)` — pillar in the trailing axis. This guarantees that
the strike pillars for one (surface, expiry, scenario) tuple form one
C-contiguous run, which is exactly what the strike-axis interpolation
in the option-curve kernel needs for SIMD-friendly reads.

The market-data builder is responsible for emitting this layout; the
kernels rely on it for `v_plane[s]` to be a contiguous 1-D pillar row.

Per-surface y-axis-type ids — POST-CALIBRATION
----------------------------------------------
`surface_y_axis_type_ids` is `(num_vol_surfaces,)` int8 and carries the
y-axis-type id AFTER the builder's DELTA -> MONEYNESS_FWD calibration
pass. DELTA_CALL rows are rewritten in place to MONEYNESS_FWD; non-
DELTA rows pass through. The pricing kernels do not read this array —
they dispatch on the per-trade copy from `IngestBatch` which is
already MONEYNESS_FWD via `surface_y_axis_kind_for_kernel` at ingest
time. The field is exposed here for diagnostics and any downstream
consumer (e.g. a downstream calibration pass) that needs the runtime
per-surface view.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
from numpy.typing import NDArray


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class MarketDataMatrix:
    """Frozen, validated SoA container of market data for vectorized valuation."""

    num_scenarios: int

    # ========== 1. SCALARS / NODES ==========

    # (num_fx_effective_pairs, num_scenarios) — deduplicated, pre-resolved FX
    # transforms. Pricers gather one row per leg per trade and multiply.
    fx_spots_effective: npt.NDArray[np.float64]

    # SCALAR-NODE LEVELS — UNIVERSE-ALIGNED (item C, option A1)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # TODO: Need some description here
    prices: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # (num_market_ids, num_scenarios) — alias of `prices` (see above)
    forward_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # (num_market_ids, num_scenarios) — alias of `prices` (see above)
    vol_nodes: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # ========== 2. DISCOUNT GRID ==========
    # (num_discount_curves, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios)
    discount_factor_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # ========== 3. CURVE-BASED DATA ==========
    # (num_market_ids, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios) — axis-0 is the
    # `market_id_universe` (item C / A1, curve layer). Forward-curve blocks
    # index `price_curve_grid[price_curve_uix, delivery_uix, s]` where
    # `price_curve_uix` is the container's `market_id_universe` row; the
    # builder scatters the dense per-curve grid into these rows. Non-curve
    # rows are unused.
    price_curve_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # ========== 4. TIME GRIDS ==========
    # (num_unique_dates,) — year fractions from as_of to each unique date.
    tte_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64),
    )

    sqrt_tte_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(init=False)

    half_tte_grid: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(init=False)

    # ========== 5. SURFACES ==========
    # (num_vol_surfaces, max_vol_strikes) — strike pillars per surface.
    vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # (num_vol_surfaces, num_unique_dates, num_scenarios, max_vol_strikes)
    # Pillar-in-trailing-axis layout — see module docstring.
    vol_surfaces: npt.NDArray[np.float64] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty((0, 0, 0, 0), dtype=np.float64),
    )

    # (num_vol_surfaces,) — POST-CALIBRATION per-surface y-axis-type id.
    # The builder runs DELTA -> MONEYNESS_FWD calibration via
    # `market_data.calibrate.delta_to_moneyness` and overwrites
    # DELTA_CALL slots with MONEYNESS_FWD; non-DELTA rows pass through.
    # Exposed here for diagnostics and any downstream consumer that
    # needs the per-surface view (the pricing kernel itself reads the
    # per-trade copy from `IngestBatch`, which already carries the
    # post-calibration value via `surface_y_axis_kind_for_kernel` at
    # ingest time — the two views agree by construction).
    surface_y_axis_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8] = field(
        default_factory=lambda: np.empty(0, dtype=np.int8),
    )

    def __post_init__(self):

        object.__setattr__(
            self,
            "sqrt_tte_grid",
            np.sqrt(self.tte_grid),
        )

        object.__setattr__(
            self,
            "half_tte_grid",
            0.5 * self.tte_grid,
        )


_F64 = np.float64


def _empty_factor_matrix_f64() -> NDArray[_F64]:
    return np.empty(0, dtype=_F64)


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class ValuationState:
    """
    Fully-resolved valuation factors consumed directly by pricing kernels.

    Frozen, validated SoA container of valuation factors for vectorized valuation.
    This is not a market-data container; it is the final, fully-resolved representation
    of market data consumed by the valuation engine.

    `ValuationState` is the final market-data representation used by the
    valuation engine. It contains only scenario-dependent quantities with
    direct economic meaning in pricing equations.

    NB! THIS IS AN INTERNAL EXECUTION-STATE OBJECT. IT DOES NOT VALIDATE OR SANITIZE
    MARKET DATA. IT IS THE FINAL, FULLY-RESOLVED REPRESENTATION OF MARKET DATA
    CONSUMED BY THE VALUATION ENGINE. IT IS NOT MEANT TO BE VIEWABLE OR AUDITABLE BY
    END USERS. THE COMPILER GUARANTEES THAT ALL MARKET DATA IS VALID AND SANITIZED
    BEFORE IT REACHES THIS OBJECT.

    Architectural role
    ------------------
    RiskQuant deliberately separates:

        Raw Market Data
            ↓
        Market Data Compiler
            ↓
        ValuationState
            ↓
        Valuation Engine
            ↓
        Pricing Kernels

    The market-data compiler is responsible for all market-data interpretation
    and transformation, including:

        * Curve interpolation
        * Surface interpolation
        * Forward extraction
        * Discount-factor generation
        * Vol calibration
        * FX inversion / resolution
        * Date and calendar handling

    Pricing kernels must not perform any of the above operations.

    Kernel contract
    ---------------
    Every array follows the convention:

        [factor_uix, scenario]

    where:

        factor_uix
            Dense execution coordinate produced by the market-data compiler.

        scenario
            Scenario index within the current scenario batch.

    Kernels gather rows from `ValuationState`, combine them with trade-level
    constants from compiled blocks, and perform arithmetic.

    Example:

        forward_price = state.forward_prices[forward_price_uix]
        discount_factor = state.discount_factors[df_uix]

        pv = qty * forward_price * discount_factor

    Design principles
    -----------------
    `ValuationState` contains valuation factors, not market structures.

    Allowed:

        * Spot prices
        * Forward prices
        * Forward rates
        * Discount factors
        * Volatilities
        * FX transforms

    Not allowed:

        * Curves
        * Surfaces
        * Dates
        * Calendars
        * Interpolation metadata
        * Pillar definitions
        * Market-data construction logic

    If a pricing kernel must derive a quantity from a curve, surface, date,
    or calendar, that derivation belongs in the market-data compiler and the
    resulting valuation factor should be materialized here instead.

    Memory layout
    -------------
    All valuation-factor arrays are stored as:

    (num_factors, scenario_batch_size)

    with the scenario axis in the trailing dimension.

    This layout matches the kernel execution model:

        factor_row = factor[factor_uix]

    followed by a contiguous scan across scenarios.

    The trailing scenario dimension is therefore C-contiguous,
    allowing SIMD-friendly streaming reads and efficient CPU
    cache utilization.

    The scenario dimension is batch-local; `ValuationState` represents only
    the currently active scenario batch, not the entire simulation universe.


    """

    # ==================================================================================
    # BATCH PROPERTIES
    # ==================================================================================

    batch_size: int

    # ==================================================================================
    # RESOLVED FACTORS
    # ==================================================================================

    # (num_spot_prices, batch_size)
    spot_prices: NDArray[_F64] = field(default_factory=_empty_factor_matrix_f64)

    # (num_forward_prices, batch_size)
    forward_prices: NDArray[_F64] = field(default_factory=_empty_factor_matrix_f64)

    # (num_forward_rates, batch_size)
    # Annualized simple forward interest rates
    forward_rates: NDArray[_F64] = field(default_factory=_empty_factor_matrix_f64)

    # (num_vols, batch_size)
    vols: NDArray[_F64] = field(default_factory=_empty_factor_matrix_f64)

    # (num_discount_factors, batch_size)
    discount_factors: NDArray[_F64] = field(default_factory=_empty_factor_matrix_f64)

    # (num_fx_effective_pairs, batch_size)
    # Effective FX spot rates (already inverted where needed) for direct gather by
    # pricer kernels - pricer kernels are as dumb as possible and do no inversion
    # math or dynamic checks, just one gather + multiply per leg.
    fx_spots_effective: NDArray[_F64] = field(default_factory=_empty_factor_matrix_f64)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/surfaces/y_axes_types.py

from enum import IntEnum

import numpy as np


class SurfaceYAxisType(IntEnum):
    STRIKE = 1
    LOG_STRIKE = 2
    MONEYNESS_SPOT = 3
    MONEYNESS_FWD = 4
    LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT = 5
    LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD = 6
    DELTA_CALL = 7


# compiler-friendly int8 constants
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_STRIKE: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.STRIKE,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_STRIKE: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_STRIKE,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_SPOT: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_SPOT,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_FWD: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_FWD,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD,
)
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL: np.int8 = np.int8(
    SurfaceYAxisType.DELTA_CALL,
)

# Names tuple
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_NAMES: tuple[str, ...] = tuple(
    method.name.upper() for method in SurfaceYAxisType
)

# Central registry
SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_REGISTRY: dict[str, SurfaceYAxisType] = {
    "STRIKE": SurfaceYAxisType.STRIKE,
    "LOG_STRIKE": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_STRIKE,
    "MONEYNESS_SPOT": SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    "MONEYNESS_FWD": SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_FWD,
    "LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    "LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD": SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD,
    "DELTA_CALL": SurfaceYAxisType.DELTA_CALL,
}


# ------------------------------------------------------------------
# Post-calibration y-axis-kind mapping
# ------------------------------------------------------------------
# Some y-axis kinds declared in the static config are NEVER seen by the
# pricing kernels — the builder calibrates them at hydrate time to a
# kernel-consumable representation. `surface_y_axis_kind_for_kernel`
# returns the int8 id the kernel will branch on, given the static
# config's declared kind string.
#
# Single source of truth: BOTH the ingest-side per-trade y-axis-type
# resolver AND the runtime per-surface y-axis-type-ids array MUST go
# through this helper. That keeps the per-trade and per-surface views
# in lockstep without round-tripping the original-DELTA signal through
# the runner's UniverseMapping.
#
# Current mapping:
#   DELTA_CALL   -> MONEYNESS_FWD  (see market_data.calibrate.delta_to_moneyness)
#   *            -> identity
_POST_CALIBRATION_YAX_ID: dict[SurfaceYAxisType, np.int8] = {
    SurfaceYAxisType.STRIKE: SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_STRIKE,
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_STRIKE: SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_STRIKE,
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_SPOT: SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    SurfaceYAxisType.MONEYNESS_FWD: SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_FWD,
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT: SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_SPOT,
    SurfaceYAxisType.LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD: SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_LOG_MONEYNESS_FWD,
    # DELTA_CALL gets calibrated to MONEYNESS_FWD by the builder.
    SurfaceYAxisType.DELTA_CALL: SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_FWD,
}


def surface_y_axis_kind_for_kernel(y_axis_kind: str) -> np.int8:
    """
    Resolve a static-config y-axis-kind string to the int8 id the
    pricing kernel will dispatch on.

    Performs the static_config -> post-calibration mapping in one
    place. DELTA-quoted surfaces are calibrated to MONEYNESS_FWD at
    builder time; this helper returns MONEYNESS_FWD's int8 id so that
    ingest- and runtime-side y-axis-type arrays both see the post-
    calibration value.

    Raises
    ------
    KeyError
        If `y_axis_kind` is not a `SurfaceYAxisType` member.
    """
    return _POST_CALIBRATION_YAX_ID[SurfaceYAxisType[y_axis_kind]]
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\market_data\transformers\delta_to_moneyness.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/market_data/transformers/delta_to_moneyness.py
"""
DELTA-quoted vol-surface -> MONEYNESS_FWD-quoted vol-surface calibration.

Why MONEYNESS_FWD and not STRIKE
--------------------------------
The Black-Scholes inversion `K = F * exp(-σ * √T * Φ⁻¹(δ) + 0.5 * σ² * T)`
gives strike as a function of the underlying's forward F. Emitting a
STRIKE-quoted shadow surface would force the calibrator to know F per
expiry per scenario — coupling it to FX rows, discount curves, and
forward-curve gathers it has no business reading.

Switching the target axis to MONEYNESS_FWD (K/F) cancels the F factor:

    K/F = exp(-σ * √T * Φ⁻¹(δ) + 0.5 * σ² * T)

The transform depends only on (δ, σ, T). The option-curve kernel
already has a `_Y_KIND_MONEYNESS` dispatch path that computes `y_target
= k / fwd` per scenario; the shadow surface drops in unchanged.

Layout-preserving approximation
-------------------------------
The natural mathematical result is per-(δ, expiry, scenario) pillars,
which would promote `vol_surface_strikes` from `(C, K)` to `(C, D, S,
K)` — a kernel signature change. We avoid that by using a per-surface
scalar reference pair (T_REF, σ_REF):

  T_REF      = tenor at the middle row of the surface's expiry axis.
               Concretely the median tenor in the raw input tenor grid.
  σ_REF      = ATM σ at (T_REF, scenario 0). ATM is the pillar whose
               original delta label is closest to 0.50 (the convention
               for ATM-fwd on a DELTA_CALL-quoted surface).

The shadow pillars are then per-(C, K), shared across expiry and
scenario, matching the existing layout. This is a fixture-grade
approximation that captures the smile's *shape* correctly at T_REF
and at scenario 0; it loses accuracy when trades expire far from
T_REF or under stressed scenarios. Production systems converge to a
proper per-(D, S) pillar grid plus a kernel signature change — that
remains future work.

Numerical floor
---------------
`Φ⁻¹(δ)` blows up as δ -> {0, 1}. The calibrator clamps δ into
`[DELTA_FLOOR, 1 - DELTA_FLOOR]` to keep `ndtri` finite. Production
DELTA-quoted surfaces typically carry pillars in [0.05, 0.95]; the
floor (1e-4) only triggers on pathological inputs.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
from scipy.special import ndtri

from riskquant.market_data.surfaces.y_axes_types import (
    SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL,
    SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_FWD,
)

_DELTA_FLOOR: float = 1e-4
_ATM_DELTA_TARGET: float = 0.50


def _calibrate_one_surface(
    *,
    deltas: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Convert one DELTA-quoted surface's pillar coordinates to MONEYNESS_FWD.

    Parameters
    ----------
    deltas
        `(K,)` delta-call pillar labels (e.g. [0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 0.90]).
    tenors
        `(M_tenors,)` raw input tenor axis in years (the surface's
        native expiry pillars; NOT the runtime unique-date axis — the
        calibrator runs BEFORE time-slicing).
    values
        `(M_tenors, K, S_scenarios)` raw σ values from the snapshot for
        this surface, sliced from the unflattened `raw_vol_surface_values
        [c]` payload BEFORE any time-slicing or layout swap. The
        calibrator reads scenario 0 only.

    Returns
    -------
    moneyness_pillars
        `(K,)` calibrated K/F pillar coordinates, shared across (expiry,
        scenario) for this surface.
    """
    if deltas.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError(f"deltas must be 1-D; got {deltas.shape}.")
    k_pillars = deltas.shape[0]
    if k_pillars == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    if values.shape[1] != k_pillars or tenors.shape[0] != values.shape[0]:
        raise ValueError(
            f"shape mismatch: deltas={deltas.shape}, tenors={tenors.shape}, "
            f"values={values.shape}"
        )

    # ---- T_REF: median tenor ----
    # Median over the surface's *own* tenor axis. Stable under any
    # monotone tenor ordering; doesn't depend on the runtime unique-
    # date axis.
    m_tenors = tenors.shape[0]
    t_ref_idx = m_tenors // 2
    t_ref = float(tenors[t_ref_idx])
    if t_ref <= 0.0:
        # Pathological: zero or negative tenor. Bail to a tiny floor;
        # the kernel's _TTE_FLOOR catches the resulting σ²·T term.
        t_ref = 1e-6

    # ---- σ_REF: ATM σ at scenario 0 ----
    atm_idx = int(np.argmin(np.abs(deltas - _ATM_DELTA_TARGET)))
    sigma_ref = float(values[t_ref_idx, atm_idx, 0])
    if not np.isfinite(sigma_ref) or sigma_ref <= 0.0:
        raise ValueError(
            f"σ_REF resolved to non-positive / non-finite "
            f"({sigma_ref!r}) at tenor index {t_ref_idx}, "
            f"delta-pillar index {atm_idx}. Surface data is malformed."
        )

    # ---- Black-76 inverse mapping: δ -> K/F ----
    deltas_clamped = np.clip(deltas, _DELTA_FLOOR, 1.0 - _DELTA_FLOOR)
    phi_inv = ndtri(deltas_clamped)  # scipy.special.ndtri == Φ⁻¹

    sqrt_t = np.sqrt(t_ref)
    half_sig_sq_t = 0.5 * sigma_ref * sigma_ref * t_ref
    log_moneyness = -sigma_ref * sqrt_t * phi_inv + half_sig_sq_t

    return np.ascontiguousarray(np.exp(log_moneyness), dtype=np.float64)


def calibrate_delta_surfaces_to_moneyness(
    *,
    raw_vol_surface_strikes: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_tenors: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    raw_vol_surface_values: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    surface_y_axis_type_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
) -> tuple[npt.NDArray[np.float64], npt.NDArray[np.int8]]:
    """
    Calibrate every DELTA_CALL row of a vol-surface batch to MONEYNESS_FWD.

    Returns a NEW `(C_surf, K_strikes)` pillar array and a NEW
    `(C_surf,)` y-axis-type-id array with DELTA_CALL rows rewritten.
    Non-DELTA rows are passed through untouched. The values array
    (`raw_vol_surface_values`) is not mutated — the same σ at the same
    pillar index just acquires a different y-axis label after this
    pass.

    Parameters
    ----------
    raw_vol_surface_strikes
        `(C_surf, K_strikes)` original pillar labels. DELTA_CALL rows
        carry delta values in [0, 1]; other rows carry strike / moneyness
        / log-moneyness coordinates and are passed through.
    raw_vol_surface_tenors
        `(C_surf, M_tenors)` per-surface tenor axes in years.
    raw_vol_surface_values
        `(C_surf, M_tenors, K_strikes, S_scn)` raw σ grid. Only scenario
        0 is read; the rest pass through the builder unchanged.
    surface_y_axis_type_ids
        `(C_surf,)` int8 y-axis-type ids declared by the static config.

    Returns
    -------
    pillars_out
        `(C_surf, K_strikes)` post-calibration pillar coordinates.
    yax_ids_out
        `(C_surf,)` post-calibration y-axis-type ids (DELTA_CALL ->
        MONEYNESS_FWD; others unchanged).
    """
    c_surf = surface_y_axis_type_ids.shape[0]

    if c_surf == 0:
        return (
            raw_vol_surface_strikes.copy(),
            surface_y_axis_type_ids.copy(),
        )

    # Edge allocation; downstream kernels never touch these directly.
    pillars_out = np.ascontiguousarray(raw_vol_surface_strikes, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    yax_ids_out = surface_y_axis_type_ids.copy()

    delta_mask = surface_y_axis_type_ids == SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_DELTA_CALL
    if not delta_mask.any():
        return pillars_out, yax_ids_out

    delta_idxs = np.flatnonzero(delta_mask)
    for c in delta_idxs:
        pillars_out[c] = _calibrate_one_surface(
            deltas=raw_vol_surface_strikes[c],
            tenors=raw_vol_surface_tenors[c],
            values=raw_vol_surface_values[c],
        )
        yax_ids_out[c] = SURFACE_Y_AXIS_TYPE_MONEYNESS_FWD

    return pillars_out, yax_ids_out
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/numba.py

"""
Centralized Numba decorator definitions used throughout RiskQuant.

Rationale
---------
RiskQuant standardizes Numba compilation settings to ensure consistent
performance characteristics, numerical behavior, and cache utilization
across the codebase.

This module provides three decorators:

    @njit
        Default choice for most compiled functions.

    @parallel_njit
        For kernels that parallelize outer loops using ``numba.prange``.

    @inline_njit
        For tiny helper functions that should be inlined into callers to
        eliminate function-call overhead.

By centralizing these definitions, compilation settings can be adjusted in
one location without touching hundreds of kernels throughout the system.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from functools import partial

import numba as nb

# Type aliases for Numba-compiled functions — enforce C-contiguous layout for all
# arrays to ensure the compiler emits straight-line SIMD loops with no dynamic
# stride checks. These are used in type signatures for compiled functions below.
_F64_1D = nb.float64[::1]
_F64_2D = nb.float64[:, ::1]
_F64_3D = nb.float64[:, :, ::1]
_F64_4D = nb.float64[:, :, :, ::1]

_F32_1D = nb.float32[::1]
_F32_2D = nb.float32[:, ::1]
_F32_3D = nb.float32[:, :, ::1]
_F32_4D = nb.float32[:, :, :, ::1]

_I64_1D = nb.int64[::1]
_I32_1D = nb.int32[::1]
_I8_1D = nb.int8[::1]

_BOOL_1D = nb.bool_[::1]

#: Default Numba compiler configuration used throughout RiskQuant.
#:
#: Use for the vast majority of compiled functions.
#:
#: Features:
#:     - Explicit fast-math optimizations
#:     - GIL-free execution
#:     - Bounds-check elimination
#:     - On-disk compilation caching
#:     - NumPy-style floating-point error behavior
#:
#: Example:
#:
#:     @njit
#:     def black_scholes(...):
#:         ...
njit = partial(
    nb.njit,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)


#: Parallel Numba compiler configuration.
#:
#: Use for computational kernels that distribute work across threads using
#: ``numba.prange``.
#:
#: Typical usage:
#:
#:     @parallel_njit
#:     def price_kernel(...):
#:         for i in nb.prange(num_trades):
#:             ...
#:
#: Avoid for:
#:     - Small loops
#:     - Helper functions
#:     - Functions that do not use ``prange``
parallel_njit = partial(
    nb.njit,
    parallel=True,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
)


#: Inline Numba compiler configuration.
#:
#: Intended for extremely small helper functions that are called repeatedly
#: inside hot loops.
#:
#: Examples:
#:     - Normal CDF approximations
#:     - Small mathematical transforms
#:     - Utility functions used inside pricing kernels
#:
#: The ``inline="always"`` hint requests that Numba inline the function body
#: into callers whenever possible.
#:
#: ``error_model="numpy"`` is set to match ``njit``/``parallel_njit``: division
#: by zero yields inf/nan rather than raising inside a nopython (and possibly
#: parallel) caller. Without it, an unguarded division in an inlined helper can
#: turn into a hard ``ZeroDivisionError`` mid-kernel.
inline_njit = partial(
    nb.njit,
    fastmath={"contract", "reassoc", "nsz"},
    nogil=True,
    boundscheck=False,
    error_model="numpy",
    cache=True,
    inline="always",
)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/universe.py
"""
Run-level UIX universe contract.

`UixMapper` is the immutable single source of truth for global-ID -> UIX
translation across a run. It is deliberately a LEAF module: it depends only
on `_arrays` and `constants`, so any tier (portfolio, market_data, simlab)
can import the contract type at runtime without a layering inversion. The
PRODUCER, `build_uix_mapper(ingest_batches)`, stays in `portfolio.engine`
because it consumes portfolio internals (`IngestBatch`); only the contract
type lives here.

This module was extracted from `portfolio.engine` (item C2) specifically to
let `market_data.builder` / `market_data.assemble` drop their `TYPE_CHECKING`
deferral of `UixMapper` — the deferral existed only to dodge the
`market_data -> portfolio` import cycle, which no longer exists now that the
contract type is a leaf.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant._arrays import searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32
from riskquant.constants import INVALID_UIX


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class UixMapper:
    """
    Immutable execution-space translator.

    This is the single source of truth for global-ID → UIX translation.

    Holds the four global universes touched by the run plus the deduplicated
    FX-effective pair table. All universe arrays are sorted ascending and
    unique; the `__post_init__` validation runs once so the runtime `map_*`
    methods can stay allocation-light and branch-light.

    Field layout
    ------------
    market_id_universe          : sorted-unique int32 IDs used by
                                  prices / forwards / vols / fx-spots.
                                  Indexed by `price_uix`, `vol_uix`,
                                  `underlying_price_curve_uix`, etc.
    discount_curve_id_universe  : sorted-unique int32 IDs used by discount
                                  curves. Kept distinct from `market_id_universe`
                                  because the discount-curve ID range is
                                  disjoint and `MarketDataMatrix.discount_factor_grid`
                                  has its own axis-0 universe.
    date_ord32_universe         : sorted-unique int32 day-ordinals
                                  (days-since-epoch) touched by the run.
                                  Indexed by `*_date_uix` on blocks.
    fx_effective_pairs          : (K, 2) int32 deduplicated `(fx_rf_uix, invert)`
                                  table. `fx_rf_uix` is itself an index into
                                  `market_id_universe`; `invert` is 0 or 1.
                                  The market-data builder turns this into a
                                  `(K, S)` `fx_spots_effective` matrix.
    """

    market_id_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    discount_curve_id_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    date_ord32_universe: npt.NDArray[np.int32]
    fx_effective_pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32]

    # --- INVARIANTS (CHECKED ONCE) -----------------------------------------

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._validate_universe(self.market_id_universe, "market_id_universe")
        self._validate_universe(
            self.discount_curve_id_universe, "discount_curve_id_universe"
        )
        self._validate_universe(self.date_ord32_universe, "date_ord32_universe")
        self._validate_fx_effective_pairs(self.fx_effective_pairs)

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_universe(arr: npt.NDArray[np.int32], name: str) -> None:
        if arr.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"{name} must be int32; got {arr.dtype}.")
        if arr.ndim != 1:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be 1-D; got ndim={arr.ndim}.")
        if not arr.flags.c_contiguous:
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be C-contiguous.")
        if arr.size > 1 and np.any(arr[1:] <= arr[:-1]):
            raise ValueError(f"{name} must be strictly increasing and unique.")

    @staticmethod
    def _validate_fx_effective_pairs(pairs: npt.NDArray[np.int32]) -> None:
        if pairs.dtype != np.int32:
            raise TypeError(f"fx_effective_pairs must be int32; got {pairs.dtype}.")
        if pairs.ndim != 2 or (pairs.size and pairs.shape[1] != 2):
            raise ValueError(
                f"fx_effective_pairs must have shape (K, 2); got {pairs.shape}"
            )
        if not pairs.flags.c_contiguous:
            raise ValueError("fx_effective_pairs must be C-contiguous.")

    # --- TRANSLATION API ---------------------------------------------------

    def map_market_ids_to_uix(
        self,
        raw_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global market IDs into `market_id_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_ids < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.market_id_universe,
            raw=raw_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_discount_ids_to_uix(
        self,
        raw_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global discount-curve IDs into
        `discount_curve_id_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_ids < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.discount_curve_id_universe,
            raw=raw_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_dates_to_uix(
        self,
        ordinals: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate global day-ordinals into `date_ord32_universe` UIXs.

        Sentinel rows (`ordinals < 0`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        return searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.date_ord32_universe,
            raw=ordinals,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

    def map_fx_legs_to_effective_uix(
        self,
        *,
        raw_fx_ids: npt.NDArray[np.int32],
        raw_fx_inv: npt.NDArray[np.int8],
    ) -> npt.NDArray[np.int32]:
        """
        Translate per-trade `(raw_fx_id, raw_fx_inv)` into per-trade
        `fx_spots_effective_uix` indices.

        Steps (all vectorized, no Python loop):
          1. Translate `raw_fx_ids` into `market_id_universe` UIXs.
          2. Look the resulting `(fx_uix, invert)` pair up in
             `fx_effective_pairs` to obtain the effective UIX.

        Sentinel rows (`raw_fx_ids == NO_FX_RF_REQUIRED`) map to `INVALID_UIX`.
        """
        if raw_fx_ids.shape != raw_fx_inv.shape:
            raise ValueError(
                f"fx legs shape mismatch: ids={raw_fx_ids.shape}, "
                f"inv={raw_fx_inv.shape}"
            )

        # Stage 1: market-id universe lookup. Sentinels collapse to INVALID_UIX.
        fx_market_uix = searchsorted_or_propagate_sentinel_int32(
            universe=self.market_id_universe,
            raw=raw_fx_ids,
            sentinel=INVALID_UIX,
        )

        # Stage 2: dedup-pair lookup. We pack each (uix, inv) pair into a
        # single int64 key for a vectorized one-shot searchsorted into the
        # equivalently-packed effective-pairs table.
        n = raw_fx_ids.shape[0]
        out = np.full(n, INVALID_UIX, dtype=np.int32)

        if self.fx_effective_pairs.size == 0:
            return out

        needs_fx = fx_market_uix != INVALID_UIX
        if not needs_fx.any():
            return out

        needed_uix = fx_market_uix[needs_fx].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        needed_inv = raw_fx_inv[needs_fx].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        needed_key = (needed_uix << 1) | needed_inv

        pair_uix = self.fx_effective_pairs[:, 0].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        pair_inv = self.fx_effective_pairs[:, 1].astype(np.int64, copy=False)
        pair_key = (pair_uix << 1) | pair_inv

        idx = np.searchsorted(pair_key, needed_key)
        # Every needed_key MUST exist in pair_key (it was deduplicated from
        # exactly this input). Validate to fail loud on contract drift.
        if idx.size and (
            idx.max() >= pair_key.size or np.any(pair_key[idx] != needed_key)
        ):
            raise RuntimeError(
                "FX-effective dedup contract violated: per-trade pair not "
                "found in fx_effective_pairs. Mapper was built from a "
                "different batch than this one."
            )

        out[needs_fx] = idx.astype(np.int32, copy=False)
        return out
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\utils\dataframes.py">
```python
from pandas import DataFrame
from pandas.api.types import is_string_dtype


def validate_string_cols(df: DataFrame, cols: tuple[str, ...] | list[str]) -> None:
    for col in cols:
        if col not in df.columns:
            continue

        s = df[col]

        if not is_string_dtype(s):
            raise ValueError(f"Column '{col}' must be of string dtype.")

        non_na = s.dropna()
        if non_na.empty:
            continue

        # Length
        max_len = non_na.str.len().max()
        if max_len > 255:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains strings longer than 255 characters "
                f"(max={max_len})."
            )

        # Canonical format
        pattern = r"^[A-Z0-9]+(?:_[A-Z0-9]+)*(?:\[(?:[A-Z]+[+-]\d+|(?:ON|TN|SN|\d+[DWMY]))\])?$"
        if not non_na.str.match(pattern).all():
            raise ValueError(
                f"Column '{col}' contains non-canonical strings. "
                f"Expected format: UPPERCASE tokens separated by underscores."
            )
```
