```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/kernels/vanilla_european_option.py
"""Pricing kernels for vanilla European options."""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

import numba as nb  # type: ignore[import-untyped]

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I8_1D,
    _I32_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricing_models.black76.model import (
    _black76_fv_with_kernel_hoists,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        # OUTPUT
        _F64_2D,  # out
        # MARKET DATA
        _F64_2D,  # forward_prices
        _F64_2D,  # vols
        _F64_2D,  # discount_factors
        _F64_2D,  # fx_spots_effective
        # BLOCK FIELDS (TRADE SPECIFICS)
        _F64_1D,  # qty
        _F64_1D,  # strike
        _I8_1D,  # cp_mult
        _F64_1D,  # sqrt_tte
        _F64_1D,  # half_tte
        # BLOCK FIELDS (LINKAGES / UIX)
        _I32_1D,  # forward_price_uix
        _I32_1D,  # vol_uix
        _I32_1D,  # fx_spot_effective_u2s_uix
        _I32_1D,  # fx_spot_effective_k2s_uix
        _I32_1D,  # df_u_exp_uix
        _I32_1D,  # df_k_exp_uix
        _I32_1D,  # df_k_settle_uix
    ),
)
def _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option(
    # OUTPUT
    out,  # (N, S)
    # MARKET DATA
    forward_prices,  # (P, S)
    vols,  # (V, S)
    discount_factors,  # (D, S)
    fx_spots_effective,  # (F, S)
    # BLOCK FIELDS (TRADE SPECIFICS)
    qty,  # (N,)
    strike,  # (N,)
    cp_mult,  # (N,)
    sqrt_tte,  # (N,)
    half_tte,  # (N,)
    # BLOCK FIELDS (LINKAGES / UIX)
    forward_price_uix,  # (N,)
    vol_uix,  # (N,)
    discount_factor_k_uix,  # (N,)
    fx_spot_effective_k2s_uix,  # (N,)
) -> None:
    m_trades, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_trades):
        # --- TRADE SPECIFICS (FOR ROW) ---
        qty_i = qty[i]
        strike_i = strike[i]
        sqrt_tte_i = sqrt_tte[i]
        half_tte_i = half_tte[i]
        cp_mult_i = cp_mult[i]

        # --- UIX (FOR ROW) ---
        forward_price_id = forward_price_uix[i]
        vol_id = vol_uix[i]
        df_k_id = discount_factor_k_uix[i]
        fx_k2s_id = fx_spot_effective_k2s_uix[i]

        # --- MARKET DATA (FOR ROW) ---
        forwards_row = forward_prices[forward_price_id]
        vols_row = vols[vol_id]
        df_k_row = discount_factors[df_k_id]
        fx_k2s_row = fx_spots_effective[fx_k2s_id]

        # --- HOISTED TRADE-SPECIFIC SCALARS (OUTSIDE SCENARIO LOOP) ---
        log_strike_i = math.log(strike_i)

        # --- PRICING ACROSS SCENARIOS (ACROSS ROW) ---
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            fwd_s = forwards_row[s]
            vol_s = vols_row[s]

            fv_s = _black76_fv_with_kernel_hoists(
                fwd_s,
                strike_i,
                log_strike_i,
                sqrt_tte_i,
                half_tte_i,
                vol_s,
                cp_mult_i,
            )

            # PV = FX RATE * DISCOUNT FACTOR * QTY * FV
            pv_s = fx_k2s_row[s] * df_k_row[s] * qty_i * fv_s

            out[i, s] = pv_s
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/kernels/vanilla_forward.py
"""
Vanilla forward pricing kernel.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numba as nb  # type: ignore[import-untyped]

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I32_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        # OUTPUT
        _F64_2D,  # out
        # MARKET DATA
        _F64_2D,  # forward_prices
        _F64_2D,  # discount_factors
        _F64_2D,  # fx_spots_effective
        # BLOCK FIELDS
        _F64_1D,  # qty
        _F64_1D,  # strike
        _I32_1D,  # forward_price_uix
        _I32_1D,  # discount_factor_u_uix
        _I32_1D,  # discount_factor_k_uix
        _I32_1D,  # fx_spot_effective_u2s_uix
        _I32_1D,  # fx_spot_effective_k2s_uix
    ),
)
def _kernel_price_vanilla_forward(
    # OUTPUT
    out,  # (N, S)
    # MARKET DATA
    forward_prices,  # (P, S)
    discount_factors,  # (D, S)
    fx_spots_effective,  # (F, S)
    # BLOCK FIELDS
    qty,  # (N,)
    strike,  # (N,)
    forward_price_uix,  # (N,)
    discount_factor_u_uix,  # (N,)
    discount_factor_k_uix,  # (N,)
    fx_spot_effective_u2s_uix,  # (N,)
    fx_spot_effective_k2s_uix,  # (N,)
):

    m_trades, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_trades):
        qty_i = qty[i]
        strike_i = strike[i]

        fwd_price_id = forward_price_uix[i]
        df_u_id = discount_factor_u_uix[i]
        df_k_id = discount_factor_k_uix[i]
        fx_u_id = fx_spot_effective_u2s_uix[i]
        fx_k_id = fx_spot_effective_k2s_uix[i]

        fwd_price_row = forward_prices[fwd_price_id]

        df_u_row = discount_factors[df_u_id]
        df_k_row = discount_factors[df_k_id]

        fx_u_row = fx_spots_effective[fx_u_id]
        fx_k_row = fx_spots_effective[fx_k_id]

        out_row = out[i]

        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            leg_u = fwd_price_row[s] * df_u_row[s] * fx_u_row[s]
            leg_k = strike_i * df_k_row[s] * fx_k_row[s]

            out_row[s] = qty_i * (leg_u - leg_k)
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricer_registry.py

"""
Registry of block pricers for the valuation engine.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any, Final, Protocol, TypeVar, cast, runtime_checkable

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

# VAULATION STATE
from riskquant.market_data.state import ValuationState

# BLOCK TYPE DATA
from riskquant.valuation.blocks._base_block import Block
from riskquant.valuation.blocks.block_type import (
    FIXED_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID,
    FLOATING_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID,
    NUM_BLOCK_TYPES,
    PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID,
    SPOT_BLOCK_TYPE_ID,
    VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_BLOCK_TYPE_ID,
    VANILLA_FORWARD_BLOCK_TYPE_ID,
    BlockType,
)

# BLOCK PRICERS
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.fixed_cashflow import (
    price_fixed_cashflow_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.floating_cashflow import (
    price_floating_cashflow_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.principal_cashflow import (
    price_principal_cashflow_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.spot import (
    price_spot_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.vanilla_european_option import (
    price_vanilla_european_option_block,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricers.vanilla_forward import (
    price_vanilla_forward_block,
)

# ==============================================================================
# PRICER PROTOCOL
# ==============================================================================

# 1. Define a contravariant TypeVar bound to Block
B_contra = TypeVar("B_contra", bound=Block, contravariant=True)


@runtime_checkable
class Pricer(Protocol[B_contra]):
    """
    Structural contract for all block pricers.
    """

    def __call__(
        self,
        *,
        block: B_contra,  # 2. Use the TypeVar instead of base Block
        valuation_state: ValuationState,
        out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
    ) -> None: ...


# ==============================================================================
# DISPATCH TABLE
# ==============================================================================

# 3. Explicitly type the list elements as Pricer[Any] | None
_BLOCK_PRICERS_MUT: list[Pricer[Any] | None] = [None] * NUM_BLOCK_TYPES


def _register_block_pricer(block_type_id: int, pricer: Pricer[Any]) -> None:
    # runtime_checkable ignores TypeVars, so this check still works natively
    if not isinstance(pricer, Pricer):
        raise TypeError(
            f"Pricer for block_type_id={block_type_id} does not satisfy the "
            f"Pricer protocol: {pricer!r}"
        )
    _BLOCK_PRICERS_MUT[block_type_id] = pricer


# --- REGISTER ALL BLOCK PRICERS HERE ---

_register_block_pricer(
    int(SPOT_BLOCK_TYPE_ID),
    price_spot_block,
)
_register_block_pricer(
    int(VANILLA_FORWARD_BLOCK_TYPE_ID),
    price_vanilla_forward_block,
)
_register_block_pricer(
    int(VANILLA_EUROPEAN_OPTION_BLOCK_TYPE_ID),
    price_vanilla_european_option_block,
)
_register_block_pricer(
    int(PRINCIPAL_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID),
    price_principal_cashflow_block,
)
_register_block_pricer(
    int(FIXED_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID),
    price_fixed_cashflow_block,
)
_register_block_pricer(
    int(FLOATING_CASHFLOW_BLOCK_TYPE_ID),
    price_floating_cashflow_block,
)

# Freeze. No mutations after module load.
# This tells the type checker: "Trust me, the runtime guard above handled the Nones."
BLOCK_PRICERS: Final[tuple[Pricer[Any], ...]] = cast(
    tuple[Pricer[Any], ...], tuple(_BLOCK_PRICERS_MUT)
)

# Hard fail at import if any block has no pricer: catches the case where
# a new Block variant is added but not wired up here.
_unregistered_blocks = [
    BlockType(i).name for i, ev in enumerate(BLOCK_PRICERS) if ev is None
]
if _unregistered_blocks:
    raise RuntimeError(
        f"No pricer registered for blocks: {_unregistered_blocks}. "
        f"Register them in registry.py."
    )


def resolve_pricer(block: Block) -> Pricer:
    """
    Resolve the pricer for a compiled block via its `BLOCK_TYPE_ID`.

    Single-array index — no `type()`, no isinstance ladder, no string keys.
    """
    pricer = BLOCK_PRICERS[block.BLOCK_TYPE_ID]
    if pricer is None:  # unreachable given the import-time guard above
        raise RuntimeError(
            f"No pricer for BLOCK_TYPE_ID={block.BLOCK_TYPE_ID} "
            f"({type(block).__name__})."
        )
    return pricer
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/vanilla_european_option_curve.py
"""
Vanilla European option pricer, curve variant.

Identical CIP-forward + Black-76 math to the node variant, with two
structural differences:

  - The underlying scalar comes from `price_curve_grid[curve_uix, delivery_uix, s]`.
  - Volatility is sourced from a 4D vol surface and requires per-scenario
    strike-axis interpolation: `np.interp` over the y-axis pillars.

The y-axis-type id determines the strike coordinate used for the
interpolation (strike, log-strike, moneyness vs forward, log-moneyness, ...).

FX rows are already EFFECTIVE — no inversion math; the strike-FX rate is
loaded once per scenario, used as a divisor for CIP forward synthesis and
as a multiplier for the PV translation back to settle ccy.

Mock-vol fallback
-----------------
When `MarketDataMatrix.vol_surfaces` is empty (e.g., calibration not yet
landed), a flat `FALLBACK_MOCK_VOLATILITY_DO_NOT_SHIP` is used. This is a
development convenience and MUST not ship in production reports.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.constants import (
    NOT_IMPLEMENTED_SENTINEL,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricer_kernels._deprecated.vanilla_european_option_curve_v1 import (
    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_curve,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.valuation.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
        VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
    )


def price_vanilla_european_option_curve_block(
    *,
    block: VanillaEuropeanOptionCurveBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """European-option-curve pricer entrypoint."""
    if np.any(block.vol_surface_y_axis_type_id == np.int8(NOT_IMPLEMENTED_SENTINEL)):
        raise NotImplementedError(
            "vol_surface_y_axis_type_id contains unresolved sentinel values. "
            "CompiledIngestSoA must supply vol_surface_y_axis_type_ids before "
            "vanilla European option curve trades can be priced."
        )

    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_curve(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.price_curve_grid,
        market_data_mtx.vol_surface_strikes,
        market_data_mtx.vol_surfaces,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        market_data_mtx.discount_factor_grid,
        market_data_mtx.tte_grid,
        block.qty,
        block.strike,
        block.cp_mult,
        block.underlying_price_curve_uix,
        block.vol_surface_uix,
        block.vol_surface_y_axis_type_id,
        block.fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.under_discount_curve_uix,
        block.fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.strike_discount_curve_uix,
        block.delivery_date_uix,
        block.expiry_date_uix,
        block.settlement_date_uix,
    )
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/vanilla_european_option_node.py
"""
Vanilla European option pricer, node variant.

Synthesises the forward in strike currency via Covered Interest Parity,
prices via Black-76, then discounts and FX-translates back to settle ccy.

Math (per trade i, scenario s)
------------------------------
    rate_k = fx_spots_effective[fx_k_eff_uix(i), s]   # k_ccy -> settle_ccy

    # CIP forward in strike currency
    fwd_strike_ccy
        = forward_node[p, s]
          * fx_spots_effective[fx_u_eff_uix(i), s]    # u_ccy -> settle_ccy
          * df_grid[df_u_uix(i), expiry_uix(i), s]    # under-DF at expiry
          / rate_k                                    # settle_ccy -> k_ccy
          / df_grid[df_k_uix(i), expiry_uix(i), s]    # strike-DF at expiry

    # Black-76 future value (cp_mult = +1 call, -1 put)
    sigma = vol_nodes[v, s]
    std_dev = max(sigma * sqrt(tte), eps)
    d1 = (log(fwd / k) + 0.5 * sigma**2 * tte) / std_dev
    d2 = d1 - std_dev
    fv = cp * (fwd * N(cp * d1) - k * N(cp * d2))

    # PV discount in strike ccy, then translate back to settle ccy
    pv_strike_ccy = fv * df_grid[df_k_uix(i), settle_uix(i), s]
    pv_settle_ccy = pv_strike_ccy * rate_k

    out[i, s] = qty[i] * pv_settle_ccy

FX rows are already EFFECTIVE in `MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective`; the
kernel does NO inversion math. The strike-FX rate `rate_k` is loaded once
per scenario and used as a divisor in the forward synthesis and as a
multiplier in the PV translation — equivalent counts to the legacy code
but with the `(1.0 / rate_k) if inv_k else rate_k` gymnastics gone.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.pricer_kernels._deprecated.vanilla_european_option_node_v1 import (
    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_node,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.valuation.blocks.vanilla_european_option_block import (
        VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
    )


def price_vanilla_european_option_node_block(
    *,
    block: VanillaEuropeanOptionNodeBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """European-option-node pricer entrypoint."""
    _kernel_price_vanilla_european_option_node(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.forward_nodes,
        market_data_mtx.vol_nodes,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        market_data_mtx.discount_factor_grid,
        market_data_mtx.sqrt_tte_grid,
        market_data_mtx.half_tte_grid,
        block.qty,
        block.strike,
        block.cp_mult,
        block.underlying_price_uix,
        block.vol_uix,
        block.fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.under_discount_curve_uix,
        block.fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.strike_discount_curve_uix,
        block.expiry_date_uix,
        block.settlement_date_uix,
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/vanilla_forward_node.py
"""
Vanilla forward pricer, node variant.

    leg_u = forward_node * fx_u_effective * df_u(stl)
    leg_k = strike       * fx_k_effective * df_k(stl)
    PV    = qty * (leg_u - leg_k)

FX rows are already EFFECTIVE (direct or pre-inverted) inside
`MarketDataMatrix.fx_spots_effective` — pricer kernels do NO inversion math.
Each FX gather is one indexed read + one multiply per scenario.

Optional legs (FX / DF) are signalled by `INVALID_UIX (< 0)` in the
corresponding `_uix` field. These predicates are trade-level invariants;
Numba's LICM hoists them outside the inner scenario loop, so the inner loop
streams straight-line arithmetic over C-contiguous memory.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.pricer_kernels._deprecated.vanilla_forward_node_v1 import (
    _kernel_price_vanilla_forward_node,
)

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import MarketDataMatrix
    from riskquant.valuation.blocks.vanilla_forward_block import (
        VanillaForwardNodeBlock,
    )


def price_vanilla_forward_node_block(
    *,
    block: VanillaForwardNodeBlock,
    market_data_mtx: MarketDataMatrix,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Forward-node pricer entrypoint."""
    _kernel_price_vanilla_forward_node(
        out,
        market_data_mtx.forward_nodes,
        market_data_mtx.fx_spots_effective,
        market_data_mtx.discount_factor_grid,
        block.qty,
        block.strike,
        block.price_uix,
        block.fx_under_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.under_discount_curve_uix,
        block.fx_strike_to_settle_effective_uix,
        block.strike_discount_curve_uix,
        block.settlement_date_uix,
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/fixed_cashflow.py
"""
Fixed cashflow pricer.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt
```

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricers/spot.py
"""
Spot pricer.

PV = qty * spot_price * fx_effective

`fx_spots_effective` rows are already the resolved transform (direct or
pre-inverted) — pricer kernels do no inversion math. Per-trade FX is one
gather + one multiply per scenario. The `fx_effective_uix < 0` sentinel
selects the "no FX" branch which is hoisted outside the scenario loop.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import TYPE_CHECKING

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt

from riskquant.valuation.pricer_kernels.spot import _kernel_price_spot

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from riskquant.market_data.state import ValuationState
    from riskquant.valuation.blocks.spot_block import SpotBlock


def price_spot_block(
    *,
    block: SpotBlock,
    valuation_state: ValuationState,
    out: npt.NDArray[np.float64],
) -> None:
    """Spot pricer entrypoint. All arrays must be C-contiguous and dtype-stable."""
    _kernel_price_spot(
        # OUTPUT
        out,
        # MARKET DATA
        valuation_state.spot_prices,
        valuation_state.fx_spots_effective,
        # BLOCK FIELDS
        block.qty,
        block.spot_price_uix,
        block.fx_spot_effective_uix,
    )
```

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricing_models/black76/api.py
"""
Public API for the Black-76 pricing model.

This module provides user-facing entry points for evaluating the
Black-76 model.

Architectural role
------------------
The API layer sits above the model-evaluation kernels and provides a
stable, convenient interface for callers that wish to evaluate the
Black-76 model directly.

Responsibilities include:

    * allocating output arrays
    * exposing user-friendly function signatures
    * input validation
    * dispatching to optimized batch kernels

The API intentionally hides implementation details such as:

    * Numba kernels
    * memory-layout requirements
    * internal helper functions

Relationship to the valuation framework
---------------------------------------
The functions in this module evaluate the Black-76 model directly and
are independent of RiskQuant's portfolio valuation architecture.

They do not require:

    * Blocks
    * ValuationState
    * Compiled portfolios

Typical consumers include:

    * calibration routines
    * market-data tooling
    * research notebooks
    * standalone pricing workflows
    * testing and validation

Portfolio valuation should generally occur through product-specific
valuation kernels rather than through this API.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
from numpy.typing import NDArray

from riskquant._arrays import _as_c_contig_bool, _as_c_contig_f64
from riskquant.valuation.pricing_models.black76.kernels import _kernel_black76_fv_batch


def price_black76_fv_batch(
    *,
    forwards: NDArray[np.float64],
    vols: NDArray[np.float64],
    strike: NDArray[np.float64],
    tte: NDArray[np.float64],
    is_call: NDArray[np.bool_],
) -> NDArray[np.float64]:
    """
    Price a batch of options under the Black 76 model. Returns FUTURE VALUE (FV) of the option, not PV.
    The caller is responsible for multiplying by the discount factor to get PV.

    Preconditions
    -------------
    The caller guarantees:

        forward > 0
        strike > 0
        volatility > 0
        tte > 0
    """

    # --- CONTIGUITY ENFORCEMENT ---

    forwards = _as_c_contig_f64(forwards)
    vols = _as_c_contig_f64(vols)
    strike = _as_c_contig_f64(strike)
    tte = _as_c_contig_f64(tte)
    is_call = _as_c_contig_bool(is_call)

    # --- SHAPE VALIDATION ---

    # Market data arrays are 2D (num_options, batch_size).

    if forwards.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError("forwards must be a 2D array")

    if vols.ndim != 2:
        raise ValueError("vols must be a 2D array")

    # Trade-specific arrays are 1D (num_options,).

    if strike.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("strike must be a 1D array")

    if tte.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("tte must be a 1D array")

    if is_call.ndim != 1:
        raise ValueError("is_call must be a 1D array")

    m_options, s_scenarios = forwards.shape

    if vols.shape != (m_options, s_scenarios):
        raise ValueError("vols must have the same shape as forwards")

    if strike.shape != (m_options,):
        raise ValueError("strike must have the same number of rows as forwards")

    if tte.shape != (m_options,):
        raise ValueError("tte must have the same number of rows as forwards")

    if is_call.shape != (m_options,):
        raise ValueError("is_call must have the same number of rows as forwards")

    # --- VALUES VALIDATION ---

    if np.any(forwards <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("forwards must be strictly positive")

    if np.any(vols <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("vols must be strictly positive")

    if np.any(strike <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("strike must be strictly positive")

    if np.any(tte <= 0.0):
        raise ValueError("tte must be strictly positive")

    # --- FINITE CHECKS ---

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(forwards)):
        raise ValueError("forwards must be finite")

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(vols)):
        raise ValueError("vols must be finite")

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(strike)):
        raise ValueError("strike must be finite")

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(tte)):
        raise ValueError("tte must be finite")

    # --- KERNEL DISPATCH ---

    cp_mult = np.where(
        is_call,
        np.int8(1),
        np.int8(-1),
    )
    sqrt_tte = np.sqrt(tte)
    half_tte = 0.5 * tte

    out = np.empty((m_options, s_scenarios), dtype=np.float64)

    _kernel_black76_fv_batch(
        # OUTPUT
        out,
        # MARKET DATA
        forwards,
        vols,
        # TRADE SPECIFICS
        strike,
        sqrt_tte,
        half_tte,
        cp_mult,
    )

    return out
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\valuation\pricing_models\black76\kernels.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricing_models/black76/kernels.py
"""
High-throughput Black-76 model-evaluation kernels.

This module contains batch-oriented Numba kernels that evaluate the
Black-76 model across many instruments and/or scenarios.

Architectural role
------------------
These kernels are model-evaluation kernels, not product-valuation
kernels.

They operate directly on arrays of Black-76 inputs:

    * forwards
    * strikes
    * volatilities
    * times to expiry

and produce arrays of Black-76 outputs.

They do not know about:

    * portfolios
    * blocks
    * valuation states
    * settlement conventions
    * FX conversion
    * discount-factor construction
    * market-data compilation

Typical consumers include:

    * pricing-model APIs
    * volatility-surface calibration
    * implied-volatility stripping
    * research tooling
    * benchmarking

Product valuation kernels should generally call the scalar functions in
`model.py` directly rather than routing through these batch kernels.

These kernels exist to provide efficient bulk evaluation of the Black-76
model outside the portfolio valuation framework.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

import numba as nb  # type: ignore[import-untyped]

from riskquant.numba import (
    _F64_1D,
    _F64_2D,
    _I8_1D,
    parallel_njit,
)
from riskquant.valuation.pricing_models.black76.model import (
    _black76_fv_with_kernel_hoists,
)


@parallel_njit(
    nb.void(
        # OUTPUT
        _F64_2D,  # out
        # MARKET DATA
        _F64_2D,  # forwards
        _F64_2D,  # vols
        # TRADE SPECIFICS
        _F64_1D,  # strike
        _F64_1D,  # sqrt_tte
        _F64_1D,  # half_tte
        _I8_1D,  # cp_mult
    ),
)
def _kernel_black76_fv_batch(
    # OUTPUT
    out,  # (m, S)
    # MARKET DATA
    forwards,  # (m, S)
    vols,  # (m, S)
    # TRADE SPECIFICS
    strike,  # (m, )
    sqrt_tte,  # (m, )
    half_tte,  # (m, )
    cp_mult,  # (m,)
):
    """
    Black 76 pricing kernel. Returns FUTURE VALUE (FV) of the option, not PV.
    The caller is responsible for multiplying by the discount factor to get PV.

    Preconditions
    -------------
    The caller guarantees:

        forward > 0
        strike > 0
        volatility > 0
        sqrt_tte > 0

    Violating these invariants results in undefined behaviour.

    Validation belongs in the compiler layer, not the pricing kernel.

    Arguments
    ---------
    out : np.ndarray, shape (m, S)
        Output array to write the FV of the option for each trade and scenario.
    forwards : np.ndarray, shape (m, S)
        Forward prices for each trade and scenario.
    vols : np.ndarray, shape (m, S)
        Volatilities for each trade and scenario.
    strike : np.ndarray, shape (m,)
        Strike prices for each trade.
    sqrt_tte : np.ndarray, shape (m,)
        Square root of time to expiry for each trade.
    half_tte : np.ndarray, shape (m,)
        Half of the time to expiry for each trade.
    cp_mult : np.ndarray, shape (m,)
        Call/put multiplier for each trade.

    """

    m_trades, s_scenarios = out.shape

    for i in nb.prange(m_trades):
        # --- TRADE SPECIFICS (FOR ROW) ---
        strike_i = strike[i]
        sqrt_tte_i = sqrt_tte[i]
        half_tte_i = half_tte[i]
        cp_mult_i = cp_mult[i]

        # --- MARKET DATA (FOR ROW) ---
        forwards_row = forwards[i]
        vols_row = vols[i]

        # --- HOISTED TRADE-SPECIFIC SCALARS (OUTSIDE SCENARIO LOOP) ---
        log_strike_i = math.log(strike_i)

        # --- PRICING ACROSS SCENARIOS (ACROSS ROW) ---
        for s in range(s_scenarios):
            fwd_s = forwards_row[s]
            vol_s = vols_row[s]

            fv_s = _black76_fv_with_kernel_hoists(
                fwd_s,
                strike_i,
                log_strike_i,
                sqrt_tte_i,
                half_tte_i,
                vol_s,
                cp_mult_i,
            )

            out[i, s] = fv_s
```
</file>

Scalar Black-76 mathematical primitives.

This module contains the core Black-76 pri...
functions: _black76_fv, _black76_fv_call, _black76_fv_put, _black76_fv_vega, _black76_fv_with_kernel_hoists

<file path="riskquant\src\riskquant\valuation\pricing_models\black76\model.py">
```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricing_models/black76/model.py
"""
Black 76 pricing model primitives.

Scalar Black-76 mathematical primitives.

This module contains the core Black-76 pricing model expressed as
small, allocation-free functions operating on scalar inputs.

Architectural role
------------------
Functions in this module represent Black-76 mathematics only.

They do not know about:

    * portfolios
    * valuation states
    * blocks
    * market-data containers
    * discounting conventions
    * FX conversion
    * settlement mechanics
    * batch execution

They are intended to be:

    * reusable
    * inline-able
    * allocation-free
    * independent of the wider RiskQuant valuation framework

Typical consumers include:

    * product valuation kernels
    * model-evaluation kernels
    * calibration routines
    * implied-volatility solvers
    * research tooling

The functions in this module form the single source of truth for
Black-76 mathematics throughout RiskQuant.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

from riskquant.numba import inline_njit
from riskquant.valuation.pricing_models.math import (
    numba_norm_cdf_abrom_steg,
    numba_norm_pdf,
)

# =======================
# PRICING
# =======================


@inline_njit
def _black76_fv_with_kernel_hoists(
    fwd: float,
    strike: float,
    log_strike: float,
    sqrt_tte: float,
    half_tte: float,
    vol: float,
    cp_mult: int,
) -> float:
    """Undiscounted Black 76 option price. Returns FV, not PV. Caller is responsible
    for multiplying by the discount factor to get PV."""

    log_fk = math.log(fwd) - log_strike

    std_dev = vol * sqrt_tte

    d1 = (log_fk + vol * vol * half_tte) / std_dev
    d2 = d1 - std_dev

    cdf_d1 = numba_norm_cdf_abrom_steg(cp_mult * d1)
    cdf_d2 = numba_norm_cdf_abrom_steg(cp_mult * d2)

    fv = cp_mult * (fwd * cdf_d1 - strike * cdf_d2)

    return fv


@inline_njit
def _black76_fv(
    fwd: float,
    strike: float,
    sqrt_tte: float,
    half_tte: float,
    vol: float,
    cp_mult: int,
) -> float:
    """Undiscounted Black 76 option price. Returns FV, not PV. Caller is responsible
    for multiplying by the discount factor to get PV."""
    return _black76_fv_with_kernel_hoists(
        fwd,
        strike,
        math.log(strike),
        sqrt_tte,
        half_tte,
        vol,
        cp_mult,
    )


@inline_njit
def _black76_fv_call(
    fwd: float,
    strike: float,
    sqrt_tte: float,
    half_tte: float,
    vol: float,
) -> float:
    """Undiscounted Black 76 call price. Returns FV, not PV. Caller is
    responsible for multiplying by the discount factor to get PV."""
    return _black76_fv(fwd, strike, sqrt_tte, half_tte, vol, 1)


@inline_njit
def _black76_fv_put(
    fwd: float,
    strike: float,
    sqrt_tte: float,
    half_tte: float,
    vol: float,
) -> float:
    """Undiscounted Black 76 put price. Returns FV, not PV. Caller is
    responsible for multiplying by the discount factor to get PV."""
    return _black76_fv(fwd, strike, sqrt_tte, half_tte, vol, -1)


# ======================
# SENSITIVITIES
# ======================

# --- DELTA ---

# TODO: Implement Black-76 delta kernel.

# --- GAMMA ---

# TODO: Implement Black-76 gamma kernel.

# --- VEGA ---


@inline_njit
def _black76_fv_vega(
    fwd: float,
    strike: float,
    sqrt_tte: float,
    half_tte: float,
    vol: float,
) -> float:
    """
    Undiscounted Black-76 vega: dC/dvol = F * phi(d1) * sqrt(T).

    Returns 0.0 for non-positive vol or tte. This makes vega the natural
    quality weight on a calibrated IV: it is 0 for failed solves (sentinel
    vol < 0) and for at-intrinsic zero-vol solves, tiny in flat-vega regions
    (deep ITM/OTM, ultra-short tenors) where the IV is under-determined, and
    large where the inversion is well-conditioned.
    """
    if vol <= 0.0 or sqrt_tte <= 0.0:
        return 0.0

    log_fk = math.log(fwd) - math.log(strike)

    std_dev = vol * sqrt_tte

    d1 = (log_fk + vol * vol * half_tte) / std_dev

    pdf_d1 = numba_norm_pdf(d1)

    return fwd * pdf_d1 * sqrt_tte
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/pricing_models/math.py
"""
Shared bare-metal math helpers for vectorized pricer kernels.

Tiny hot-path functions: `@nb.njit(inline='always')` collapses them into
the surrounding kernel at compile time, so the calling kernel sees fused
code with no call-frame overhead.

Anything that needs `prange` or more than a handful of ops should not
live here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

from riskquant.numba import inline_njit

# 1 / sqrt(2). Lifted so the kernel never recomputes it.
_INV_SQRT_2: float = 0.7071067811865476

# 1 / sqrt(2 * pi). Lifted so the kernel never recomputes it.
_INV_SQRT_2PI: float = 0.39894228040143267794


@inline_njit
def numba_norm_cdf(x: float) -> float:
    """Standard normal CDF: Phi(x) = 0.5 * (1 + erf(x / sqrt(2)))."""
    return 0.5 * (1.0 + math.erf(x * _INV_SQRT_2))


@inline_njit
def numba_norm_pdf(x: float) -> float:
    """Standard normal PDF: phi(x) = exp(-0.5 * x^2) / sqrt(2 * pi)."""
    return _INV_SQRT_2PI * math.exp(-0.5 * x * x)


@inline_njit
def numba_norm_cdf_abrom_steg(x: float) -> float:

    ax = abs(x)

    t = 1.0 / (1.0 + 0.2316419 * ax)

    poly = t * (
        0.319381530
        + t * (-0.356563782 + t * (1.781477937 + t * (-1.821255978 + t * 1.330274429)))
    )

    pdf = 0.39894228040143267794 * math.exp(-0.5 * ax * ax)

    cdf = 1.0 - pdf * poly

    if x < 0.0:
        return 1.0 - cdf

    return cdf
```
</file>

```python
# src/riskquant/valuation/result.py
"""
Valuation results container.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass

import numpy as np
import numpy.typing as npt


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True, kw_only=True)
class ValuationResult:
    trade_uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]  # (n_trades,)
    values: npt.NDArray[np.float64]  # (n_trades, n_scenarios)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if self.values.shape[0] != self.trade_uids.shape[0]:
            raise ValueError("UIDs and values row mismatch")

    @property
    def num_trades(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[0]

    @property
    def num_scenarios(self) -> int:
        return self.values.shape[1]

    def get_trade_values(self, uid: int) -> npt.NDArray[np.float64]:
        idx = np.searchsorted(self.trade_uids, uid)
        if self.trade_uids[idx] != uid:
            raise KeyError(uid)
        return self.values[idx]

    def select(self, uids: npt.NDArray[np.int64]) -> "ValuationResult":
        idx = np.isin(self.trade_uids, uids)
        return ValuationResult(
            trade_uids=self.trade_uids[idx],
            values=self.values[idx],
        )
```

<file path="riskquant\tests\market_data\calibrate\implied_vol\black76\test_calibrate.py">
```python
# tests/market_data/calibrate/implied_vol/black76/test_calibrate.py
"""
Correctness tests for the Black-76 implied-volatility calibration path.

These exercise the *real* compiled kernels (numba required), cross-checked
against an independent SciPy reference. Runs under pytest, or standalone via
``python test_calibrate.py``.

Reference model
---------------
Undiscounted Black-76 call: C = F*Phi(d1) - K*Phi(d2),
    d1 = (ln(F/K) + 0.5*sig^2*T) / (sig*sqrt(T)),  d2 = d1 - sig*sqrt(T).
Undiscounted vega: dC/dvol = F * phi(d1) * sqrt(T).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import brentq
from scipy.stats import norm

from riskquant.constants import IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL, MIN_RELIABLE_VEGA_OVER_FWD
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.implied_vol.black76.calibrate import (
    _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays,
    calibrate_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.master import (
    CalibratedOptionQuotes,
    CanonicalOptionQuotes,
)

_LOWER_VOL = 1e-8
_UPPER_VOL = 10.0


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- refs
def _b76_undisc(fwd, strike, tte, vol):
    fwd = np.asarray(fwd, float)
    strike = np.asarray(strike, float)
    tte = np.asarray(tte, float)
    vol = np.asarray(vol, float)
    vst = vol * np.sqrt(tte)
    d1 = (np.log(fwd / strike) + 0.5 * vol * vol * tte) / vst
    d2 = d1 - vst
    return fwd * norm.cdf(d1) - strike * norm.cdf(d2)


def _vega_ref(fwd, strike, tte, vol):
    """Reference undiscounted vega; 0 where vol <= 0 (failed / zero-vol)."""
    fwd = np.asarray(fwd, float)
    strike = np.asarray(strike, float)
    tte = np.asarray(tte, float)
    vol = np.asarray(vol, float)
    out = np.zeros(np.broadcast(fwd, strike, tte, vol).shape, float)
    m = vol > 0.0
    if np.any(m):
        st = np.sqrt(tte * np.ones_like(out))[m]
        d1 = (
            np.log((fwd / strike) * np.ones_like(out))[m]
            + 0.5 * (vol * vol * tte * np.ones_like(out))[m]
        ) / ((vol * np.ones_like(out))[m] * st)
        out[m] = (fwd * np.ones_like(out))[m] * norm.pdf(d1) * st
    return out


def _scipy_iv(fwd, strike, tte, target):
    f = lambda v: float(_b76_undisc(fwd, strike, tte, v)) - target
    return brentq(f, _LOWER_VOL, _UPPER_VOL, xtol=1e-12, rtol=1e-12, maxiter=200)


def _vega_over_fwd(fwd, strike, tte, vol):
    st = math.sqrt(tte)
    d1 = (math.log(fwd / strike) + 0.5 * vol * vol * tte) / (vol * st)
    return math.exp(-0.5 * d1 * d1) / math.sqrt(2.0 * math.pi) * st


_FWDS = (0.5, 1.0, 50.0, 100.0, 4000.0)
_MONEYNESS = (0.6, 0.8, 0.95, 1.0, 1.05, 1.25, 1.6)
_TTES = (1.0 / 365.0, 0.05, 0.25, 1.0, 5.0)
_VOLS = (0.01, 0.05, 0.20, 0.80, 2.5)


def _build_grid():
    """Solvable (F, K, T, target, true_vol) tuples (above intrinsic, below fwd)."""
    rows = []
    for F in _FWDS:
        for m in _MONEYNESS:
            K = F * m
            for T in _TTES:
                for sig in _VOLS:
                    target = float(_b76_undisc(F, K, T, sig))
                    intrinsic = max(F - K, 0.0)
                    if target <= intrinsic + 1e-12 * max(F, K, 1.0):
                        continue
                    if target > F:
                        continue
                    rows.append((F, K, T, target, sig))
    return rows


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- tests
def test_grid_reprices():
    """Every solved IV must reprice to its target (price round-trip)."""
    rows = _build_grid()
    F = np.array([r[0] for r in rows])
    K = np.array([r[1] for r in rows])
    T = np.array([r[2] for r in rows])
    tgt = np.array([r[3] for r in rows])

    iv, vega = _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays(F, K, T, tgt)
    assert np.all(iv >= 0.0), "no quote in this grid should fail to invert"

    reprice = _b76_undisc(F, K, T, iv)
    rel = np.abs(reprice - tgt) / np.maximum(tgt, 1.0)
    # solver vol tolerance is 1e-8; the implied price residual stays << 1e-5
    assert rel.max() < 1e-5, f"max rel reprice error {rel.max():.3e}"

    assert not np.isnan(vega).any()
    assert np.all(vega >= 0.0)


def test_scipy_vol_parity_well_conditioned():
    """Where vega is healthy, the kernel IV matches SciPy brentq to ~1e-6."""
    worst = 0.0
    n = 0
    for F, K, T, target, sig in _build_grid():
        if _vega_over_fwd(F, K, T, sig) <= 1e-2:
            continue
        iv, _ = _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays(
            np.array([F]), np.array([K]), np.array([T]), np.array([target])
        )
        ref = _scipy_iv(F, K, T, target)
        worst = max(worst, abs(float(iv[0]) - ref))
        n += 1
    assert n > 0
    assert worst < 1e-6, f"max |kernel - scipy| vol = {worst:.3e}"


def test_vega_matches_reference():
    """Kernel vega matches the closed-form reference at the solved iv."""
    rows = _build_grid()
    F = np.array([r[0] for r in rows])
    K = np.array([r[1] for r in rows])
    T = np.array([r[2] for r in rows])
    tgt = np.array([r[3] for r in rows])

    iv, vega = _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays(F, K, T, tgt)
    ref = _vega_ref(F, K, T, iv)
    rel = np.abs(vega - ref) / np.maximum(ref, 1.0)
    assert rel.max() < 1e-9, f"max rel vega error {rel.max():.3e}"


def test_rotation_regression():
    """
    Guard for the NR-zbrent rotation bug: OTM / short-dated quotes that the
    pre-fix solver resolved to the lower bracket bound (non-repricing). These
    must now invert and reprice.
    """
    cases = [
        (4000.0, 6400.0, 1.0, 0.80),  # deep OTM, 1y
        (4000.0, 6400.0, 5.0, 0.20),  # deep OTM, 5y
        (4000.0, 5000.0, 5.0, 0.05),  # OTM, low vol, long T
        (1.0, 1.6, 0.25, 0.20),  # OTM small notional
        (100.0, 110.0, 1.0 / 365.0, 0.80),  # OTM, 1 day, resolvable time value
    ]
    for F, K, T, sig in cases:
        target = float(_b76_undisc(F, K, T, sig))
        iv, _ = _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays(
            np.array([F]), np.array([K]), np.array([T]), np.array([target])
        )
        v = float(iv[0])
        assert v > _LOWER_VOL, f"regressed to bracket bound: {F, K, T, sig} -> {v}"
        reprice = float(_b76_undisc(F, K, T, v))
        assert abs(reprice - target) / max(target, 1.0) < 1e-5


def test_failure_regimes():
    F = np.array([100.0, 100.0, 100.0])
    K = np.array([90.0, 90.0, 90.0])
    T = np.array([1.0, 1.0, 1.0])
    # [0] target above forward -> no solution
    # [1] target below intrinsic -> arbitrage -> no solution
    # [2] target at intrinsic -> valid zero-vol solve
    tgt = np.array([F[0] + 1.0, 5.0, 10.0])
    iv, vega = _calibrate_black76_iv_arrays(F, K, T, tgt)

    assert iv[0] == IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL
    assert iv[1] == IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL
    assert iv[2] == 0.0  # at-intrinsic is a SUCCESS, not a failure
    assert not np.isnan(iv).any(), "failures must use the sentinel, never NaN"

    # vega is 0 for failed AND for at-intrinsic zero-vol solves
    assert np.all(vega == 0.0)


def test_master_contract():
    rows = _build_grid()
    # inject two guaranteed failures (target above forward) and one at-intrinsic
    # success (target == intrinsic -> iv 0.0, mask True, vega 0.0)
    F = np.array([r[0] for r in rows] + [100.0, 50.0, 100.0])
    K = np.array([r[1] for r in rows] + [90.0, 40.0, 90.0])
    T = np.array([r[2] for r in rows] + [1.0, 1.0, 1.0])
    undisc = np.array(
        [r[3] for r in rows] + [200.0, 100.0, 10.0]
    )  # fail, fail, intrinsic

    quotes = CanonicalOptionQuotes(
        fwd_price=F, strike=K, tte=T, undiscounted_call_price=undisc
    )
    out = calibrate_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices(quotes)

    assert isinstance(out, CalibratedOptionQuotes)
    assert out.n_quotes == F.shape[0]
    assert out.n_solved + out.n_failed == out.n_quotes
    assert out.n_failed >= 2
    assert not np.isnan(out.iv).any()
    # iv-sign contract
    assert np.array_equal(out.success_mask, out.iv >= 0.0)
    assert np.all(out.iv[~out.success_mask] == IV_SOLVER_FAILED_SENTINEL)
    assert np.all(out.iv[out.success_mask] >= 0.0)

    # vega contract: non-negative, finite, zero on failure (but NOT necessarily
    # nonzero on success -- at-intrinsic zero-vol is a success with zero vega)
    assert not np.isnan(out.vega).any()
    assert np.all(out.vega >= 0.0)
    assert np.all(out.vega[~out.success_mask] == 0.0)
    intrinsic_success = out.success_mask & (out.iv == 0.0)
    assert np.any(intrinsic_success)
    assert np.all(out.vega[intrinsic_success] == 0.0)


def test_reliable_mask_gates_flat_vega():
    """reliable_mask keeps well-conditioned quotes and drops flat-vega ones."""
    # ATM, healthy vega
    good = (100.0, 100.0, 1.0, 0.20)
    # deep OTM ultra-short, near-zero vega (under-determined iv)
    flat = (100.0, 150.0, 1.0 / 365.0, 0.20)

    F = np.array([good[0], flat[0]])
    K = np.array([good[1], flat[1]])
    T = np.array([good[2], flat[2]])
    undisc = np.array([float(_b76_undisc(*good)), float(_b76_undisc(*flat))])
    quotes = CanonicalOptionQuotes(
        fwd_price=F, strike=K, tte=T, undiscounted_call_price=undisc
    )
    out = calibrate_black76_iv_from_undiscounted_call_prices(quotes)
    rel = out.reliable_mask()

    assert rel.dtype == np.bool_
    assert np.all(rel <= out.success_mask)  # reliable implies solved
    assert rel[0], "ATM healthy-vega quote should be reliable"
    # flat-vega quote: either failed or vega below the floor -> not reliable
    assert not rel[1], "flat-vega quote should not be reliable"
    # default floor is the centralized constant
    assert MIN_RELIABLE_VEGA_OVER_FWD > 0.0


def _run_all():
    fns = [v for k, v in sorted(globals().items()) if k.startswith("test_")]
    for fn in fns:
        fn()
        print(f"PASS {fn.__name__}")
    print(f"\nALL {len(fns)} TESTS PASSED")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _run_all()
```
</file>

<file path="riskquant\tests\market_data\calibrate\option_quotes\test_option_quotes.py">
```python
# tests/market_data/calibrate/option_quotes/test_option_quotes.py
"""
Tests for the option-quotes edge: no-arbitrage validation and canonical
compilation (put-call parity / undiscounting, trust-the-master dedup).

Plain-assert, pytest-discoverable, or standalone via ``python test_option_quotes.py``.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.compile import (
    _compile_canonical_option_quotes_arrays,
    compile_canonical_option_quotes,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.master import (
    CanonicalOptionQuotes,
    ValidatedOptionQuotes,
)
from riskquant.market_data.calibrate.option_quotes.validate import (
    validate_option_quotes,
)

_CALL = 1
_PUT = 0


def _assert_raises(fn, substr):
    try:
        fn()
    except ValueError as e:
        assert substr in str(e), f"wrong message: {e!r}"
        return
    raise AssertionError(f"expected ValueError containing {substr!r}")


# --------------------------------------------------------------- validation
def test_validate_accepts_valid_calls_and_puts():
    F = np.array([100.0, 100.0, 100.0])
    K = np.array([100.0, 80.0, 120.0])
    T = np.array([1.0, 1.0, 1.0])
    flag = np.array([_CALL, _CALL, _PUT], dtype=np.int8)
    df = np.array([0.98, 0.98, 0.98])
    # all strictly inside the no-arbitrage band (row 2 is an ITM put,
    # intrinsic = K - F = 20, so its price must exceed ~df*20 = 19.6)
    price = np.array([10.0, 25.0, 25.0])

    out = validate_option_quotes(F, K, T, flag, df, price)
    assert isinstance(out, ValidatedOptionQuotes)
    assert out.n_quotes == 3
    assert out.fwd_price.dtype == np.float64
    assert out.call_put_flag.dtype == np.int8


def test_validate_rejects_call_above_forward():
    _assert_raises(
        lambda: validate_option_quotes(
            [100.0], [100.0], [1.0], np.array([_CALL], np.int8), [0.98], [99.0]
        ),
        "upper no-arbitrage",
    )


def test_validate_rejects_call_below_intrinsic():
    # ITM call: discounted intrinsic = 0.98 * (100 - 80) = 19.6; price 10 < that
    _assert_raises(
        lambda: validate_option_quotes(
            [100.0], [80.0], [1.0], np.array([_CALL], np.int8), [0.98], [10.0]
        ),
        "lower no-arbitrage",
    )


def test_validate_rejects_put_above_strike():
    # put upper bound = df * K = 98; price 99 > that
    _assert_raises(
        lambda: validate_option_quotes(
            [100.0], [100.0], [1.0], np.array([_PUT], np.int8), [0.98], [99.0]
        ),
        "upper no-arbitrage",
    )


def test_validate_rejects_put_below_intrinsic():
    # ITM put F<K: discounted intrinsic = 0.98 * (100 - 80) = 19.6; price 10 < that
    _assert_raises(
        lambda: validate_option_quotes(
            [80.0], [100.0], [1.0], np.array([_PUT], np.int8), [0.98], [10.0]
        ),
        "lower no-arbitrage",
    )


def test_validate_accepts_boundaries():
    # exactly at discounted intrinsic (lower) and at df*F / df*K (upper)
    F = np.array([100.0, 100.0])
    K = np.array([100.0, 100.0])
    T = np.array([1.0, 1.0])
    flag = np.array([_CALL, _PUT], dtype=np.int8)
    df = np.array([0.98, 0.98])
    price = np.array([0.0, 98.0])  # ATM call at intrinsic 0; put at df*K
    out = validate_option_quotes(F, K, T, flag, df, price)
    assert out.n_quotes == 2


def test_validate_error_lists_offending_rows():
    # rows 1 and 3 violate the upper bound
    F = np.array([100.0, 100.0, 100.0, 100.0])
    K = np.array([100.0, 100.0, 100.0, 100.0])
    T = np.array([1.0, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0])
    flag = np.array([_CALL, _CALL, _CALL, _CALL], dtype=np.int8)
    df = np.array([0.98, 0.98, 0.98, 0.98])
    price = np.array([10.0, 99.0, 10.0, 99.0])
    try:
        validate_option_quotes(F, K, T, flag, df, price)
    except ValueError as e:
        msg = str(e)
        assert "2 quote(s)" in msg, msg
        assert "1" in msg and "3" in msg, msg
        return
    raise AssertionError("expected ValueError")


# --------------------------------------------------------------- compilation
def _expected_undisc_call(F, K, flag, df, price):
    undisc = price / df
    return np.where(flag == _CALL, undisc, undisc + F - K)


def test_compile_putcall_parity_and_undiscount():
    F = np.array([100.0, 100.0, 50.0])
    K = np.array([100.0, 120.0, 40.0])
    T = np.array([1.0, 0.5, 2.0])
    flag = np.array([_CALL, _PUT, _PUT], dtype=np.int8)
    df = np.array([0.98, 0.97, 0.95])
    price = np.array([10.0, 18.0, 12.0])

    quotes = ValidatedOptionQuotes(
        fwd_price=F,
        strike=K,
        tte=T,
        call_put_flag=flag,
        discount_factor=df,
        option_price=price,
    )
    out = compile_canonical_option_quotes(quotes)
    assert isinstance(out, CanonicalOptionQuotes)
    expected = _expected_undisc_call(F, K, flag, df, price)
    assert np.allclose(out.undiscounted_call_price, expected, rtol=0, atol=1e-12)
    # canonical coordinates are passed through unchanged
    assert np.array_equal(out.fwd_price, F)
    assert np.array_equal(out.strike, K)
    assert np.array_equal(out.tte, T)


def test_compile_trust_master_matches_raw_seam():
    F = np.array([100.0, 100.0])
    K = np.array([100.0, 120.0])
    T = np.array([1.0, 0.5])
    flag = np.array([_CALL, _PUT], dtype=np.int8)
    df = np.array([0.98, 0.97])
    price = np.array([10.0, 18.0])

    quotes = ValidatedOptionQuotes(
        fwd_price=F,
        strike=K,
        tte=T,
        call_put_flag=flag,
        discount_factor=df,
        option_price=price,
    )
    via_master = compile_canonical_option_quotes(quotes)
    via_raw = _compile_canonical_option_quotes_arrays(F, K, T, flag, df, price)
    assert np.array_equal(
        via_master.undiscounted_call_price, via_raw.undiscounted_call_price
    )


def test_compile_arrays_seam_normalizes_dtype_and_layout():
    # non-contiguous, wrong dtype raw inputs must be normalized by the seam
    F = np.asfortranarray(np.array([[100.0, 100.0]]))[0]  # non-C-contiguous view
    K = np.array([100, 120], dtype=np.int64)  # wrong dtype
    T = np.array([1.0, 0.5])
    flag = np.array([1, 0], dtype=np.int64)  # wrong dtype
    df = np.array([0.98, 0.97])
    price = np.array([10.0, 18.0])

    out = _compile_canonical_option_quotes_arrays(F, K, T, flag, df, price)
    assert out.undiscounted_call_price.dtype == np.float64
    assert out.fwd_price.flags["C_CONTIGUOUS"]


def _run_all():
    fns = [v for k, v in sorted(globals().items()) if k.startswith("test_")]
    for fn in fns:
        fn()
        print(f"PASS {fn.__name__}")
    print(f"\nALL {len(fns)} TESTS PASSED")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _run_all()
```
